# New API Fork Memory

- **2026-09-21 shared-checkout isolation and billing review:** An upstream-sync worker must verify its absolute Git root and pinned HEAD before every mutation; a default working directory is not the assigned worktree. Do not use repository-wide stash/reset/abort in a shared checkout with concurrent work. A later ref update can coexist with stale merge metadata, so recovery must use the preserved index, tree snapshots and path-level provenance. The rc39 incident is recorded in the isolated candidate's `.review/rc39/main-checkout-recovery-audit.md`; the user's other work and backup refs must remain intact. For pre-consume tests, distinguish legacy input-floor/full-output reservation from tiered expression policy, and inspect both paths before accepting an upstream input-only assertion. A green refund test using fixed request pricing cannot establish that output-token reservation survived the merge.

- **2026-09-21 staged upstream integration checkpoints:** When reusing a reviewed merge tree through explicit-base `git merge-tree`, the real `MERGE_HEAD` names the intended upstream parent but does not prove that its delta is already present in the index. Record the input base, both tree inputs and output tree for every materialization. Finish and stage each worker's changes, require no unresolved entries or tracked unstaged edits, and save `git write-tree` before applying the next tree. rc39 recovery found the index still at the rc38 checkpoint plus newer fork commits even though `MERGE_HEAD` already pointed to rc39. Attribute locale additions to the actual stage inputs; the first ten new retry keys came from fork commits. A final upstream sync still needs full fork-only commit survival and parent/result comparisons; incremental checks of the newest fork commits alone cannot detect an older fix being overwritten.

- **2026-09-20 rc38 merge verification lessons:** Bind a sync to the exact release tag (`v1.0.0-rc.38` = `2906e4f77`); `upstream/main` already contained eight later commits. The candidate preserves fork retry overrides in both the request-policy form and the backend option allowlist/defaults; checking only the visible controls misses silently dropped persisted values. Channel WebSocket eligibility must share `supportsResponsesWebSocket` with serialization (types 58/59/60 otherwise save as false). Moving a dropdown into a portal breaks inherited privacy masking: pass the existing mask class to the popup and verify both Hide and Show. Frontend locale reconciliation retained the fork bytes plus 175 upstream keys and two policy-event labels, giving 7320 keys per language; backend OAuth additions also require seven-language parity.

- **Upstream test expectations must preserve fork billing contracts:** HTTP/SSE Responses failures with no observed usage retain the fork refund behavior; accepted WebSocket requests have separate existing settlement semantics. Do not expand HTTP charges merely to make an upstream transport-parity test pass. Health metrics count request outcomes independently of consume-log rows. During a long isolated review, re-fetch and record mainline drift before landing: evidence for the original fork parent does not validate newer concurrent commits or uncommitted work.
- **Verification tooling during parallel merges:** `web/scripts/format-with-protected-headers.mjs --check` temporarily rewrites all web files before restoring its snapshot. Never run it over concurrent edits; on Windows a running dev server can also cause mapped-file write errors. A tracked-source snapshot with the same installed dependencies supports the same check without modifying the active worktree. The official test runner must enumerate both `src` and `scripts` and execute both Vitest and node:test phases even after failures. Compare exact failing test names and lint diagnostics with the fork parent, not just failure counts. MariaDB through the MySQL driver is useful additional coverage but does not establish that Oracle MySQL was tested.
- **Authentication findings from the rc38 review remain open:** The fork parent already allowed anonymous OAuth login state without browser binding, built-in provider ownership without a database uniqueness guarantee, and GitHub profile-email fallback when no verified email was obtained. rc38 does not introduce these root causes; its legacy GitHub migration also needs the shared ownership guarantee. Attribution is not a security waiver. Before approving a production release, bind login state to the initiating browser, enforce provider/subject ownership atomically across all supported databases with an explicit legacy-duplicate policy, and use only verified GitHub email as account evidence. Keep these changes and their database/browser regression checks explicit; a passing existing auth test suite does not cover these gaps.
- On 2026-09-21 the fork stopped failing over on an upstream 4xx that rejects the request itself, then made that rule operator-editable (commits `5cc245bb2` + `c0fb3c0a9` + `b12213846`). Trigger: a mainland buyer's `deepseek-v4-pro` request with a 1.27M-token prompt was answered by **five different channels with the same context-window 400** (`cdp-key-1`, `RetryTimes=4`), each attempt logging an error row and the client waiting ~89s for a verdict no channel could change. Root cause is deliberate, not a bug: `ForceRetryStatusCodeRanges` (default `{400}`) exists so a channel-shaped rejection — an endpoint or parameter one channel cannot serve while another can — fails over, and the rule cannot distinguish that from a request the model refuses. Fix keeps the force-retry rule for channel-shaped 400s and stops the request-shaped ones in `service/IsNeverRetryUpstreamError`, read by both retry decisions (`controller.shouldRetry`, `service.ShouldRetryRelayError`). The rule is the **"Never-retry error keywords"** field on Routing Reliability — one keyword per line, persisted as the `NeverRetryKeywords` option exactly like its status-code siblings, so an operator meeting a new wording adds a line instead of waiting for a release, while clearing the list turns the rule off and restores the 400 force-retry. The shipped default is the wordings the live channels actually returned, recorded from the production `logs` table: `context length` (covers the official/OpenAI "maximum context length", the AI-SDK `{"error":{"message":...}}` envelope, and the official channel's "longer than the model's context length"), `prompt is too long`, `input is too long`, `token exceed the limit`, `exceeds the maximum allowed input length`, `exceeds the maximum length`, Bailian's `Range of input length should be`, and the `context_length_exceeded` code form. Replaying the seven-day window over that list stops **311 of the 471 requests that spent a retry chain** at their first attempt (259 before the two extra wordings were added). Two design constraints worth keeping: only 4xx qualifies (the same wording behind a 5xx may be transient, so it stays retryable), and the check sits **after** the commit-state switch instead of using the `skipRetry` marker, because `ClearSkipRetry` is by design dropped on a keep-alive-only commit — a streaming client that waited long enough for a ping would otherwise have reopened the retry. Tests: `TestIsNeverRetryUpstreamErrorMatchesLiveContextOverflowTexts` (the eleven recorded wordings), `TestIsNeverRetryUpstreamErrorIgnoresChannelAndTransientFailures`, `TestIsNeverRetryUpstreamErrorFollowsOperatorKeywords`, `TestShouldRetryContextOverflowStopsFailover` (covers the keep-alive path), the `TestNeverRetryKeywords*` pair in operation_setting, and a component test that renders and edits the field. Verified `go build ./...`, `gofmt`, `go test ./...`, frontend typecheck, and `i18n:sync` (missing 0 across all seven locales); the frontend suite's pre-existing failures (5 vitest, 7 node:test) were confirmed unchanged by re-running it with every change stashed. **Not deployed to either site** (mainland needs a `tokeness-cn` tag release; the shipped default reproduces the built-in behaviour, so the upgrade needs no operator action). Deliberately out of scope — the other request-shaped 400 families that also burned retries in the same window (`The reasoning_content in the thinking mode must be passed back`, `请求参数错误`, `field 'messages' is required`, content-moderation rejections): they stay retryable, because a strict official channel can reject what a lenient aggregator accepts, so the wording alone does not prove the whole pool refuses. Same day the section itself was regrouped (commit `32777cf16`): the seven fields now sit under three subheadings in decision order — when to retry another channel / when never to retry (wins over the rules above) / multi-key channels — with descriptions rewritten to say what each field is for instead of restating the mechanism. That rewrite also closed a real gap: the auto-retry keyword description had never been in any locale file, so a Chinese admin saw one English paragraph in the middle of an otherwise Chinese page; every `t()` key in the section now resolves (72/72) and the five replaced descriptions are gone from all seven locales. Verified in a real browser against a throwaway instance (fresh SQLite, first registered user promoted to role 100 with an email set to clear `EmailBindingGate`), which also confirmed the shipped keyword default renders when the option row does not exist yet.
- On 2026-09-21 the retry rules were collapsed into **three decisions** (commit `3a2b41d86`), checked in this order and shown on Routing Reliability in the same order: (1) **never retry** — status codes ∪ keywords, returns the error to the client; (2) **retry another key of the same channel** — multi-key channels only, status codes ∪ keywords; (3) **retry another channel** — status codes ∪ keywords. Matching nothing means no retry. The order is the design: (1) is a verdict about the *request*, which every channel answers identically, so it must precede both remedies; (2) is cheaper and more precise than (3) — the next key of the same channel has identical upstream configuration, so it cannot produce a format/endpoint surprise, and it **does not consume the retry budget** (the loop bounds it with the request's untried-key set) — so it precedes failover. Key-rotation exhaustion already falls through to (3) via `IsMultiKeyRetryExhausted`, which is the desired semantics, not a special case. Implementation notes worth keeping: the retired `ForceRetryStatusCodes` list was merged into `AutomaticRetryStatusCodeRanges` (both meant "this channel cannot serve the request, try another"); the new default is the old automatic ranges **plus 400**, which equals what production already had configured, so no behavior change there. A persisted `ForceRetryStatusCodes` row is migrated into the failover row and deleted by `migrateForceRetryStatusCodes` (called from `MigrateRetiredFrontendOptions`, master-node only), and `ShouldRetryByStatusCode` unions both lists so a process that has not migrated yet behaves identically; **the migration must start from the compiled default when no failover row exists**, otherwise it silently drops the defaults (found by testing that case against a throwaway instance, now pinned by `TestMigrateForceRetryStatusCodes*`). The multi-key path gained a keyword list (`MultiKeyCredentialRetryKeywords`, default `insufficient credits` / `insufficient balance` / `balance insufficient`) because the live out-of-balance 400s are key-scoped: 562 in seven days, all on one multi-key channel, 28 of which spent a second channel before dying — the balance belongs to the key. `ShouldRotateMultiKeyCredentialOn` is the shared predicate for all three call sites (`shouldRetry`, `ShouldRetryRelayError`, `shouldRetryTaskRelay`) plus affinity exemption and `prepareChannelRetryWithMessage`. The local-error gate on the failover list was deliberately **kept**: production shows the local 400s that do retry are channel capability gaps carried by their `channel:` error-code prefix (handled at the top of both decisions), while request-shaped local 400s carry an explicit skip-retry marker; removing the error-type gate would newly make local request errors burn the retry budget. Verified `go build ./...`, `go vet`, the full `go test ./...` suite (53 packages, zero failures), frontend typecheck, build and vitest for the section, `i18n:sync` (missing 0 in all seven locales), and a browser pass against a throwaway instance covering render, edit-to-database round-trip, and both migration cases. **Not deployed to either site** (mainland needs a `tokeness-cn` tag release; the new defaults reproduce built-in behaviour, so the upgrade needs no operator action).

- On 2026-09-17 the client-disconnect stream 502 fix shipped to both sites from commit `4e34391d9` as overseas `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.3` (GHCR digest `sha256:103bfb668918a2e33623ba683736cc8bb284e526b0dd7ab7a308bb9b0f740b32`) and mainland `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.4` (CNB `ml-` digest `sha256:ff48cfe6dd941337eae504368ebd8cfde9fd97d45c4bf5006f26981a78327162`), both also carrying the two previously-unreleased partner commits (`e057187f5` persist+hydrate, `dd52042fa` deadlock/pg-quoting fix) that the user approved shipping together. **Overseas rollout required a drift repair first:** the first `tokeness-deploy.yml` run failed at preflight with `JP-M preflight verification failed` because JP-M's running container was the untracked local build `pc-newapi-local:latest` (`cfd4914fe4a1afcfed1c9ceada46aea7dcfba7064a31e77c82fb6b66e9fd17b4`, reporting `v1.0.0-rc.43-tokeness-official-fit-hybrid.8`) while its `release.env`/`.blue-green.state` declared `intl.2` — `wait_for_ready` requires the runtime image to equal the selected image, so the pre-existing run/publish mismatch blocked the fleet. Repaired by running `deploy ghcr.io/l1i1/new-api@sha256:103bfb66...` through the JP-M restricted SSH forced command (`/usr/local/sbin/tokeness-new-api-deploy`, key `private/keys/tokeness-gha-deploy` staged to WSL `~/.ssh/tokeness-deploy` 0600); the blue-green deploy pulled the new digest, started `new-api-green` on 8201, switched OpenResty, and committed, so JP-M is now under release management again. The other three nodes (EV-JP/EV-JP2/US-N0-CC) were already on `intl.2` and verified clean. Second workflow run completed with `deployment complete: all nodes and CDN routes expose version v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.3` (the `v-test-new`/`rolling back` lines in the log are the deploy test suite, not production). All four nodes were verified afterwards and are consistent — each one's `release.env` selects `103bfb66...` and the running container matches it (EV-JP/EV-JP2 via `/opt/new-api-slave`, US-N0-CC via `verify`), so the JP-M mismatch was the only drift. Partner console env survived the JP-M rebuild (`PARTNER_CONSOLE_SECRETS` present in `new-api-green`, `/internal/v1/partner/health` still 401). Post-deploy proof of the fix: mainland logged **0** `upstream stream terminated` rows in the 15 minutes after the 07:07Z container restart (previously ~30/hour), and overseas logged 0 in the 6 minutes after its 07:24Z rollout. **Not fixed by this change:** overseas still logs `500 upstream error: do request failed` for client cancels (36 rows in the same 6-minute window) because that is the request-stage path in `relay/channel/api_request.go:577`, a different code path from the streaming 502 — the same "don't record a client abort as a gateway error" principle has not been applied there.
- On 2026-09-17 the fork stopped synthesizing `502 upstream stream terminated (...)` for callers that disconnect mid-stream (mainland was logging this on `/v1/chat/completions` in bursts: 24h showed 597 `client_gone` + 125 `scanner_error`, 100% `context canceled`, every one a single attempt). Root cause of the burst was **not** an upstream outage: joining all log rows by `request_id` proved each request had a fast healthy upstream first chunk (`frt` 75-125ms in the **consume** row, `completion_tokens` 29-56, billed `quota` 186-302, note `incomplete upstream stream`) and the caller left afterwards. The two messages are one event: `client_gone` is the main-loop detection, `scanner_error` is the same cancelled context closing `resp.Body` so the scanner's next `Read` reports `context canceled`; who wins the race decides the label. **Important correction to earlier reading:** `frt`/`quota=0` on the error row is meaningless — `frt` is only written by `GenerateTextOtherInfo` on consume rows, never on error rows, so "no first byte / nothing billed" was a wrong inference. Change: `OaiStreamHandler` now returns the settled usage with a nil error when `StreamStatus.EndReason == client_gone` **or** `c.Request.Context().Err() != nil`, placed after `applyUsagePostProcessing` and before the 502 construction, so observed partial usage is still billed, no `[DONE]` is emitted, and no error row is written; `StreamScannerHandler` logs the client-caused scanner error and end line at info instead of error. This matches upstream, which never had the OpenAI-chat 502 synthesis (upstream only defines `StreamEndReason` and uses `client_gone` for a Gemini-only downstream-stop special case, and finishes the OpenAI chat stream with `return usage, nil`). Genuine upstream stream failures are untouched and still return the skip-retry 502, preserved by two existing tests plus a new `TestOaiStreamHandlerTreatsClientGoneAsNonError` that cancels the request context mid-read. Verified: `gofmt` clean, `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./... -count=1` (all green), `relaykit` `GOWORK=off go build ./...`, `git diff --check`. Files: `relay/channel/openai/relay-openai.go`, `relay/helper/stream_scanner.go`, `relay/channel/openai/relay_openai_stream_usage_test.go`, `docs/stream-termination-hardening-tech-spec.md`. A deliberate non-change: `appendStreamStatus` still records `status=error` for an aborted stream (upstream does the same, and adding an `aborted` value would surface an untranslated red badge in `usage-logs/details-dialog.tsx`, whose `stream_status.status` is rendered verbatim). **Not committed and not deployed** — production still runs the previous behavior.

- On 2026-09-16 the dual-site release shipped from commit `a824e37ca` (two commits on top of `33160b599`): overseas `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.2` and mainland `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.3`. Content: (1) `a22da55a5` `feat(partner): white-label tagging, partner stats API and signed bypass` — the backend half of the partner/reseller console (`controller/partner.go`, `model/partner_stats.go`, `model/partner_white_label.go`, `setting/operation_setting/partner_setting.go`, and `middleware/partner_bypass.go`). It is **inert until configured**: every `/internal/v1/partner/*` route is guarded by `requirePartnerBypass` and answered `401 partner_unauthorized` unless the request carries a valid HMAC (`X-Partner-Signature`/`X-Partner-Timestamp`/`X-Partner-Key-ID`, hex HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body, 300s anti-replay window) — verification reads the signing secret from the `PARTNER_CONSOLE_SECRETS` env var (comma-separated `key-id:secret` entries; a bare secret matches any key id), so an unset/empty var means the bypass never activates and the whole feature is dormant (verified on both live sites: `/internal/v1/partner/health` and `POST .../users` both 401). Registration-time white-label stamping keys off `partner_setting.partners[].inviter_user_ids`, which defaults empty. Frontend: the wallet hides the referral card per white-label marker and renders a per-partner contact copy plus a partner notice block (`web/src/features/wallet/lib/white-label.ts`, `components/partner-notice-card.tsx`). (2) `a824e37ca` `chore(home): remove the LM Speed provider badge from the footer` — deletes the `https://lmspeed.net/provider/tokeness-cn` footer link + claim-badge image from `tokeness-home.tsx`, its now-dead `.tokeness-home__footer-badge` CSS, and the matching test assertion. Overseas path: publish run `35083642303` (approved `tokeness-release` 18799338808; `gh api -X POST .../pending_deployments -F 'environment_ids[]=18799338808'`) → immutable digest `sha256:95932fcb590e25e5a4a922b50e7ac6912d36b821c70d8cf7f43eb02d40b3950a` (read from the publish log's `IMAGE_DIGEST`, and the webdist CDN switched in the same publish: `l1i1/newapi-webdist` `manifest.json` `current=v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.2`) → deploy run `35084706002` (approved `tokeness-production` 18799354801) → rollout log ends `deployment complete: all nodes and CDN routes expose version v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.2` (the earlier `version v-test-new` line is the deploy test suite, not production). Mainland path: annotated tag object `b3997a029b31a10aa16718c693d4275eb6cd0d15` at `a824e37ca` pushed to the internal `origin` (mirrors to CNB + GitHub) → CNB `tag_push` build `cnb-ln8-1k2kr9ebu` success → chained unattended `deploy to production`, no environment approval. Verification: preflight `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`go test ./... -count=1` all exit 0, `relaykit` independent `GOWORK=off` build+vet clean, web `bun run build:check` (tsgo typecheck + rsbuild production build) clean, and the `tokeness`/`tokeness-cn` deploy suites (`rollout-test.sh`, `remote-command-test.sh`, `blue-green-test.sh`, `deploy-test.sh`) plus `bash -n` and both `nodes.json` all PASS under WSL (Windows Git Bash fails the rollout suite). Post-deploy on both sites: `/api/status` reports `.2`/`.3`, `/health/live` + `/health/ready` 200, `/v1/models` 401, the four overseas node endpoints (`n`/`n-cf`/`n-us`/`n-us-cf`.tokeness.dev) all 401, and partner endpoints 401. Frontend proof without an account: the served entry bundles (`tokeness.ai/static/js/index.c8b62d8360.js`, `tokeness.cn/static/js/index.6d3246adcd.js`) are both 4301773 bytes with **0** occurrences of `lmspeed` and 2 of `All rights reserved` (the second string is a content-loaded control so a 404/empty fetch cannot produce a false pass). Rollback targets: overseas `sha256:967cf27349474c85e723dd19b67b8fa9396c1df7983881fb5173e3f43772dfe3` (`v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.1`), mainland `sha256:c6ba878fa7f1b25618191e2c7e905c933436412501fe5facd1f9e1005034cb0e` (`.2`). Local hygiene in the same pass: deleted four local branches already fully contained in `origin/tokeness/main` (`feat/hotpay-per-app-settlement`, `feat/officialfit-family-registry`, `fix/failed-request-refund-log`, `tokeness/release/mainland.28`); `codex/sync-upstream-rc36` and `codex/sync-upstream-8529f209` remain because the `E:/Ai/Worktrees/*` worktrees pin them, and both dirty worktrees (`newapi-plugin-test-20260915`, `newapi-upstream-8529f209`) were left untouched. The removed footer badge was the only fork **code** reference to lmspeed; `marketing/research/competitors.md` still tracks it as a third-party review site (research notes, not shipped code).

- On 2026-09-16 the mainland-only currency/language change shipped as `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.2` (annotated tag object `b715be81fc599dac775af5553ccf5a14ecde0c95` at commit `fb3c79a91`, the two commits above): CNB `tag_push` build `cnb-c7o-1k2k1ju6m` (validate → build → push, success 294s) chained into `deploy to production` `cnb-ho8-1k2k1or04` / pipeline `cnb-ho8-1k2k1or04-001` (success 128s, title `CN production deploy v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.2`). Certified digest `sha256:c6ba878fa7f1b25618191e2c7e905c933436412501fe5facd1f9e1005034cb0e` (resolved from the tag by `docker buildx imagetools inspect`, re-checked between stages) is exactly the digest the new running instance `eci-uf6bihk7zkurtm62cyq6`@10.0.0.238 carries; the deploy replaced `eci-uf6351h3y3i8v3v9t14o`@10.0.0.236. The pipeline ran the rollout and the checks itself — the log shows two transient `ERROR: expected exactly one active server in upstream newapi_ml` while the ESS was still converging, then `verify: OK (upstream=10.0.0.238)` on attempt 3 and `postcheck: public version v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.2` / `postcheck: OK (head rendered server-side, /v1/models answered 401)`; the trailing `cannot kill container ...-dind-proxy` line is teardown noise after success. Live state: `/api/status` reports `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.2` with `usd_exchange_rate=1`, `quota_per_unit=500000`, `quota_display_type=CNY`; `/health/live` and `/health/ready` 200, `/v1/models` 401. **Verifying a frontend-only release without an account**: the site redirects every public path to `/sign-in`, so the authenticated views cannot be inspected — instead (1) the served entry bundle `static/js/index.76b46f0cf9.js` contains `如果人民币结账不可用` and `Waffo Pancake 汇率`, strings that only exist in this commit, and (2) presetting `localStorage.i18nextLng='en'` (plus a `currency-display-storage` USD preference) and reloading still renders Chinese with no language or currency switcher, which is exactly the detection path the mainland build now bypasses. Note the **CI bundle hash differs from a local build** (`index.76b46f0cf9.js` vs `index.5d8b852ab9.js`) because the CNB build passes `VITE_ICP_BEIAN`/`VITE_POLICE_BEIAN` build-args that a bare local `bun run build` omits; compare content, not hashes. Rollback target: `.1` `sha256:2c55e1e1942e27c1f97bd9c9106ba7e2ec3ea74fc5e07ae814de0f99b0f2bd8d` (commit `970510d91`). Also note the CNB registry's `get-package-tag-detail` reports a *different* digest for the same tag (`sha256:b298dcf0…` for `.2`, `sha256:6c324582…` for `.1`) than the deploy's certified digest — use the deploy log or `aliyun eci DescribeContainerGroups`, not the registry detail, when checking what is actually running.

- On 2026-09-16 the mainland edition was pinned to RMB end to end and to Simplified Chinese, as a **build-flavour change with no overseas effect** (branch `tokeness/main`; not yet released — production is still `rc.37-mainland.1` / `rc.37-intl.1`, so the next mainland tag is a fix-only `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.2`). Product facts that drive it: the mainland site's `usd_exchange_rate` is 1, display is 元, and **500000 quota is one yuan**, so the platform's own unit is RMB in consumption, top-up and display alike — the upstream copy that still says "USD"/"美元" reads as if it billed in dollars. What changed: (1) **new `web/src/i18n/mainland-currency.ts`** applies per-locale term rules (`USD`→`CNY`, `美元`→`人民币`, `米ドル`→`人民元`, `доллар…`→`CNY`, price-prefix symbols `$/1M`→`¥/1M` and `${{price}}`→`¥{{price}}`, leaving `HK$` and regex anchors alone) plus a small hand-written override table for the keys whose mechanical result would be broken or false (`CNY per USD` → 汇率/Exchange Rate because "人民币兑人民币汇率" is nonsense, `US dollar (USD)` → "CNY (CNY)", the Waffo pairs that would claim Pancake settles in CNY, and the WeChat-Pay fallback note which becomes 等值外币); the transform runs **only** when `IS_MAINLAND_SITE` (`i18n/config.ts`), the seven `locales/*.json` files stay untouched so upstream i18n syncs keep merging cleanly and any later upstream string saying "USD" is reworded automatically — and a unit test fails if an override key stops existing upstream. (2) The billing settings page (`features/system-settings/general/pricing-section.tsx`) now forces `quota_display_type='CNY'` and `USDExchangeRate=1` into the form defaults and disables both controls on mainland **without ever writing those keys** (the form submits only changed fields), so the displayed state no longer depends on what the option rows hold. (3) The model-pricing editors default to a new flavour-aware `DEFAULT_PRICING_CURRENCY` (mainland → CNY at rate 1, overseas → USD), the pricing-currency selector is hidden and the USD/site preference store is forced to the site currency on mainland, so the admin price editor never shows `$`. (4) Language is fixed to Simplified Chinese on mainland: no `LanguageDetector`, `lng: 'zhCN'`, `supportedLngs: ['zhCN']` (so `changeLanguage('en')` is rejected by i18next), the header footer/wizard language switcher and the profile language card render nothing, and `getSavedLanguage` returns `undefined` so an account whose `setting.language` is `en` can no longer switch the UI at sign-in. Verification: `tsgo -b` typecheck, oxlint and oxfmt clean on every touched file; new tests are `i18n/__tests__/mainland-currency.test.ts` (7 cases: rewrites, no dollar wording left in any locale, symbol safety, overrides, override-key existence, untranslated バンドル/コードルール untouched, input not mutated), `mainland-i18n-config.test.ts` / `overseas-i18n-config.test.ts` (real `i18n/config` module with the flavour mocked: mainland resolves `zhCN`, renders 充值金额 (CNY) and refuses `en`; overseas resolves `en`, keeps `Recharge Amount (USD)` and still switches) and `system-settings/__tests__/pricing-section-mainland.test.tsx` (a section seeded with hostile `USD`/`7` rows renders CNY + 1 disabled on mainland and editable 7 overseas); the full suite is unchanged against the `970510d91` baseline — the same 5 vitest failures (vendor-management 1, pricing-controls 3, redemptions-export 1, all reproduced in a HEAD worktree) and the same 7 node:test failures — and both `VITE_SITE_FLAVOR` builds succeed; served-bundle probes (`bun x serve` + Playwright) show the mainland bundle reports `htmlLang=zh-CN` with no currency/language switcher buttons while the overseas bundle keeps both. Two traps worth remembering: **the wording layer was initially applied for both flavours** (only the overseas flavour test caught it — keep the `IS_MAINLAND_SITE` gate on `resources`), and **`git worktree remove` on a worktree containing a junction to `web/node_modules` deletes the target's contents** because Windows follows the reparse point — recover with `bun install --frozen-lockfile` (1193 packages, ~2 min) and remove such junctions with `rmdir` (never `del`) before removing the worktree.

- On 2026-09-15 both production sites shipped the rc37 upstream sync from commit `70d4dbe47` (dual-site, source-identical): overseas `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.1` (digest `sha256:967cf27349474c85e723dd19b67b8fa9396c1df7983881fb5173e3f43772dfe3`, publish run 34972353220 → deploy run 34973397798, rollout log ends `deployment complete: all nodes and CDN routes expose version v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.1`, four `n.tokeness.dev`/`n-cf`/`n-us`/`n-us-cf` endpoints plus `tokeness.ai/v1/models` all 401, frontend CDN manifest `current=v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-intl.1` `previous=v1.0.0-rc.36-tokeness-intl.9` `source_commit=70d4dbe47` and the served entry asset hash matches the release dir) and mainland `v1.0.0-rc.33-tokeness-mainland.30` (annotated tag object `5e06e650`, CNB `tag_push` build `cnb-pev-1k2iifqd5`: validate → build (358s) → push → **chained `deploy to production` (101s) all success**, unattended; running ECI `eci-uf6cci3niaeuw6bpjv5a` at 10.0.0.234 carries digest `sha256:12ae05a64b65a2bbf50ccac35d7383053a27eed5253d3683b2484e008351fe19`, nginx upstream .234, `deploy.sh verify` and `postcheck` both OK). **Scope differs per site and that is expected**: mainland was already at `.29` = `1e40b4177`, so its delta is only the 47-file follow-up; overseas sat at `b5b7c2d08` (intl.9) and therefore also absorbed the whole `.28`/`.29` chain, i.e. 596 files / 66 commits. Content: upstream rc37 snapshot merge (`42bc64c17`, upstream `69a500298`) bringing per-route pass-through for advanced custom channels (#7386), request stream usage on every cross-protocol conversion to OpenAI chat (#7389), `:countTokens` rejected as an unknown route instead of relaying as `generateContent` (#7388), and case-insensitive Gemini `thinkingLevel` with canonical-effort logging (#7387); plus follow-up `3cee7e1a8` restoring fork behavior the merge had overwritten — **task polling and plugin-artifact paths now resolve the persisted `ChannelCredentialID` and `ProxySnapshot`** via the new `model.BuildTaskPollingRelayInfo(channel, task, baseURL, key, proxy)` (used by `controller/task.go`, `controller/video_proxy_gemini.go`, `relay/relay_task.go`, `service/task_polling.go`, including the removed/reordered-credential case; `updateBatchTasks` groups by `credential|proxy`, not credential alone), the in-memory rate limiter refreshes `lastActive`/LRU position **only for allowed requests** so a rejected request can no longer keep a dead key alive, `ollama` keeps relay mode completions on its own handler even with the OpenAI-compatible chat switch on, midjourney honors an explicit `ProxySnapshotSet` instead of re-deriving from the credential, and `controller/channel-billing.go` closes the response body via `defer`. Rollback targets: overseas `intl.9` `sha256:e081c9383ea864a73ae5eaf6051a6620694aaabeaeba57b4a564ece7ab619568`, mainland `.29` (commit `1e40b4177`, CNB `cnb-77o-1k2i4nrov`). Verification on `70d4dbe47`: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./... -count=1` (exit 0), gofmt clean on every changed Go file, `relaykit` standalone `GOWORK=off` build+vet+test (exit 0), web `tsgo -b` typecheck and production build (exit 0), i18n flat parity 7143 keys × 7 locales (0 missing / 0 extra), and both deploy suites under WSL (`deployment/tokeness/tests/*-test.sh`, `deployment/tokeness-cn/tests/deploy-test.sh`, `bash -n`, `nodes.json`) — **note Windows Git Bash fails the rollout suite; run it from WSL**. Frontend test baseline improved rather than regressed: HEAD has 5 vitest failures (redemptions-export 1, pricing-controls 3, vendor-management 1) plus 7 `matchMedia is not defined` unhandled errors from `antd-style` under the node:test runner, while a sibling worktree at the previous production commit `1e40b4177` shows **7 vitest failures across 4 files and 37 node:test failures**, so HEAD is strictly better; these files, `src/test-setup.ts`, the vitest config and the runner are all untouched by this diff, the node:test errors come from the shim living in the vitest-only `test-setup.ts`, and none of it is covered by the publish/deploy workflows. Operational gotchas from this run: `gh` resolves this checkout to the upstream remote, so every workflow command needs `--repo l1i1/new-api` (otherwise HTTP 404 `workflow not found on the default branch`); approving a pending deployment needs `-F 'environment_ids[]=<int>'` because `-f` sends the id as a string and the API rejects it with 422; `deploy.sh verify`/`postcheck` need `SWAS_SSH_KEY_PATH` pointed at a copy of the key **inside** WSL with `chmod 600` (the `/mnt/e/...` mount is 0777 and ssh refuses it as an unprotected private key), and the digest cannot be read from the CNB CLI, so confirm it with `aliyun eci DescribeContainerGroups`; the overseas deploy log's `rolling back N updated node(s)` lines are the deploy test suite exercising rollback, not a production rollback. Both release gates are GitHub environments with `l1i1` as reviewer and `prevent_self_review=false`: `tokeness-release` (id 18799338808, publish = image build + frontend CDN dist only) and `tokeness-production` (id 18799354801, the actual rollout). Version-label convention (decided 2026-09-15, documented in `deployment/tokeness-cn/README.md`): **both lines track the upstream release they are synced to and are re-aligned on every upstream sync** — `<semver>` in `v<semver>-tokeness-<line>.<N>` is the current upstream base, and only `<N>` advances for fix-only releases. Overseas already worked this way (`rc.36` for intl.1–.9, re-based to `rc.37` for intl.1); the mainland line had been frozen on the `rc.33` base since `mainland.1` (2026-09-05) and is now re-based to `rc.37` too, so the two labels are comparable instead of implying the mainland build derives from an older upstream than it does.

- On 2026-09-16 the mainland version base was re-aligned to the synced upstream: shipped as `v1.0.0-rc.37-tokeness-mainland.1` (commit `0fda21747`, annotated tag object `4a34f105c132c0895cabc877b3e20c92a969495d`; CNB `tag_push` build `cnb-9ad-1k2jpqksr`: validate → build → push → **chained `deploy to production` (96s) all success**; running ECI `eci-uf6351h3y3i8v3v9t14o` at 10.0.0.236 carries digest `sha256:2c55e1e1942e27c1f97bd9c9106ba7e2ec3ea74fc5e07ae814de0f99b0f2bd8d`; `deploy.sh postcheck` OK, live `/api/status` reports `rc.37-mainland.1`, `/v1/models` 401, relay smoke test 200). **This release changes no code** — the delta from `70d4dbe47` is docs only (`MEMORY.md` + `deployment/tokeness-cn/README.md`); its purpose is to stop the mainland label from advertising an upstream base three releases stale. Reason: the mainland line was frozen on `rc.33` from `mainland.1` (2026-09-05, fork point upstream `eb99ab1b4` = `v1.0.0-rc.33`) through `.30`, even though rc34/rc36/rc37 syncs had landed in between — provable from history, since the rc36-sync commit `7d67b1dc1` still carries the tag `v1.0.0-rc.33-tokeness-mainland.19`. Overseas already re-aligned per sync (`rc.36` for intl.1–.9 → `rc.37` for intl.1). Convention now recorded in `deployment/tokeness-cn/README.md`: `<semver>` = the currently synced upstream base, re-aligned on every upstream sync, `<N>` restarts at 1 per base and advances only for fix-only releases on that base; **the pipeline regexes pin only `-tokeness-mainland.<N>`, so a stale base passes silently — nothing catches a missed re-align**. Rollback target: mainland `.30` `sha256:12ae05a64b65a2bbf50ccac35d7383053a27eed5253d3683b2484e008351fe19` (commit `70d4dbe47`).
- On 2026-09-15 the CDP buyer-verbatim acceptance re-test drove the mainland gateway from 20 gateway-side differences to 3, and **the remaining 3 are the route decision, not code**. Shipped as `v1.0.0-rc.33-tokeness-mainland.29` (commit `1e40b4177`, CNB tag_push `cnb-77o-1k2i4nrov` → chained `api_trigger` `cnb-qsl-1k2i4t16d`, both success; live `/api/status` reports `.29`). What shipped: (1) **the official serde location suffix is now reproduced** — every `Failed to deserialize the JSON body into the target type: <path>: <reason>` text ends in ` at line 1 column N`, and N is the 1-based **byte** offset of the offending value token, i.e. a pure function of the request bytes. The gateway synthesizes these texts locally (it never forwards the upstream error body), so it emitted them suffix-less and every such case was a permanent diff; new `relay/helper/serde_location.go` scans the **stored request body** along the message's field path (object keys + array indices) instead of hardcoding a column that would rot with any body edit. A missing field reports against the **innermost named container** (trailing array indices dropped), which is what the live endpoint does for both `thinking: missing field` (end of the thinking object) and `messages[2]: missing field` (end of the messages array). Only the deserialization class carries the suffix — the body-parse class (`Failed to parse the request body as JSON: ...`) and all business rejections stay suffix-free, per live probing. The scanner's unit tests pin the **exact columns recorded from the live official endpoint** for all ten audited cases, so the derivation is checked against upstream rather than against itself. (2) The official remote-image failure text is recognised by **shape** (`.messages[<i>].image[<j>]: Failed to download image from <url>` — two indices, so no single prefix works); without it the gateway masked the URL to `https://***.com/***` and appended a gateway request id, so a routed request would still differ. (3) `tools/cdp-bench`'s baseline comparator was itself wrong: it stripped the location suffix on both sides and reported the leftover as a labelled diff, which made a correct gateway **permanently** diff whenever the baseline was captured on a different model id than the subject (the column is body-derived, so `deepseek-flash` vs `deepseek-v4.1-flash` can never match). It now compares the exact column only when both runs sent the same body (same model) and strips the suffix otherwise, so a genuinely wrong column on a same-model run is no longer hidden behind the same label. **Live verification (mainland, `deepseek-v4.1-flash`, official flash baseline): diffs 36 → 3, buyer-verbatim replay (R6-1..R6-10) 10/10.** The remaining 3 all trace to ONE cause — aggregator mixing, not a gateway bug: 2 are random `system_fingerprint` omissions out of 50 200-responses (official sends it on 6/6 request types, re-probed today; 48/50 mainland responses carry it) and DS-131 is answered HTTP 200 by the OpenRouter aggregator (response id `gen-*`) where official flash returns the 400 download failure. The gateway already computes the right column for its own bodies (verified: gateway 135/140 == computed 135/140) — `route=true` on user#3's `deepseek-v4` family is what makes the official endpoint the answerer, and **that is still the user's pending decision** (MEMORY: 方案 1/2/3 未拍板, 全站无 `route=true` 用户). Production config confirmed today: user#3 (cdp) profile = `deepseek-v4`/`kimi-k3`/`glm-5.3` × `{validate,errors,shape}`, **no route**; the `official_fit_models` whitelist lives in the channel **`settings`** JSON (not `other`) and only ch9 (`deepseek-v4.1-flash`) and ch3 (`deepseek-v4-flash`,`deepseek-v4.1-flash`, manually disabled) carry it, matching the documented 09-14 state. Note the deploy gotcha from last time still applies: `tokeness/main` must be fast-forwarded on **origin** first — CNB mirrors it automatically, so the direct CNB push is rejected as an incorrect old value and is not needed.

- On 2026-09-15 Official Fit became family-pluggable. Family knowledge now lives in ONE leaf package, `officialfit` (`officialfit/officialfit.go`, depends only on `constant`): one `Family` row per family declares id, display label, model prefixes, the exact official model ids, the official channel type and the error wire shape. Every consumer delegates to it instead of keeping its own prefix table — `relay/helper/valid_request.go` (validators + family predicates), `relay/helper/common.go`, `relay/channel/openai/{adaptor,relay-openai,deepseek_v4_fit}.go`, `relay/channel/deepseek/adaptor.go`, `controller/relay.go` (strict-fit error wire shape is now declared by the family, not branched per family), `model/channel_cache.go` and `middleware/distributor.go` (pin + affinity). **Adding a family = one registry row + that family's validator**, no consumer edits — but the per-user `official_fit.profile` keys ARE the registry ids, so profile dimension semantics (`validate`/`errors`/`shape`/`route`) are unchanged and users without a profile are byte-identical to before. The management surface no longer hardcodes the family list: new admin endpoint `GET /api/user/official-fit/families` returns `officialfit.List()` (`[{id,label}]`, table order) and the web user drawer renders from it via `normalizeOfficialFitFamilies`, falling back to the local `OFFICIAL_FIT_MATCHES` only when the response is missing/malformed/non-array (the list crosses a trust boundary and its ids become profile keys). `Models(familyID)` now returns a COPY — it used to hand out the shared process-wide slice, so any caller's index assignment could corrupt the family table for the whole process. Known limit: `relaykit` is a nested module and cannot import the root `officialfit`, so `relaykit/relayconvert/reasoning/suffix.go`'s `deepseek-v4` prefix check must be synced by hand when a family needs suffix semantics. Landing refactor commit `9348223ba` on `feat/officialfit-family-registry`; the endpoint/drawer/list-copy round is the follow-up on the same branch. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./officialfit/... ./controller/... ./relay/... ./middleware/...` green, web `tsgo -b` clean.

- On 2026-09-15 both production sites shipped commit `b5b7c2d08` (dual-site, source-identical): overseas `v1.0.0-rc.36-tokeness-intl.9` (digest `sha256:e081c9383ea864a73ae5eaf6051a6620694aaabeaeba57b4a564ece7ab619568`, publish run 34868825514 → deploy run 34869831845, rollout verified all 4 nodes + both CDN routes at intl.9, frontend CDN manifest `current` switched to the same release and the served asset hashes match it) and mainland `v1.0.0-rc.33-tokeness-mainland.27` (digest `sha256:44bd6f4cc6f5a467a0511f6a4a894faccd04786d8781b8d157be319d0b5225b0`, CNB `tag_push` build `cnb-lpo-1k2gct8u5` → chained deploy `cnb-0bg-1k2gd2oio`; host container and the single ECI group `new-api-ml000` (10.0.0.228) both run that digest, nginx upstream .228, public `/api/status` confirms). Content: HotPay per-application settlement (`settlement_url` on every checkout; receiver verifies ANY configured secret) + invoice settled-currency display with a CNY-equivalent minimum gate. Rollback targets: overseas `intl.8` `sha256:2dd720b10dc64ed22c50e7d1bcb24f8e9012f3c9ed458d5679b73c984d7bc9a1`, mainland `.26` `sha256:2881ba9e52f4ca7478997a5581cbe140b402decda6d428b1e876e7655ef547c0`. Two gotchas worth keeping: the GHCR digest can be re-verified anonymously (`curl https://ghcr.io/token?service=ghcr.io&scope=repository:l1i1/new-api:pull` → manifest `docker-content-digest`), and the documented `private/access/ghcr-read.pat` now lives only in the workspace root, not in `apps/new-api/private/`; and the mainland deploy log's "lightweight nginx upstream" line can still show the pre-scale-down instance IP, so assert the final state with `deploy.sh verify` plus `aliyun eci DescribeContainerGroups` rather than reading that line mid-rollout. Remaining unverified: a real paid HotPay order after the checkout contract change — the settlement receiver answers 400 `invalid_settlement` to an unsigned probe (route live, fails closed) and the derived settlement URL equals `https://tokeness.cn/internal/v1/payment/settlements` (`HotPaySettlementURL` empty, `ServerAddress=https://tokeness.cn`), but no order was exercised.

- On 2026-09-15, the user-visible refund log for failed relay requests was dropped from `tokeness/main` before any push (former commit `e860d9225`, `service.RecordFailedRequestRefund` + its `controller/relay.go` call sites + `service/billing_session_test.go` case). `tokeness/main` is now `7591d6d54` → `9804ec9e0` (invoice) → `b5b7c2d08` (HotPay per-application settlement), and the tree contains NO refund-log writer for failed requests - do not assume it exists. The dropped commit is reachable only through `git reflog` (pre-rebase entries); its commit message carries the full design rationale (NeedsRefund-before-Refund gate, nil-safe `GetChannelID()` read).

- On 2026-09-13, HotPay settlement became per-application and the checkout sends this deployment's receiver (uncommitted). Companion change to HotPay's multi-tenant rewrite (it removed its single-tenant mode, so `HOTPAY_MERCHANT_API_KEY_HASH` no longer exists on the gateway side and every merchant key is now an application record). New API side: (1) new setting `HotPaySettlementURL` (option key `HotPaySettlementURL`, `setting/payment_hotpay.go` + `model/option.go` register/apply, BillingSettings type, form default, section-registry) — `hotPaySettlementURL()` (`controller/hotpay_checkout.go`) returns the configured URL or derives `{callback}/internal/v1/payment/settlements` like the EPay callbacks, and every checkout request now carries `settlement_url` (`SettlementURL` field on `service.HotPayGatewayCreateOrderRequest`) so HotPay stores and settles to this deployment instead of a deployment-wide default. Set it to `https://tokeness.cn/internal/v1/payment/settlements` and add `tokeness.cn` to that application's settlement allowed hosts in HotPay. (2) The settlement receiver (`controller/payment_gateway_settlement.go`) now verifies against ALL configured secrets via new `model.VerifyPaymentGatewaySettlementSignatureWithSecrets` (constant-time, every candidate compared): `setting.HotPaySettlementSecret` + `HOTPAY_SETTLEMENT_SECRET` + comma-separated `HOTPAY_SETTLEMENT_SECRETS`, so one New API instance can accept commands signed by several upstream applications. The single-secret helper is now a strict subset. Frontend: new "Settlement URL" field + updated alert copy in the HotPay billing tab; i18n keys are component-level `t(...)` (en base) so no locale-file edits were needed. Tests: `model/payment_gateway_settlement_test.go` (any-configured-secret accept + wrong/blank reject; single-secret subset), `controller/hotpay_checkout_test.go` (settlement URL preference/derivation; secret collection + dedup). Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -count=1 ./controller/... ./model/... ./service/... ./setting/...` (13 packages ok, exit 0), web `tsgo -b` typecheck clean and production build ok. OPERATIONAL: each HotPay application must carry a settlement secret equal to one of this list, an EPay pid/key pair, and allowed hosts including this site's host — HotPay now fails closed at startup otherwise.

- On 2026-09-12, per-channel concurrency limits with automatic channel switching shipped on `tokeness/main` and went live on both production sites the same day: overseas `v1.0.0-rc.36-tokeness-intl.8` (image digest `sha256:2dd720b10dc64ed22c50e7d1bcb24f8e9012f3c9ed458d5679b73c984d7bc9a1`, publish run 34676834267, deploy runs 34677279955 FAILED-then-rolled-back-healthy + retry 34678077500 success) and mainland `v1.0.0-rc.33-tokeness-mainland.26` (annotated tag on 38074fc0a, CNB tag_push `cnb-618-1k2a31obf` build+chain-deploy success). The first overseas deploy attempt auto-rolled back cleanly to intl.7 because the public-validation probe hit a transient TLS reset (`curl: (35) Connection reset by peer` on `https://tokeness.ai/api/status`, 2s after the same-domain dashboard probe already returned 200 with intl.8) — an application defect cannot reset the TLS handshake, so this was runner/CDN path flakiness; the retry with the identical digest passed all gates. Same release carries task-plugins 1.0.5-1.0.9 (openai-video-agg: rolldek upstream, resolution-suffix wan3/minimax SKUs, retired non-official wan3.0-image and grok-1.5-video; lint valid, fixture 48/48). NOTE: plugin activation is DB-side — verify `GET /api/plugin/task/runtime/status` reports active=1.0.9 per site after deploy. Config: `setting.concurrency_limit` (int, 0 = unlimited, save-time validated via `ChannelSettings.ValidateConcurrencyLimit`, max 10000) edited in the channel drawer's Routing Strategy section (field `concurrency_limit`, i18n keys "Concurrency Limit" + description in all 7 locales). Runtime (`service/channel_concurrency.go`): `AcquireChannelConcurrency(c, channel)` claims one in-flight slot per relay ATTEMPT — global exact counting via a Redis ZSET semaphore (key `channel_concurrency:v1:{channelID}`, member = fresh request id, score = expiry from Redis `TIME` so multi-node clock skew is safe; acquire script purges expired members then ZCARD/ZADD/PEXPIRE, release = ZREM + DEL when empty, both called with a 100ms timeout). Member expiry = 30 minutes, so a node that dies between acquire and release self-heals on the next acquire; requests legitimately outliving 30 min (rare very long streams) temporarily soften the limit — accepted trade-off. Redis failure fails OPEN (log + proceed ungated): the gate is protective, deliberately opposite to the fail-closed user/group rate limiters — do not "fix" without revisiting. No Redis = process-local counter fallback (dev only, per-node semantics). The limit is resolved from a precomputed `channel2concurrencyLimits` cache index (`model/channel_cache.go`, mirrors `channel2officialFitModels`; maintained in InitChannelCache + CacheUpdateChannel; `CacheGetChannelConcurrencyLimit(channel)` falls back to parsing the passed channel's settings when the memory cache is off, and never self-heals corrupt rows). Switching (`controller/relay.go` main loop only — task/MJ submit loops intentionally NOT covered, in-flight task counting would need task-lifecycle integration): after `getChannel`, acquire; on saturation `retryParam.ExcludeSaturatedChannel(id)` + `ResetRetryNextTry()` so the switch does NOT consume the RetryTimes budget and works even with RetryTimes=0; the attempt body runs in a closure whose defer releases the slot (held for the full stream/WSS session). `getChannel`'s `info.ChannelMeta == nil` early-return branch now honors `retryParam.IsChannelExcluded(contextChannelID)` — without that guard a saturated distributor channel would be re-returned forever (infinite loop). Pure saturation exhaustion (`RetryParam.HasSaturatedChannel`: every exclusion so far was saturation, no real upstream failure) surfaces as a local 429 `newChannelConcurrencySaturatedError` (SkipRetry, NoRecordErrorLog, no auto-ban/no channel-error record); pinned `specific_channel_id` requests do not switch and get the 429 directly. Channels without a limit never touch Redis (~2 RTT cost only for limited channels). Verified: full `go test ./...` green, vet, gofmt; `relaykit GOWORK=off go build` (ChannelSettings lives in relaykit); web typecheck + oxlint clean, production build ok, `bun run test` shows only the documented pre-existing baseline failures (Vitest 16 vs 21 on clean HEAD — flaky set; node:test vendor-management fail present on both); i18n parity 0 missing; full-suite failures were compared against a `git stash` baseline before concluding no regression. New tests: `service/channel_concurrency_test.go` (miniredis: cap/deny/release/self-heal/fail-open/local-fallback/unlimited), `TestRetryParamPureSaturationClassification` (service/channel_retry_test.go), cache-index sync tests (model/channel_cache_test.go), and `controller/relay_channel_saturation_test.go` proving getChannel switches away from a saturated distributor channel and 429s when all candidates are saturated (gotcha: the DB selection path needs `initCol()`/dialect globals that tests don't run — the test uses the memory-cache path via `model.InitChannelCache()`).

- On 2026-09-11 the channel editor's multi-key probe-model selector was narrowed to the channel's own models (commit `253c29db7` on `tokeness/main`, one file: `web/src/features/channels/components/drawers/channel-mutate-drawer.tsx`, 12 insertions / 1 deletion). Advanced → 多Key渠道定时测试 → 测试模型 (`multi_key_test_model`, persisted as `setting.multi_key_test.model`) previously shared the default test-model field's option list (`modelOptions` = global `/api/channel/models` catalog + channel models); it now uses a dedicated `multiKeyTestModelOptions` memo built from `currentModelsArray` (= 模型与分组 → 模型), while `allowCustomValue` stays on so an off-list/upstream-only probe model can still be typed and the field can be cleared back to "使用各渠道的测试模型". The sibling default 测试模型 (`test_model`) field deliberately keeps the full catalog — that is why the two dropdowns differ. Verified live, not just by inspection: rebuilt `web/dist` (embedded via `go:embed`), built the server binary and ran an isolated instance (`PORT=3100`, `SQL_DSN=local` + `SQLITE_PATH` pointing at a throwaway SQLite file so the real dev DB on :3000 was never touched), created a multi-key channel (`mode: "multi_to_single"` + `multi_key_mode: "random"`, models `verify-chat-a,verify-chat-b,verify-embed-c`), opened its edit drawer, toggled 定时测试密钥 on, opened the combobox → exactly those 3 models, while the still-global default 测试模型 combobox on the same drawer listed 584 (581 catalog + 3 channel models). Frontend typecheck + oxlint clean (oxfmt diff on my hunk empty; the file has pre-existing format drift, do not reformat it), `bun run test` 257 pass / 1 fail = the documented pre-existing `vendor management dialog > preserves a disabled vendor status when editing metadata` node:test baseline. Reusable gotchas for local end-to-end verification: a freshly created root account is locked behind `EmailBindingGate` → `EmailBindDialog`, which needs a real 6-digit SMTP code, so for throwaway instances set `users.email` directly in SQLite (WSL python3 cannot read the Windows WAL file — use Windows python) and restart the app so the in-memory user cache reloads; and in this session the IAB browser could not dispatch native pointer input at all (Playwright `click`, `dom_cua.click`, `cua.click` no-op'd; `screenshot` failed), yet DOM-dispatched `pointerdown/mousedown/pointerup/mouseup/click` sequences on snapshot-proven elements drove login, row menus, the drawer and the combobox.

- On 2026-09-11, the mainland channel `DEF_OpenCode-GO_A` (id 1, `https://opencode.ai/zen/go`) failed every channel probe with `400 Request is missing x-opencode-session` while real traffic on the same channel succeeded. Root cause: the synthesized probe request is built with `httptest` and sets only `Content-Type`, so it carries no `Authorization`; that channel's `param_override` copies a chain of session headers into `X-Opencode-Session` and falls back to `copy_header from Authorization` (`keep_origin: true`). With no client `Authorization` the fallback resolves nothing, the upstream console rejects the probe, and the multi-key auto-test path then disables all keys (`status_reason: "All keys are disabled"`, channel auto-disabled). Real relay traffic always carries `Authorization` (it is stripped from wildcard passthrough but still header-resolved), so production was unaffected. Fix: `seedProbeAuthorizationHeader` (controller/channel-test.go) copies the selected channel key into the probe's synthesized `Authorization` header after `SetupContextForSelectedChannel`; it never overwrites an existing value and is a no-op without a context or key. Note this is a different failure from the earlier `{client_header:<name>}` placeholder work (93be3cf29) — that covers `header_override` templates, not `param_override` `copy_header`; both are needed for a full probe path. Verified: new controller tests (`channel_test_probe_header_test.go`) plus a relay/channel test proving the copied header reaches the upstream request map; `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` clean, and full `go test ./...` green. Committed on `tokeness/main`; not deployed — the fix only takes effect in a probe once an `ml-<tag>` mainland release ships it (channel 1 stays auto-disabled until then).

- On 2026-09-08 (payment routing redesign, committed across 93783d7dc / 4d3648f7e / 400f5873b by the parallel rc35 finalize), HotPay switched from an exclusive gateway switch to per-method routing modeled on waffo_pancake. (1) New registry option `HotPayPayMethods` (setting/payment_hotpay_methods.go; entries {name, icon, type, min_topup} with type "hotpay:<canonical>" and canonical in alipay/wechat_pay/card/apple_pay/google_pay; defaults = hotpay:alipay + hotpay:wechat_pay), read via `setting.GetHotPayPayMethods()`, registered in model/option.go (read straight from OptionMap). (2) `GetTopUpInfo` appends each registered entry to pay_methods when `IsHotPayGatewayEnabled()`. (3) Dedicated endpoints POST /api/user/hotpay/pay + /api/subscription/hotpay/pay (controller/hotpay_pay.go: `RequestHotPayPay`/`SubscriptionRequestHotPayPay`) parse the hotpay: type, verify it against the registry (`isRegisteredHotPayMethodType`), then reuse the canonical checkout code; `RequestEpay`/`SubscriptionRequestEpay` lost their `IsHotPayGatewayEnabled()` takeover and now reject hotpay:* types with a redirect hint. (4) `GetEpayClient` is pure legacy again (no HOTPAY_EPAY_* override); new `getHotPayEpayVerifier` + `verifyEpayNotification` try legacy creds then the HotPay EPay pairing so both callback sources verify on the shared notify endpoints; `acknowledgeHotPayEpayNotification` now accepts any HotPay provider (waffo_pancake OR gopay_alipay) with a gateway order binding. (5) Frontend mirrors waffo_pancake: `PAYMENT_TYPES.HOTPAY_PREFIX`, `isHotPayPayment`, hotpay processor in `dispatchSelectedPayment`, `use-hotpay-payment.ts` (same-tab redirect), subscription dialog branches to `paySubscriptionHotPay`, method-based icon fallback in ui.tsx, and the HotPay tab gained a `HotPayPayMethods` registry editor reusing PaymentMethodsVisualEditor with hotpay templates. Previous round's optional-pin account change (no provider_account_id sent when unset; `BindPaymentGatewayOrderID` 4th arg backfills the routed account) is retained and was re-applied. OPERATIONAL WARNING: after this change, legacy alipay/wxpay entries follow PayAddress literally; if PayAddress still points at the HotPay EPay ingress, those orders use the compat transport that can never settle - point PayAddress back at legacy Rainbow or remove the legacy entries when enabling HotPay. Verified in a clean worktree: controller/model/service/setting/router build+vet+test green, wallet 25 frontend tests pass, i18n sync missing 0.
- On 2026-09-08 (follow-up, per user feedback): the HotPay registry was simplified. `HotPayPayMethods` now stores only channel ids (`[]string` of "hotpay:<method>"; defaults hotpay:alipay + hotpay:wechat_pay) via setting/payment_hotpay_methods.go, NOT {name,icon,type,min_topup}. `GetTopUpInfo` no longer auto-injects registered entries; the General `PayMethods` list is the single source of buyer-visible display, and a `hotpay:*` row shows only when `IsHotPayGatewayEnabled()` AND the channel is registered (`filterRegisteredHotPayMethods`). 常规 PayMethods references a registered `hotpay:<method>` and sets name/icon/min_topup there. Frontend: a new `HotPayMethodsVisualEditor` (channel-id list) replaces PaymentMethodsVisualEditor in the HotPay tab; the 常规 editor keeps the hotpay templates; locale keys updated in all 7 files. This supersedes the registry/auto-append points (1)/(2) above. Also on 2026-09-08 the `HotPayAlipayAccountID` setting field was removed entirely (`setting/payment_hotpay.go`, `model/option.go`, types/index/section-registry/payment-settings-section, tests): alipay checkout no longer forwards any account pin — `hotPayProviderAccountIDForMethod("alipay")` returns "" so HotPay routes the gopay_alipay channel by its own priority and the routed account is backfilled onto the local order (via `BindPaymentGatewayOrderID` 4th arg). Locale alipay-account keys removed from all 7 files. NOTE: the parallel rc35 session reverted this session's unstaged locale edits once during the run (the HotPay registry i18n keys vanished); they were re-applied and re-verified — keep re-checking locale key presence before reporting. On 2026-09-09: (a) the General PayMethods add/edit dialog was wired to reference registered HotPay channels — `PaymentMethodsVisualEditor` receives `hotPayTypes` (parsed from `HotPayPayMethods` via `parseHotPayTypes` in payment-settings-section) and threads it to `PaymentMethodDialog`, which appends one selectable option per registered `hotpay:<method>` (default name/icon, raw-id fallback for custom methods) so an operator can reference an already-registered HotPay type there. (b) The parallel rc35 session's sidebar work (from fix/sidebar-modules-i18n-overview) was merged: it left a `git stash pop` conflict on the 7 locale files; I resolved it as a clean union (payment keys + Audit Logs / Security & Access sidebar keys) and committed it — the sidebranch content now lives on tokeness/main (register audit & security modules + static i18n keys + translations), plus the dashboard API info-panel height tweak. The one-shot helper and scratch debug test were deleted afterwards (no untracked artifacts remain). Follow-up `918024950`: the rc35 sync also brought `web/src/hooks/__tests__/sidebar-config.test.tsx` from upstream, and two of its assertions had never passed in this fork because they expect a fixed registry order while the fork renders entries in the saved JSON key order (`getNavItemOrder` in use-sidebar-config, shipped with c222eeda5). Both were aligned to the fork semantics (Security & Access: assert presence + admin/user visibility gates, not a fixed Wallet-before-Profile position; Audit Logs: keep the legacy-config presence/active-route checks and add a separate case asserting the default config still renders Audit Logs right after Usage Logs). sidebar-config.test.tsx is now 14/14 green. KNOWN PRE-EXISTING FRONTEND FAILURES (not from the sidebar work): the full `npm test` suite has ~30 failures on tokeness/main in audit details/viewer, pricing model-cards/pricing-controls, quota-adjustment, vendor-management and setup-guide tests — they are rc35-sync i18n/format expectation mismatches (e.g. audit summaries returning English where tests expect zh) and were already failing on a clean HEAD before this merge. They are NOT covered by the release workflows (CI frontend job only runs on pull_request; publish/deploy are workflow_dispatch), so they do not block releases, but they should be fixed before enabling PR-gated frontend CI.

- **CRITICAL 2026-09-08 workspace hazard**: a parallel rc35-upstream session ran git operations mid-session that reverted ALL unstaged changes to tracked files (both HotPay rounds' edits were wiped; only untracked new files survived). All edits were replayed from context and a full snapshot is kept at workspace `private/hotpay-per-method/replay-snapshot-20260908.patch`. This happened twice on 2026-09-08. Rule of thumb: while the rc35 merge workspace has staged-but-uncommitted state, any session doing unstaged edits must re-verify (`rg` anchor checks) before reporting, and keep patch snapshots in an ignored directory (snapshot kept at workspace private/hotpay-per-method/replay-snapshot-20260908.patch).

- On 2026-09-06, the HotPay canonical checkout paths gained native alipay support (not committed; rides the next release with the empty-output affinity fix). `hotPayWalletMethod` now accepts `alipay` (CNY only; USD alipay rejected), `hotPaySubscriptionMethod` accepts `alipay` for CNY plans (Waffo Pancake has no CNY subscription products, so wechat_pay CNY subscriptions stay rejected). New helpers `hotPayProviderForMethod` (alipay→`model.PaymentProviderGoPayAlipay`="gopay_alipay", everything else→`waffo_pancake`) and `hotPayProviderAccountIDForMethod` (alipay account ID from env `HOTPAY_GATEWAY_ALIPAY_ACCOUNT_ID`, which must equal HotPay's channel `gopay_app_id`; fail-closed when unset; Waffo methods keep the existing `hotPayProviderAccountID()` fallback chain). The wallet (`topup.go`) and subscription (`subscription_payment_epay.go`) gateway branches now persist and echo the method-derived provider/account on both the pending order and the idempotent-replay mismatch check. `CompatibilityProtocol:"epay"` is deliberately kept on all canonical orders: HotPay only emits the EPay-shaped informational notify for epay-protocol orders and its replay-equality check compares the field verbatim. The settlement receiver needed zero changes (`validGatewayPaymentMethod` already includes "alipay"; wallet/subscription invariants match provider/method/account/env/amount from the command). HotPay needed zero code changes (`GoPayAlipayCanonicalProvider.Capabilities` already lists wallet_topup+subscription × CNY × alipay). Frontend needs no change (`PAYMENT_TYPES.ALIPAY` exists and default `PayMethods` includes alipay). Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./controller ./model ./service`, gofmt clean, full `go test ./...` pass. Production gates remaining: HotPay env `HOTPAY_SETTLEMENT_ENDPOINT`/`HOTPAY_SETTLEMENT_SECRET` pair (currently absent → native settlement can never complete), NewAPI per-node env (`HOTPAY_GATEWAY_URL/API_KEY/ALLOWED_HOSTS/ALIPAY_ACCOUNT_ID`, `HOTPAY_SETTLEMENT_SECRET`), real scan-to-credit evidence, and rotating the gopay_alipay merchant private key that leaked into a session transcript.

New `web/src/lib/site-flavor.ts` exposes `IS_MAINLAND_SITE` via a try/catch-guarded `import.meta.env.VITE_SITE_FLAVOR` read (plain Node has no `import.meta.env`, and node:test imports the store/headers directly; the exact expression is still textually replaced by rsbuild `source.define`, confirmed by zero leftover identifiers in dist). `currency-display-store` forces CNY in mainland: default short-circuits, `setCurrency` coerces to CNY, and a persist `merge` drops any stale saved USD preference so pre-change users cannot stay on USD. Not committed; rides the next `ml-<tag>` CNB release.

The merge keeps local Cyber Policy, content moderation, channel affinity, multi-key retry, log redaction, and task polling behavior while adopting rc33 Responses/Gemini/Claude conversion, canonical billing usage, and the new task polling contract. `BillingUsage` remains an internal billing sidecar: all OpenAI, Responses, Gemini, and Claude client output paths strip it, including Claude's nested `message_start.message.usage`. - On 2026-09-05, the page `<head>` became server-editable through the new root-only `CustomHeadHTML` option. `web/index.html` now ships a minimal structural head (charset/viewport/favicon) plus three placeholders: `<!--head-html-->` (replaced per request in `router/web-router.go` `renderIndexPage` with the option value, falling back to `common.DefaultCustomHeadHTML` — the former full Tokeness meta block — when empty/whitespace), and the existing env-driven `<!--umami-->` / `<!--Google Analytics-->` placeholders. Rendering per request makes edits effective immediately (no restart) and works on follower nodes via `SyncOptions`; the build tool's empty `<title></title>` (rsbuild `title: false as unknown as string`, typed string-only) is stripped at render time so the page keeps exactly one title. Two JS head-overrides were removed so server-rendered SEO is authoritative: `main.tsx` `initSystemBranding` no longer rewrites `document.title`/`meta[name=title]` (favicon logic kept) and the home `use-home-metadata` hook was deleted along with its tests. The admin editor lives at `/system-settings/site/html-head` (new `HeadHtmlSection`, textarea, saved via the generic option PUT). E2E verified with a disposable SQLite instance: default render, custom render after option PUT, whitespace fallback, option readback, restart persistence, and umami/GA placeholder replacement. Verification passed: focused + full `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, gofmt; frontend 246 tests, typecheck, changed-file oxlint/oxfmt, i18n sync (4 new keys across 7 locales), production build. No deployment.

- On 2026-09-04, sidebar module management became registry-driven so newly added admin entries no longer need to be wired into the sidebar-modules system by hand. `web/src/lib/sidebar-modules.ts` is now the single registration point (`SIDEBAR_MODULE_REGISTRY` + `registerSidebarModule()` for runtime registration at module load): the URL→module filter map, `SIDEBAR_MODULES_DEFAULT`, and the admin editor's section/module labels in `sidebar-modules-section.tsx` are all derived from it, and `use-sidebar-config.ts` consumes the registry instead of its own hardcoded `URL_TO_CONFIG_MAP`/`DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_MODULES`. Task Plugins (`admin.task_plugins`, `/task-plugins`, SUPER_ADMIN-gated) is registered there, so it is now enable/hide/reorderable from `/system-settings/site/sidebar-modules`. Unregistered URLs (e.g. system-settings drill-ins) keep defaulting to visible. New i18n key "Manage installed task plugins and the marketplace." added to all 7 locales. Verification passed: 247 frontend tests, typecheck, changed-file oxlint/oxfmt, i18n sync, production build. No backend change (the `SidebarModulesAdmin` option remains an opaque JSON string); no deployment.

- On 2026-09-01, local branch remediation restored task and wallet billing invariants. A missing batch-task channel now leaves the task and reserved quota pending for recovery or the existing timeout policy instead of forcing a terminal state followed by a non-idempotent refund; an upstream diagnostic reason no longer turns an explicit success into failure. Wallet settlement debits and positive refunds persist synchronously even when batching is enabled, positive changes enforce `MaxWalletQuota`, `GetUserQuota` reads the database authority, and wallet mutations invalidate rather than asynchronously increment the user snapshot. `BillingSession.Refund` synchronously advances funding, subscription-reserve, and token stages and does not repeat completed stages within the same session; cross-process compensation still requires a future durable idempotency ledger. Frontend task pricing now reuses the task expression/matrix helpers while showing multi-tier ranges, usage units, schema-owned labels, and task breakdowns, with real translations for the upstream-model-name setting. Verification passed: root `go test ./...` and `go vet ./...`; RelayKit standalone test/build with `GOWORK=off`; frontend typecheck, 674 tests, production build, locale sync, and lint/format checks on all 12 modified TypeScript files; `git diff --check`. Repository-wide frontend lint still fails on pre-existing violations in unchanged files. No push, publication, deployment, or credentials were used.

- On 2026-08-31, the security hardening batch added provider-side Waffo wallet settlement invariants: the locked top-up row now requires a positive decimal amount at the supported currency precision and an exact normalized currency match before crediting quota; repeated success notifications remain idempotent. Verification codes now store SHA-256 digests, expire/delete on lookup, are capped at five failed attempts per purpose/key, and are deleted after successful registration or email binding. Relay/dashboard CORS and trusted-proxy handling are opt-in hardening: `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` unset keeps the legacy allow-all CORS behavior and invalid values fail closed, while setting exact origins enables the strict allowlist; `TRUSTED_PROXIES` unset keeps the legacy loopback/RFC1918/ULA trust defaults and `none` or an explicit CIDR list enables exact trust. Production JP-M deliberately configures the second-hop CDN proxy IP explicitly per node docs, so all production nodes must keep explicit `TRUSTED_PROXIES` in `.env` (the legacy default does not solve the public-CDN case). Added regression tests for settlement tampering, verification attempt exhaustion, legacy-versus-strict CORS behavior, and direct-peer client IP handling. Focused tests, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, and `git diff --check` passed; no production deployment or credentials were used.

- On 2026-08-31, production logs confirmed the reported wallet pre-consume failure was caused by an asynchronous refund window: a request reserved about ¥6.527444, consumed about ¥0.267498, and returned about ¥6.259946 in a background goroutine, so a concurrent request briefly observed the reduced balance. Wallet refunds now execute synchronously both in `BillingSession.Refund` and in the negative-delta wallet `Settle` path before returning, while token/subscription follow-up remains asynchronous; `Settle` also skips sessions already marked refunded to avoid a refund/settle race. The database remains the synchronous authority; Redis cache refresh is still asynchronous. Tiered billing keeps the conservative full `max_tokens` estimate until a reservation ledger exists.
- On 2026-08-31, wallet pre-consume investigation found two correctness issues. The atomic wallet reservation remains database-authoritative and must retain its `quota >= amount` guard; `aff_quota` is not part of ordinary wallet pre-consume. The insufficient-wallet error path now reads the balance from the database instead of Redis so the diagnostic matches the reservation source. In ratio billing, ordinary pre-consume now estimates input tokens with `modelRatio` and the requested completion limit with `completionRatio`, using decimal arithmetic, matching final text settlement and avoiding float truncation drift. The conservative full `max_tokens` reservation policy remains unchanged.

  container for both axes — the old double-container layout buried the
  horizontal scrollbar at the content bottom; index/status are pinned left and

  + separate time column; four 24h metric columns merged into one tooltiped

  tests the whole enabled pool (backend `all=true`) instead of the visible

  delete: `ManageMultiKeys` gained `delete_keys` (credential_ids +
