- On 2026-08-25, `BillingSession.Settle` was corrected for the exact-match
  case: when actual usage equals the pre-consumed estimate, it now invokes the
  funding source's zero-delta settle before marking the session complete. This
  preserves wallet `used_quota`/`request_count` updates armed by
  `FoldUsageIntoWalletSettle`; `TestBillingSessionSettleExactMatchRecordsWalletUsage`
  covers the regression. A `HasActiveUserSubscription` `LIMIT 1` experiment was
  reverted because its 45-second run did not show stable improvement. Before
  later unrelated channel-selection edits, the full Go suite passed; the
  service billing/Midjourney race tests, RelayKit standalone test/build,
  formatting, and `git diff --check` also passed. During final validation,
  concurrent channel-selection edits briefly caused a signature mismatch; the
  typed selection follow-up then fixed the auto-group regressions, and the
  service package now passes its full suite and vet checks.

- On 2026-08-25, a follow-up performance audit evaluated two database toggles and
  a local `channels.used_quota` aggregator, but retained none of them. Disabling
  GORM prepared statements made the local PostgreSQL seed fail with
  `invalid input syntax for type json` while writing `channel_info`; skipping
  GORM default transactions was compatible but measured about 1.6% slower for
  non-stream traffic and had no stable gain for the other cases. The channel
  counter aggregator was tested with bounded queueing and reset locking, but
  45-second WSL2 A/B runs were not stable (nonstream +2.5%, stream -1.1%,
  body-large -4.0% in the repeat) and a process-local pending delta cannot make
  a reset from another instance observe the current value. The experiment was
  fully reverted, including its performance Compose flag; channel `used_quota`
  remains synchronous to preserve the existing multi-instance reset contract.

- On 2026-08-25, the DeepSeek V4 compatibility fix added redacted runner `scripts/feature-probe` with an 84-case matrix audit, HTTP-200 SSE top-level error normalization, and retry-path coverage for multi-key credential errors versus channel failures. Local full tests/vet/RelayKit/deployment transaction tests passed; live authenticated tiers remain runtime-injection dependent and are never marked pass without evidence.

- On 2026-08-25, the fifth performance round merged the users-row hot-path UPDATEs and established a fair same-duration comparison proving the 2x throughput target. `model.DecreaseUserQuotaWithUsage` (user.go) applies the wallet deduction and the request's used_quota/request_count statistics in ONE UPDATE; `WalletFunding.Settle` uses it when armed (`SettleBilling` accepts an optional `usageDelta` variadic and type-asserts `*BillingSession` → `FoldUsageIntoWalletSettle`, keeping the relay-layer `BillingSettler` interface untouched). `PostTextConsumeQuota` passes `summary.Quota` for wallet-billed billable requests only (subscription and non-billable paths keep the legacy separate statistics write). This also makes usage counters atomic with the deduction instead of being written even when the settle fails. Regression `TestDecreaseUserQuotaWithUsageMergesStatistics` covers the merged statement. Fair 300-second same-spec runs (concurrency 64, all HTTP 200, both sides zero errors; short 30s runs underestimate throughput because warmup effects dominate): baseline (HEAD + perf infra) vs candidate: nonstream 68.8→172.9 RPS (+151%), stream 71.7→174.6 (+143%), body-large 54.3→122.4 (+125%) — the "2x throughput" target is MET at steady state. CPU per successful request 0.417→0.272 (-35%) — the "-30% CPU" target is MET. Peak RSS 80.6→75.9 MiB (-6%) — the "-30% memory" target remains unmet; the analysis stands that the ~10.5MB tiktoken vocabulary (already lazy-loaded per model family), pgx statement cache, and Go runtime are functional requirements, so -30% memory has no safe implementation path without cutting features; pending user decision on adjusting that acceptance criterion. Full `go test ./...`, vet, relaykit build, focused race tests, gofmt, and `git diff --check` pass. Nothing deployed or published; LOG_BATCH_ENABLED stays opt-in.

- On 2026-08-25, the fourth performance round fixed a self-deadlock introduced by the round-2 fast path, reverted a counterproductive async change, and added trusted token accounting deferral, reaching +59-65% throughput. Critical bug found and fixed: the `ClaimQuotaWarning` wallet fast path was initially placed inside the `DB.Transaction` closure but executed on the outer `DB` handle — the transaction held the (single, in tests) pool connection while the fast path's `Take` waited for it, deadlocking `TestMidjourneyRefundRestoresEveryAccountingElementOnBillingChannel` and, in production pools, consuming a second connection while reading outside the transaction snapshot. The fast path now runs entirely before the transaction opens (bisected and verified by stash experiments). Round-3's async statistics counters were reverted to synchronous: with the hot-row lock queue gone, their gopool versions re-serialized the users row through the settle UPDATE (`UPDATE users` latency 400-830ms, nonstream dropped to 51 RPS in that run); they must stay ordered with the wallet settle because both UPDATE the same users row. New change: `BillingSession.Settle` defers `DecreaseTokenQuota` for trusted sessions (`delta > 0 && s.trusted`) via gopool — trusted sessions skipped pre-consume, so this accounting write is the only token mutation and no gate reads it before the next request re-loads the token; non-trusted and refund paths stay synchronous. tiktoken vocabulary memory was evaluated: cl100k loads at init, o200k lazy-loads on first `gpt-4o-*` request via `tokenEncoderMap`; the ~10.5MB vocabulary is functional cost with no safe reduction, and RSS is dominated by required structures, so the memory -30% target has no safe path without cutting features. Final same-spec results (30s, 64 concurrency, all HTTP 200, LOG_BATCH_ENABLED=true): nonstream 67.2→106.7 RPS (+59%), stream 66.0→105.6 (+60%), body-large 51.7→85.1 (+65%); CPU per successful request 0.514→0.309 (-40%); peak RSS 67.3→64.2 MiB (-5%). Full `go test ./...`, vet, focused race tests (model quota/log-batch, service billing/Midjourney), relaykit build/test, gofmt, and `git diff --check` pass. The 2x throughput target (+100%) and the -30% memory target remain unmet; throughput is now +59-65% and CPU per request -40%. Nothing deployed or published; log batching and all behavior changes behind opt-in flags or trust-path analysis documented here.

- On 2026-08-25, the third performance round added billing-safe consume-log batching. `model/log_batch.go` introduces `LOG_BATCH_ENABLED` (default off, enabled via env at startup like `BATCH_UPDATE_ENABLED`): when on, `createLog` enqueues into a bounded 10k channel and a gopool worker flushes batches (500 rows or 1s tick) via `CreateInBatches`; a full queue falls back to a synchronous insert (no silent drops under backpressure), and a failed batch retries entries individually so one bad row cannot drop its peers. Billing deduction paths are untouched — only the audit-log INSERT moves off the request path. Regression tests cover enqueue→flush→persist, queue-full fallback, and batch-failure individual retry. Same-spec run (30s, 64 concurrency, all HTTP 200, PERF_LOG_BATCH_ENABLED=true): nonstream 67.2→100.2 RPS (+49% cumulative vs fixed baseline), stream 66.0→91.2 (+38%), body-large 51.7→76.2 (+47%); CPU per successful request 0.514→0.387 (-25%); RSS ~69 MiB unchanged. The remaining synchronous post-response writes are `DecreaseUserQuota` + `DecreaseTokenQuota` (settle, different tables) — merging them into one transaction was rejected because log/quota failures must stay independent, and deferring the wallet write would break trust-mode overdraft protection since settle is the only deduction point for trusted users. The 2x throughput target is now within reach (currently +49%) but CPU -30% and memory -30% are not met; log batching is an opt-in runtime flag, so production behavior is unchanged until an operator enables it deliberately. Full test suite, vet, focused race tests, relaykit build, gofmt, and `git diff --check` pass. Nothing deployed or published.

- On 2026-08-25, the second performance round eliminated the dominant hot-row lock queue: `ClaimQuotaWarning` previously ran `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the user row inside a transaction on every request even for healthy balances, and its "reset" branch wrote `quota_warning_sent=false` unconditionally — serializing all post-response notify checks of one user behind the settle transaction on the same row. `model/quota_notification.go` now has a wallet fast path: a plain (non-locking) read checks balance + flag; a healthy balance with no outstanding warning returns without any write or lock, and a healthy balance with the flag set rearms via a single conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE quota_warning_sent = TRUE` without holding a row lock across the decision. Warning-claim paths (balance below threshold) keep the original locked transaction, preserving the one-shot episode semantics and stale-snapshot guard; regression `TestClaimQuotaWarningHealthyBalanceSkipsLockedTransaction` covers both fast-path branches, all prior state-machine tests pass, and focused race runs are clean. Same-spec re-run (30s, concurrency 64, all HTTP 200): nonstream 67.2→90.3 RPS (+34%), stream 66.0→87.7 (+33%), body-large 51.7→58.5 (+13%) versus the fixed baseline; container CPU avg rose 34.5%→43.0% purely because throughput rose — CPU per successful request dropped ~7-8% (0.514→0.476 CPU-seconds per request on nonstream). RSS stayed ~67-69 MiB. Cumulative candidate delta vs baseline: +34% nonstream throughput. The 2x/30% targets are still NOT met. Settle/log transaction merging (one tx for wallet UPDATE + token UPDATE + log INSERT) was evaluated and rejected: it would make quota deduction roll back when the log INSERT fails, violating the billing-safety invariant that deduction and logging fail independently. The full `go test ./...`, vet, relaykit build/test, `git diff --check`, and gofmt all pass; note the pre-existing `TestDecreaseUserQuotaIfEnoughConcurrentDoesNotOverdraw` race-detector failure is a test-fixture defect (async gopool cache sync reading `common.RedisEnabled` across cleanup) in files untouched by this work. Nothing deployed or published.

- On 2026-08-24, isolated relay performance work (WSL2 Docker stack: mock upstream, Postgres, Redis) re-established a reproducible baseline and measured the first real optimization. The earlier candidate run's 68,520 HTTP 503s were root-caused to two environment faults, not channel disabling: (1) the `SystemPerformanceCheck` middleware is enabled by default with a 90% CPU threshold and gopsutil reads host-level `/proc/stat` inside the container, so load-generated host CPU rejected every `/v1` request in microseconds; (2) the default GORM pool (1000 conns) exhausted Postgres `max_connections=100` under 64-way load (SQLSTATE 53300) via queued `ClaimQuotaWarning` `FOR UPDATE` transactions. Fixes are test-env only: `scripts/performance/seed` disables `performance_setting.monitor_enabled` through the option API, `docker-compose.performance.yml` caps `SQL_MAX_OPEN_CONNS=90` and raises Postgres `max_connections=300`, and `run-wsl.sh` refuses to record official results when warmup has errors and warns when official scenarios have failures. Production code keeps behavior: `model/log.go` skips consume-log JSON serialization when DEBUG is off; `model/channel_cache.go` precomputes priority/weight selection metadata at cache refresh (single-channel, blocked-channel, requestPath, and status-update paths fall back to the original slow path, metadata is discarded on cache mutation); `service/text_quota.go` moves the two statistics counters (`UpdateUserUsedQuotaAndRequestCount`, `UpdateChannelUsedQuota`) onto gopool after the client response is written, removing two serial DB round-trips per request while `SettleBilling` stays synchronous. Verified same-spec runs (30s, concurrency 64, all HTTP 200): baseline 67.2/66.0/51.7 RPS vs candidate 69.5/67.4/53.0 RPS (nonstream/stream/body-large, +2-3.4%), container CPU avg 34.5%→31.6%, RSS ~67 MiB unchanged. pprof (25s CPU sample under 67 RPS load) shows total CPU utilization only ~24%: the throughput ceiling is the remaining serial post-response DB path (SettleBilling wallet UPDATE + token quota + consume-log INSERT, each its own transaction) on WSL2 fsync latency, plus per-request subscription count query in `billing_session.go`; channel selection and consume-log serialization were confirmed NOT bottlenecks (the channel-cache fast path gained nothing measurable). The 2x throughput / 30% CPU+memory targets are NOT met; no claims beyond the measured deltas. The next meaningful lever is BATCH_UPDATE_ENABLED-style batching or transaction merging for the settle/log path, which changes billing timing semantics and needs its own design. Full `go test ./...`, vet, relaykit build/test, race tests on model/middleware/service, and `git diff --check` passed. Nothing was deployed or published.

- On 2026-08-24, a read-only production audit of Isen found `/v1/responses` requests selecting `CN_Ollama` channel 65 (priority 10), which rejects that endpoint locally as `400 invalid_request` with `skip_retry`; `use_channel` therefore contained only `65` even though the `default` group had lower-priority alternatives and `RetryTimes=3`. The local fix classifies Ollama endpoint-capability failures as `channel:unsupported_endpoint`, preserves retryability through helper wrappers, and excludes them from automatic channel disabling, so the existing retry loop can select the next priority channel. Focused controller/service/Ollama tests, RelayKit standalone tests, vet, and diff checks passed. Production was not changed or deployed.

- On 2026-08-24, the OpenAI-compatible stream and non-stream paths were patched locally to estimate completion tokens when an upstream usage event has prompt tokens but zero completion tokens and the gateway has already received output text. Exact nested OpenAI output tokens and top-level totals are preferred before estimation; prompt/cache fields remain authoritative, total tokens and nested OpenAI billing usage are synchronized, and estimated fallback is marked as `billing-usage-openai-estimated` for auditability. A regression test covers `gemini-3.7-flash` prompt-only usage with `finish_reason=length`; the provider finish reason is now retained in `stream_status` so `length`, `stop`, and transport end states can be distinguished. Focused OpenAI/helper/service/Gemini tests, vet, and isolated `go test ./relay/...` pass. The candidate is not deployed. The supplied test key could not reach this path because its `Claude-Official` group currently exposes no available channels.

- On 2026-08-24, a read-only production audit found `gemini-3.7-flash` output-token zeros concentrated on OpenAI-compatible channel 125 (`Gemini_walk`, `st.walkcoding.top`): the latest 48-hour window had 51 streamed consume rows, 50 with `completion_tokens=0`, all zero rows marked `stream_status=ok/end_reason=done`. The relay's `ValidUsage` accepts prompt-only SSE usage, so `OaiStreamHandler` skips `ResponseText2Usage` and settles completion at zero; native Gemini has an analogous zero-completion patch but the OpenAI-compatible path did not. The local candidate now estimates completion from forwarded content while retaining upstream prompt/cache fields and synchronizing nested OpenAI billing usage. `ok/done` excludes New API scanner timeout/client disconnect as the primary cause of truncation; the focused regression uses `finish_reason=length` to prove New API forwards an upstream length stop unchanged. Production was not changed or deployed.

- On 2026-08-24, the channel-blocking design in `Tech-Spec.md` was expanded into a base-user-group `GroupAccessPolicy`. One policy per subject group can deny channels, models, and target groups, plus explicitly exempt that group from platform content moderation. Existing `GroupSpecialUsableGroup` remains the source of truth for established group allow/deny mappings; the new policy is an overlay. Moderation exemptions default-deny on errors and invalidate moderation caches when changed. No code, database schema, cache state, deployment, or credentials were changed.

- On 2026-08-23, the Ollama simulated prompt-cache path was hardened after production comparison showed `CN_Ollama` cache rates far below other channels. Explicit upstream `cached_tokens: 0` is now treated as an upstream miss while positive values remain authoritative; each partition retains a bounded set of recent prompt chains and chooses the longest strict prefix, preserving interleaved token-fallback conversations and legacy Redis `h/p` entries. `HybridCache.UpdateWithTTL` uses Redis WATCH retries for atomic read-modify-write and serializes the in-memory fallback. Cache failures remain fail-open but are returned and logged with channel/model metadata without prompt or credential data. Focused Ollama/cachex tests, race tests, targeted vet, RelayKit build, and diff checks passed. No production deployment was performed.

- On 2026-08-23, wallet and subscription quota warnings became one-shot per low-balance episode. The users table stores separate atomic notification claims; repeated requests while the same balance remains below its threshold no longer resend, and recovery above the threshold rearms the next warning. Full Go tests and targeted vet passed; no deployment was performed.

- On 2026-08-19, the rc25 audit fixes were completed in the candidate branch. Wallet and token reservations now use conditional SQL decrements as the authorization source; Redis is only an asynchronous mirror and is invalidated on synchronization failure. Batch quota flushes re-enqueue failed writes. Compact model aliases, wildcard pricing/ratio fallbacks, and Codex compact mappings were restored. Multi-key credential tests use a bounded job buffer so cancellation cannot strand the producer behind exited workers; the UI keeps the cancel action while a timed-out task remains pending/running and derives its polling budget from key count. OAuth binding updates validate the target user and require one affected row inside a transaction. Async task refunds now keep the quota marker when token-quota restoration fails. No commit, image publication, or deployment was performed.
- On 2026-08-19, Midjourney billing hardening was added before production preparation. Asynchronous status polling now updates only task/result fields and cannot overwrite quota, token, or billing-channel markers from a stale snapshot. Legacy settlement compensates a successful wallet charge when token charging fails, clears billing state only after compensation, and reports billing success only when both funding and token stages succeed. Midjourney refunds now retain the durable quota marker when token restoration cannot be completed. Regression coverage verifies the partial-settlement compensation path.

- On 2026-08-19, the Tokeness branch integrated upstream `v1.0.0-rc.25` in a
  review-only merge candidate. Atomic wallet quota ceilings and Redis/DB
  reservation fallback are combined with HotPay, Waffo Pancake, Stripe, Creem,
  Waffo, and EPay settlement guards; compact alias routing, Midjourney
  credential/proxy snapshots, and local channel observability remain intact.
  Channel `used_quota` intentionally bypasses process batching so transactional
  reset reads the current value. Frontend Vitest and the existing Bun
  `node:test` suite are run as separate runners. Full backend, relaykit,
  frontend typecheck/build/tests, i18n sync, and vet checks passed; the Windows
  HTTP/2 graceful-GOAWAY loopback test is skipped because the platform
  transport cannot reliably reconnect after GOAWAY. No image was published or
  production deployment performed.

- On 2026-08-18, ordinary newline-based multi-key channel creation and editing gained an optional per-key proxy line format: a strict `http`, `https`, `socks5`, or `socks5h` URL on the next non-empty line belongs to the preceding key. The frontend sends ordered `multi_key_credentials` while `channels.key` stores secrets only; the backend also parses legacy raw text, persists proxies atomically in `channel_credentials`, preserves existing proxy modes on append, resets omitted proxies to inherit on replace, and reveals the same multiline format only through the existing root plus second-factor protected key endpoint. Vertex/Codex JSON credential formats remain on their legacy paths.
- On 2026-08-18, legacy Codex OAuth multi-key updates preserve each credential as one JSON object: append accepts valid objects or object arrays and stores the merged set as a JSON array, so formatted JSON cannot be split on its internal newlines. The channel mutation UI maps structured multi-key and Codex append validation failures through all supported locales.
- On 2026-08-18, `/system-info` was added to the administrator sidebar module contract as `admin.system_info`, including editor metadata, default configuration, and URL-to-module ordering. It can now be enabled, hidden, and moved from `/system-settings/site/sidebar-modules`; regression tests cover default presence and saved ordering.
- On 2026-08-18, compact API information items on `/keys` now show the route and API URL inline with copy and latency-test actions; the localized description is attached to the route through the shared theme-aware Tooltip instead of consuming the URL's horizontal space. Regression coverage verifies URL visibility, hidden inline description, and the Tooltip trigger.
- On 2026-08-18, the channel observability dialog explicitly overrides the shared dialog's `sm:max-w-2xl` rule with a responsive `min(96vw, 1440px)` maximum, so desktop model columns are not clipped by the default 42rem width. A component regression test verifies the responsive width class is present and the default width class is absent.
- On 2026-08-18, the shared Tooltip surface now uses `popover`/`popover-foreground` semantic tokens for both content and arrow colors, preventing light tooltip panels in dark mode. A focused DOM regression test covers the themed classes; the full frontend suite, typecheck, changed-file lint/format, and production build passed.
- On 2026-08-18, OpenAI compact model requests gained an exact-first runtime alias fallback. When a requested model ends in `-openai-compact`, the distributor checks the effective group for the exact alias first and only falls back to the suffix-free base model when the alias is absent and the candidate supports the current request path. Advanced Custom routes are filtered before precedence is applied, including Auto groups, affinity, retries, and specific-channel selection. The resolved base is carried through channel selection and token model-limit validation, while the client-facing compact name remains the billing/rate-limit identity. Relay applies channel mappings after the alias and rewrites only the upstream request model. Model discovery responses are unchanged.
- On 2026-08-17, public header navigation became administrator-configurable through the existing `HeaderNavModules` option: Home is fixed first, built-in entries can be reordered or hidden, and custom entries carry a title, safe internal or HTTP(S) URL, visibility, and new-tab flag. `SidebarModulesAdmin` now preserves section/module JSON key order for rendering and editing, while `/api/status.invoice_enabled` hides both user and administrator invoice entries when billing invoices are disabled. Wallet payment methods use intrinsic flex-wrap sizing with a full-width mobile fallback and a 12rem desktop minimum instead of stretched grid columns, and Playground uses a conversation icon. Verification passed 368 frontend tests, typecheck, changed-file lint/format, i18n sync, production build, focused controller status tests, and public-header browser checks at 1440x900, 1280x800, 1024x800, and 390x844 with no horizontal overflow. Authenticated settings and wallet pixel checks were limited by the absence of a local browser login session.

- On 2026-08-17, the console UX final audit closed the remaining observability, multi-key, announcement, Playground, pricing, and public-navigation gaps. Channel p95 keeps millisecond resolution for new samples, Redis records TTFT and no-TTFT coverage explicitly, model aggregation keys are collision-safe for slash-containing IDs, and availability status segments open the detail view without losing row interaction. The observability dialog fits its columns without horizontal scrolling; multi-key diagnostics preserve upstream status/errors while masking credentials and handling malformed indexes; API information is compactly integrated into `/keys`; announcement and timeline revisions use one bell-driven list across remounts; Playground request state, drafts, attachments, mode/model/parameters, title search, ordering, and image downloads are session-isolated; tiered pricing retains struck-through original prices and two-decimal ratio labels. Public nav text remains centered, utility controls sit immediately left of the bell, and search is an icon button. Verification passed 351 frontend tests, frontend typecheck/build, changed-file lint and format checks (23 TS/TSX files), i18n sync, full Go tests, Go vet, race tests, and independent relaykit test/build/vet. Full-repository lint still reports pre-existing baseline violations outside this change. No production deployment or image publication was performed. `MEMORY.md` was updated.

- On 2026-08-17, the console UX follow-up hardened multi-key diagnostics: provider/API-key/Bearer credentials are masked before persistence or display, channel-test bad-response errors explicitly retain the upstream HTTP status (including wrapped API errors), and credential-test persistence failures are logged with channel and credential IDs instead of being silently discarded. Verification passed the focused and full Bun frontend suites (349 tests), frontend typecheck, production build, i18n sync, changed-file format/lint checks, focused Go tests, relaykit build/test/vet, and the full Go suite except the known Windows HTTP/2 graceful-GOAWAY transport test. No production deployment was performed.

- On 2026-08-16, New API added channel/model observability and durable multi-key credential management. `channel_model_perf_metrics` records request and attempt outcomes, cache coverage, fixed-bucket p95 latency, downstream TTFT, and upstream FRT; the current bucket is aggregated through Redis and completed buckets are persisted to SQL. Multi-key credentials now have stable IDs/fingerprints, optimistic revisions, transactional batch enable/disable, asynchronous bounded availability tests, and per-key `inherit`/`direct`/`custom` proxy modes reused by HTTP, WebSocket, and async polling paths. Key tests do not charge quota or create consume logs, and automatic channel error handling now updates the in-memory credential mirror so disabled keys are skipped immediately. Verification passed for the focused and full Go test suites, targeted Go vet, frontend typecheck/lint, production build check, and diff whitespace checks; no production deployment was performed by this change.
- On 2026-08-16, the multi-key proxy audit was closed for asynchronous result paths and auxiliary channel operations. Video content/result resolution now uses the task's credential ID and proxy snapshot; Midjourney tasks persist the selected credential and effective proxy and reuse them for polling, actions, image forwarding, and video forwarding. Ollama management, upstream model discovery, and channel balance helpers now resolve a single enabled key and its matching effective proxy. Legacy tasks without a stable identity fall back to an enabled credential without exposing the raw key. No production deployment was performed.

- On 2026-08-16, Gemini cache investigation found two independent failure classes. Upstream direct replays with identical request body hashes alternated between `cachedTokens=12263` and `cachedTokens=0`, including both streaming and non-streaming calls, proving provider cache-domain/key/shard instability can cause real misses. Independently, `relay/channel/gemini/relay-gemini.go` replaced usage on every SSE metadata event, so a later incomplete metadata event could clear an earlier `cachedContentTokenCount`; `patchGeminiZeroCompletionUsage` also rebuilt estimated Gemini billing metadata without copying `PromptTokensDetails.CachedTokens`, while settlement prefers that billing object. OpenAI-compatible streaming had the same last-usage replacement pattern. The fix now merges non-zero usage fields across events and carries Gemini cache counts into estimated billing metadata; regression tests cover both paths. No production deployment was performed.
- The 2026-08-16 streamed-usage merge contract keeps Gemini billing metadata and protocol-neutral usage aligned after partial events: thoughts and candidates are combined, total tokens cannot be lower than the derived prompt plus completion total, and modality detail slices merge by modality. OpenAI-compatible SSE forwards the merged usage in the last usage event and preserves unknown upstream usage extensions. Every public OpenAI Chat, OpenAI Responses, Claude, and Gemini output removes internal `billing_usage` from a client copy while retaining the internal object for settlement.
- The 2026-08-17 JP-M blue-green contract matches the live topology: host slots are 8201/8202, the OpenResty container is selected by its `/opt/1panel/www` host mount, and only explicitly marked proxy entries are rewritten. `cleanup-pending` recovery switches back to the verified old slot if the new active slot is unhealthy. Forced commands report version `2026-08-17.4`; fleet preflight rejects stale commands, and `install-remote-command.sh` installs the reviewed script atomically with SHA-256 verification. Standby containers start with Compose service aliases so internal EPay callbacks continue resolving `new-api` after old-slot cleanup.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.24-tokeness-release-safety.1` from commit `8e0aa2a186bd40bd070babb746433b687f0e82b4` was published at digest `sha256:d07acfe3d925ad657045af058cab3b4ba7ee2525cbb70874dfe3f4ab91252ee3`. Publish workflow `31970855268`, fleet verification `31971386401`, and production deploy `31971533364` all passed. Start times were JP-N2 `2026-08-16T20:50:20.989110278Z`, JP-N4-EV `2026-08-16T20:50:38.246215959Z`, JP-M `2026-08-16T20:51:22.132163033Z`, and JP-N0-EV `2026-08-16T20:51:57.108731982Z`. All nodes run forced-command `2026-08-17.3` with SHA-256 `d348749736005be8a3cbf72de05997f16c7af5f99e31885c51dcccbaab76827a` and `root:root:755`. JP-M is committed on `127.0.0.1:8202` in `new-api-blue`; the old `new-api` slot is removed, the managed marker is active, and the OpenResty live route passes verification. Public availability probes returned `360/360` HTTP 200 with zero 502 responses. Authenticated production Playground SSE acceptance entered the streaming state and completed with the expected five-line response; the UI reported 32.94 seconds without interruption.
- JP-M blue-green deployment uses explicit adjacent markers in the form `# TOKENESS_BLUE_GREEN_MANAGED host=<public-hostname>` and never infers ownership or probe hosts from loopback ports or 1Panel directory names. Recovery reloads and probes the live OpenResty route before draining, requiring the target version and per-container status identity to match; a failed live-route probe immediately restores the verified previous slot. `cleanup-pending` persists old-slot cleanup across interruption, and established connections in a running old container's `/proc/<pid>/net/tcp{,6}` namespace must reach zero before it receives a stop signal; an already exited old container is removed directly. Once the new route is verified and `cleanup-pending` is durable, the node reports deployment success to the fleet. Later commands retry cleanup, but a still-pending cleanup blocks another deploy so its recovery record cannot be overwritten. `new-api-slave` retains the recreate flow.
- Channel-affinity default expansion is historical-default-only: startup compares strict compacted JSON snapshots for the five unique two-rule values shipped from `c91d07466` through `dad57a6bb`, preserving number lexemes, key order, and duplicate keys so edited values are not normalized into a match. The master-only migration reads with `lockForUpdate` and writes with a key/value CAS, so an administrator save wins over a stale migration read.
- The pricing billing-expression preview supports backend-compatible `hour`, `minute`, `weekday`, `month`, and `day` functions. It evaluates all functions against one captured instant, uses IANA timezones through `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, and matches the backend fallback to UTC for empty or invalid timezone names.

- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-business-analysis-performance.1` from merge `997570b4b` (PR #34) was published by workflow `30782403421` at digest `sha256:e73cb659314fe91331c2fc52fb89ee143121069db1946db513c7333e51f92890` and deployed by staged workflow `30783068222`. The business-analysis UI now follows the browser-local CNY/USD selector and renders one currency amount per value without raw quota text; consumption aggregation changed from 22 serial period queries to two conditional aggregate queries, and the frontend report cache is five minutes. The staged rollout verified the four nodes; public `tokeness.io/api/status` returned 200 with the release version, the unauthenticated business-analysis route returned 401, and `n.tokeness.io` plus `n-cf.tokeness.io` `/v1/models` returned the expected 401.

- This repository is the Tokeness-maintained fork of `QuantumNous/new-api`. The default integration branch is `tokeness/main`; `origin` is `l1i1/new-api` and `upstream` is read-only.
- Tokeness production images are built from `Dockerfile.tokeness`, published to GHCR, and deployed by immutable digest through the staged four-node workflow. Do not deploy mutable tags or bypass the production approval environment.
- The canonical frontend is `web/`. Tokeness-specific UI work must be implemented in the React source and built into the application image, not injected into compiled HTML by Nginx or a CDN-hosted script.
- The historical injected frontend lives outside this repository under `tools/nginx-dev-proxy` and its independent `repo-newapi-webdist` repository. It remains a rollback reference while the native migration is in progress.
- Dynamic administrator-authored content uses the compact Tokeness localization syntax `<tnt l="zh">...</tnt>`. Localization belongs at explicit display boundaries and must not mutate arbitrary API responses or install global fetch/XHR/DOM hooks.
- `<tnt>` resolves one adjacent translation group at a time using active language, English, then Chinese fallback. Invalid, duplicate, nested, unsupported, and unclosed tags remain raw; the legacy `<tokeness-text>` tag and language-keyed JSON are intentionally unsupported.
- Native `<tnt>` display boundaries include home/about/legal/footer, notices and announcements, FAQ/API information, model descriptions, wallet/payment/subscription copy, chat preset names, and `UserUsableGroups` descriptions. Editors, API caches, chat URLs, group IDs, ratios, payment IDs, and request payloads always retain raw values.
- Content-format detection must run after `resolveTntContent`; raw `<tnt>` tags resemble HTML and can otherwise force localized Markdown through the HTML renderer. The invoice notice follows this ordering before choosing the sanitized Markdown or HTML boundary.
- Interface language code `zhTW` must survive browser-detector cache reloads and map to document/Intl locale `zh-TW`. Home metadata owns localized SEO on `/` and restores the configured system name on SPA navigation. Localized TanStack accessor rows must be invalidated when the language changes so visible sorting is not stale.
- Wallet add-funds amounts are always presented in CNY regardless of the global quota display type. Preset/top-up credit values convert system USD with the configured USD-to-CNY rate, while payable and savings values remain the backend-calculated CNY amounts and receive formatting only. Do not restore the historical client-side payment formula.
- Waffo and Waffo Pancake amount-preview endpoints are currency-aware. Waffo still converts according to its configured provider currency; Waffo Pancake wallet checkout now follows the frontend CNY/USD selector, sends the selected currency and matching amount, stores `PaymentCurrency` accordingly, and validates signed/manual settlements against that snapshot. New wallet products created by the admin pair flow advertise both CNY and USD. Pancake subscription checkout remains on the plan currency because the provider's current CNY market is one-time only.
- Production deployment treats both previous and target images as trusted immutable GHCR digests. A node result is successful only after final runtime-image, state, health/status, and version checks; interruption and ambiguous SSH results participate in idempotent rollback.
- The production rollout job reserves 240 minutes for the complete forward and reverse transaction. After an ambiguous SSH result, rollback reconciliation waits for the remote lock and repeatedly verifies or restores the preflight digest within a 20-minute per-node budget; transaction tests cover full-fleet success, CDN-triggered reverse rollback, and lock-release recovery.
- The native Tokeness public header exposes language, theme, and navigation controls at mobile widths. Browser acceptance must keep the language switch reachable at 390 px and confirm no horizontal overflow after changing languages.
- The mobile public-header language entry is protected by a component test that finds it through the navigation-menu control group, opens the real language menu, changes the active language, and verifies the opt-out prop removes both desktop and mobile entries.
- Real-browser acceptance on 2026-07-27 confirmed that isolated administrator HTML is rendered through a Shadow DOM after DOMPurify removes scripts, event attributes, and event handlers. Happy DOM is not treated as equivalent security evidence.
- Production `HomePageContent` is empty. The React-native Tokeness home is the only home-page source of truth; do not restore placeholder content or Nginx/CDN CSS and JavaScript injection.
- Never store production credentials, private keys, API keys, database connection strings, or secret values in this file or the repository.
- The browser-local CNY/USD display preference applies to balance, pricing, recharge previews, credited order amounts, paid-order amounts, and API usage costs. It only changes UI formatting with the configured USD exchange rate; it never changes price ratios, payment requests, account values, orders, API contracts, or billing calculations. New top-ups persist an immutable `payment_currency` source snapshot: CNY and USD payment amounts convert for display, while EUR/other currencies and legacy orders without a snapshot remain in their original/raw unit.
- On 2026-07-28, the complete production login 429 root cause was a two-hop trust failure: EV-JP2 resolved CDN client IPs correctly, but JP-M did not trust EV-JP2's public proxy address, so all non-`/v1` dashboard requests shared the EV-JP2 `rateLimit:v2` bucket. JP-M now trusts only the exact EV-JP2 `/32` in addition to loopback/private Docker networks; Global API and Critical limits are enabled. Public status, password-login, and passkey-begin probes returned 200, three repeated passkey probes stayed at 200, and correlated JP-M logs contained the actual client IPv6. The signed-cookie compatibility fix is released as `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-session.1` at digest `sha256:220018991ddccba27277b8557e229bca0dbcbffb8ff1c4478c7feb4b5f8477a4` on all four nodes.
- Channel `used_quota` increments must remain direct atomic database writes. Tokeness runs multiple New API processes against one database, so per-process batch buffers can replay pre-reset deltas and make an operator-cleared counter reappear. The `/channels` reset operation therefore reads and clears the channel under one database row lock; consume logs remain the historical billing source of truth.
- Channel used-quota reset now also supports `POST /api/channel/used_quota/reset` with an `ids` array. The batch path deduplicates and deterministically locks selected rows in one transaction, then preserves the existing per-channel reset audit records; stale IDs are ignored when at least one selected channel still exists.
- On 2026-07-28, the next official upstream candidate merged `QuantumNous/new-api:main@c3db41407` into `codex/sync-upstream-20260728`. Upstream extracted protocol DTOs, relay formats, and conversion code into the independently buildable `relaykit` module; Tokeness cache/passthrough usage regression tests must import `relaykit/dto` and `relaykit/types`. Root Go build/tests, independent `relaykit` build/tests, frontend typecheck/tests/build, i18n synchronization, workflow lint, and deployment transaction tests passed. The broad frontend lint baseline still contains upstream violations outside the Tokeness conflict-resolution files. No image was published and production was not deployed by the merge task.
- Pricing vendors keep `Vendor.Name` as the canonical unique identity used by model sync, filters, URLs, counts, and colour seeds. Localized vendor labels belong in the optional `Vendor.DisplayName` field using `<tnt>`; `/api/pricing` exposes both fields and the frontend localizes only at display boundaries. Never put `<tnt>` in the canonical vendor name because exact-name sync would create duplicates.
- Region-based discovery compliance applies to `/api/pricing`, `/api/user/self/groups`, `/api/user/models`, `/v1/models`, the Anthropic/Gemini model-list representations, and `/v1/models/:model`. It defaults to country `CN`, model keywords `gpt,gemini,claude,grok`, and group keywords `gpt,gemini,claude,grok,genpic`; administrators can replace each list. Auto discovery uses only surviving groups, and a restricted direct model lookup returns the existing `model_not_found` response. This is discovery filtering only and does not deny a direct relay request for a known model name. Region-sensitive discovery responses are private/no-store and emit the matched lower-case country in `X-Compliance-Filtered`; `/api/pricing` also emits `X-Pricing-Filtered`. Unknown country remains unfiltered.
- Compliance GeoIP client lookup reads `CF-IPCountry`, `EO-Client-IPCountry`, `CF-Connecting-IP`, `EO-Client-IP`, `X-Real-IP`, `X-Forwarded-For`, Gin `ClientIP`, and the TCP peer. A configured country result from either country header filters immediately; non-matching or unrecognized country-header values continue to GeoIP, so the deployment proxy chain must overwrite and sanitize all supported forwarded headers. Supported settings are `compliance_geoip.enabled`, `.country_codes`, `.model_keywords`, `.group_keywords`, `.retry_backoff_minutes`, `.db`, `.url`, and `.sha256`, with corresponding `COMPLIANCE_GEOIP_*` environment defaults. The defaults are enabled with a five-minute retry backoff; lists must be non-empty with at most 64 entries, country codes are two ASCII letters, and retry backoff is 1-1440 minutes. The earlier `PRICING_GEOIP_*` and `pricing_geoip.*` names are neither read nor migrated. Database-backed values override environment variables when non-empty. When the database path is missing, the first lookup downloads `GeoLite2-Country.mmdb` over HTTPS and can verify SHA-256; failure remains fail-open during the configured backoff and is then retried. The administrator billing UI exposes all supported options under `GeoIP Compliance`; request-header trust, HTTPS enforcement, download timeout, and size limit remain fixed security controls.
- API Info `route` and `description` fields are localized only in `ApiInfoItemComponent`; `url` and the source API objects remain raw. Production `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-localization.1` renders the Chinese and English labels correctly on a fresh dashboard load. Because the frontend is an SPA with hashed bundles, tabs that stayed open across a release can keep showing the old rendering until reloaded; production acceptance must use a fresh navigation or reload before diagnosing a deployment regression.
- Tokeness theme customization defaults are `sunset-glow` and `none`. Apply stored or default customization to the DOM before React mounts to prevent a first-paint preset/radius flash; existing valid cookies remain authoritative and reset removes custom cookies before restoring these defaults.
- The native wallet keeps backend-calculated payment amounts. Its custom amount row may show the configured CNY base-price multiplier as an explanatory label, but the payable amount must always come from the backend calculation and be formatted as CNY without conversion. Standard EPay keeps the payable row and redirect reminder in the confirmation dialog; Stripe, Waffo, Waffo Pancake, and Creem omit that row because their checkout owns settlement display. The affiliate card matches historical `home.js`: it states the automatic 20% first-top-up reward, shows only invite count and referral link, and omits pending/history balances and manual transfer because native settlement credits ordinary inviter quota directly.
- The wallet referral card now reads the authenticated user's native invite-reward ledger through `GET /api/user/invite-topup-rewards`. The endpoint is self-scoped, cache-disabled, paginated, and returns only server-owned program/rate metadata, applied/pending/skipped summary counts, applied-only reward total, and sanitized reward status items. The UI keeps the historical link/copy flow, displays completed first-top-ups, total CNY-equivalent rewards, processing count, and recent statuses with independent loading, empty, and retryable error states. It never renders invitee identity, order/provider data, base top-up amount, `aff_quota`, or transfer controls.
- Native invite first-top-up rewards use `top_ups.credited_quota` plus the `invite_top_up_rewards` database outbox. EPay, Stripe, Creem, Waffo, and Waffo Pancake create one pending event inside settlement; payout to ordinary inviter quota is atomic with ledger status and retries through a master-node system task. The feature is fail-closed unless `INVITE_FIRST_TOPUP_REWARD_ENABLED=true` and an explicit `INVITE_FIRST_TOPUP_REWARD_START_TIMESTAMP` are shared by every node. Stop and audit the legacy Tokeness Ops cron before enabling it; never infer that legacy `pending`/`failed` JSON entries are unpaid.
- On 2026-07-31, production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-business-analysis.1` from merge `25ca520cf1f2ccf1d45be9c306c0f3aac6c069e0` was published at GHCR digest `sha256:5e32b9bca32cc245f6fa7bc3520f20c485f0d9d2561d597373e7793c834be213`. Deployment workflow `30632267567` completed the staged rollout across JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 with runtime-image, health/status, version, and CDN checks passing. Public `tokeness.io` and both API CDN domains exposed the new version; unauthenticated `GET /api/tokeness/business-analysis` correctly returned HTTP 401. The route remains administrator-protected; authenticated production browser acceptance was not run because the available browser session had no administrator login, while local authenticated acceptance had already passed.
- On 2026-07-31, a production TTFT investigation found the main delay is upstream channel first-byte latency and channel selection: GPT channels 97 and 13 share the same priority and zero weight, while recent EV-JP2 logs showed roughly 6.7 s and 6.4 s average FRT respectively; channel 33 was roughly 12.8 s. The OpenAI SSE relay buffers the first `data:` event until the next event before forwarding, so client-visible TTFT can exceed the recorded upstream FRT. Current FRT metrics are set when the scanner reads the first upstream event and therefore under-report client-visible TTFT. EdgeOne added about 0.56-2.24 s in controlled probes versus about 0.64-0.67 s through Cloudflare; OpenResty buffering is already disabled. Do not treat CDN configuration as the primary fix before slow channels and the SSE first-chunk behavior are addressed.
- On 2026-07-31, an unauthenticated large-body CDN isolation probe (three samples each, 401 responses, so not a model TTFT measurement) found no stable EdgeOne-versus-Cloudflare gap: for 256 KiB and 1 MiB bodies Cloudflare medians were about 0.9 s and 0.6 s slower, while for 4 MiB and 8 MiB bodies EdgeOne was about 0.5 s and 0.2 s slower. Per-request jitter was several seconds. Large-context comparisons must separate client upload, CDN/origin transfer, New API preprocessing, upstream model FRT, and downstream SSE flush; do not infer a fixed CDN penalty from short requests.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-waffo-currency.1` from merge `ef49c0a5a` runs on JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 at image digest `sha256:6c0896e89c0ca20bc0da2fd88f94c20bbbfe9c8429e6662de37d0087537cb4d9`; the staged rollout completed successfully with JP-N2, EV-JP, and EV-JP2 healthy and JP-M passing its internal status probe without a Docker healthcheck. The dashboard route and CDN workflow probes exposed the new version; subsequent direct EdgeOne probes may still return the previously documented transient 520 response reset.
- User creation must persist `InviterId` in both `Insert` and OAuth `InsertWithTx`. Registration-time rewards alone are not a durable invitation relationship and cannot support later first-top-up settlement.
- Invitation attribution is separate from payment rewards: a valid registration increments the inviter's `aff_count` immediately even when compliance is unconfirmed or legacy `QuotaForInviter` is zero; the first-top-up ledger remains empty until a qualifying successful settlement.
- Tokeness home metadata is localized at the React home-route boundary and restored on SPA navigation. `web/index.html` carries the English Tokeness title, description, keywords, Open Graph, and Twitter fallback for crawlers while preserving the New API generator attribution.
- The native Tokeness home owns its complete footer. Do not render the generic New API `Footer` below it; preserve New API attribution as a localized link beside `© 2026 Tokeness. All rights reserved.` inside the Tokeness footer. Browser acceptance must verify one Tokeness footer, no generic footer, no horizontal overflow, and visible LM Speed and legal links at desktop and 390 px in both themes.
- Native home parity is measured against the historical `home.js`, including its pinned OpenAI, Gemini, and Qwen SVG artwork and its locale behavior: `zh-TW` uses the legacy simplified-Chinese home copy, while `ru`, `ja`, and `vi` use the legacy compact English fallback. Do not let icon-library upgrades or broad translation synchronization silently change this page-specific contract.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-home-parity.1` from merge `198fcc227` runs on all four New API nodes at image digest `sha256:f2c3be4c77b152d697291ba3e3ce1e77d2c32edb013afaee537260ff49275611`. The JP-M legacy home CSS/JavaScript and `/static` rewrite directives were removed from the active OpenResty config after backup, config validation, and reload; EV-JP2 has no matching injection rules. Public browser acceptance on 2026-07-28 confirmed legacy `home.js` structural and locale parity, one Tokeness footer, no legacy injected assets, no horizontal overflow, and visible LM Speed/legal links at 1440x900 and 390x844 in both themes.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-wallet-seo.1` from merge `b71655cb3` runs on JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 at image digest `sha256:18dfc4144b2ab2c544aa03e42951802d7e476f9b3a83ee657e4c3d86a9535895`. Container start times were `2026-07-29T15:58:51Z`, `15:59:19Z`, `16:00:05Z`, and `16:00:59Z` respectively. The staged workflow verified every node selected the digest and remained running; JP-N2, EV-JP, and EV-JP2 were healthy, while JP-M has no container healthcheck. It also verified the release through `tokeness.io`, `n.tokeness.io`, and `n.tokeness.cn`. Public HTTP acceptance confirmed the Tokeness title, description, keywords, Open Graph and Twitter metadata, preserved New API generator attribution, a 200 passkey-begin response, expected unauthenticated 401s on both API domains, and five consecutive status responses on the new version. Real-browser 390 px wallet screenshot acceptance remains pending because the browser-control runtime was unavailable during this release.
- On 2026-07-30, official `QuantumNous/new-api:main@66ee6b8f9` was merged into `tokeness/main` through PR #14 with merge commit `9bf36f54d`. The sync preserved Tokeness frontend, passthrough/cache, billing, and deployment invariants while adding upstream Qwen `thinking_budget` zero-value forwarding, configurable OIDC display names, RelayKit documentation, and root/RelayKit `go vet` CI coverage. Local root and RelayKit build/vet/test, frontend checks, and deployment transaction tests passed; GitHub CI passed all three required checks. No image was published and production was not deployed.
- Waffo Pancake's production `MerchantProvidedBuyerIdentity` is the customer's email, not the synthetic `new-api-user-{userID}` value. Rejecting a signed `order.completed` webhook on that comparison and then returning HTTP 200 creates permanent pending orders. Settlement validation should use merchant external order ID, provider, amount, and currency, or compare against an identity snapshot persisted when checkout is created; retryable failures must not be acknowledged as successful delivery.
- EPay callback processing must be one idempotent database transaction covering row locking, provider and amount validation, top-up status, `CompleteTime`, and user quota. Return literal `success` only after commit; return a retryable failure otherwise. Administrator completion must reconcile the provider's paid order first and detect already-credited or mismatched orders, because delayed notifications and incorrect order IDs can otherwise produce duplicate credit.
- EPay administrator reconciliation rejects the configured public payment host after normalizing DNS case, trailing dots, and IP spelling. Literal private/loopback origins remain valid over HTTP or HTTPS for Docker-local production paths; every other HTTPS origin requires an exact `EPAY_RECONCILIATION_ALLOWED_HOSTS` entry. Do not add wildcard, suffix, URL, or port matching because the merchant key is sent in the query string.
- Payment webhooks should not share the general dashboard/API IP bucket. Apply a dedicated provider/path limiter and retain a pending-order reconciliation job as the recovery path for missing or exhausted callbacks.
- Payment callback routes are registered before `GlobalAPIRateLimit` and use independent `PW:<path>` source-IP buckets (300 requests per 60 seconds), so provider retries cannot consume or be rejected by the interactive API bucket. A master-node scheduled `payment_reconciliation_scan` records aggregate counts and oldest timestamps for external top-ups pending beyond 30 minutes every 15 minutes and emits a warning without changing order or wallet state. Automatic settlement remains intentionally blocked until each provider has a verified paid-state query and amount/currency validation path.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-payment-settlement.1` from merge `96eb0f1aa` runs on JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 at image digest `sha256:6bf88b04030cd1992f2cbace52d92114900cc69fe40a2ccb0f381714b8baa90d`. The staged rollout verified every node selected the digest and exposed the release version; JP-M passed its internal status probe without a container healthcheck and the replicas were healthy. Public acceptance confirmed dashboard/passkey availability, dedicated EPay callback reachability without shared-rate-limit 429s, Waffo Pancake signature rejection, and expected unauthenticated API responses through both CDN domains. The master reconciliation task completed immediately after startup and found 1,553 overdue pending rows without crediting them. EPay administrator recovery remains fail-closed because `EPAY_RECONCILIATION_QUERY_URL` is not yet configured on JP-M; do not point it at the public payment origin because the standard query carries the merchant key in the URL.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-payment-settlement.2` from merge `9b008809a` runs on all four nodes at image digest `sha256:6717e009e8e37be0ebaa5466e4c055f805a8ca8181a63032092a1f2723d8631b`. JP-M loads the EPay query URL for a pinned OpenResty sidecar at the static private address `172.18.0.250:18080`; the sidecar has no published ports, disables access logging, mounts the EPay root read-only, uses a read-only root filesystem, and drops all capabilities. Real provider queries for three recent pending EPay orders returned `not_paid` with matching merchant, trade, payment method, amount, and provider order ID checks, so the private reconciliation path is operational without changing balances.
- Administrator top-up completion is fail-closed and provider-verified. Waffo Pancake uses its signed merchant GraphQL query and requires one successful, completed, production, unrefunded payment in the configured store with matching external order ID, the order snapshot currency (CNY for new wallet orders; legacy empty snapshots fall back to USD), and subtotal. EPay requires an explicitly configured `EPAY_RECONCILIATION_QUERY_URL` on a dedicated internal `/api.php` origin with query-string access logging disabled. Hostname-based and public-address origins require HTTPS; plain HTTP is allowed only for a literal RFC1918 or loopback IP so same-host Docker traffic does not cross the public CDN. The public payment origin remains rejected. Other providers cannot use generic manual wallet credit until they have an equivalent verified query path.
- On 2026-07-30, native invite first-top-up rewards were enabled after a four-node audit found no active legacy `tokeness-ops` cron/systemd task and no legacy reward state files on JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, or EV-JP2. All nodes now run release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-invite-rewards.1` from `cdb5457854a9d304366124d395491516ca7b1ed4` at GHCR digest `sha256:6e4dc0598ebdbca45382b81aa05d4a9d6cf56af6550e6330a6bba14c7b2ab14f`; deployment workflow `30526879763` recreated and health-checked every New API container. The shared cutover is Unix timestamp `1785402106` (`2026-07-30T09:01:46Z`), with `INVITE_FIRST_TOPUP_REWARD_ENABLED=true` and the same `INVITE_FIRST_TOPUP_REWARD_START_TIMESTAMP` on all nodes. Starts were JP-N2 `08:57:40Z`, EV-JP `08:58:03Z`, JP-M `09:00:07Z`, and EV-JP2 `09:00:34Z`; replicas report healthy and JP-M passes its internal `/api/status` probe without a Docker healthcheck. Public `/api/status` returned 200, the unauthenticated reward endpoint returned the expected 401, and both CDN `/v1/models` probes returned the expected 401.
- On 2026-08-05, a JP-M reconciliation found the deprecated `/home/tokeness/tokeness-ops` reward path had 122 applied legacy state entries (51,550,000 quota), while the native `invite_top_up_rewards` ledger had 20 applied entries (8,200,000 quota); all 20 top-up IDs overlapped with identical reward amounts, confirming double-credit exposure. The legacy `tokeness-ops.timer` was first disabled, then the old directory, `invite-risk` symlink, four legacy systemd unit files, the 1Panel `tokeness-risk:invite` cronjob record, its task logs/file history, and matching 1Panel audit records were removed. Verification found no legacy files, processes, scheduled tasks, database records, or WAL strings on JP-M; the native New API reward path remains active.

- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-upstream.20260731.1` from `73a1a1c7c` runs on JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 at image digest `sha256:46186e949e1e793e1f16c8350b106ce8db5bad54b96eebfd2df6bcbb46eacf76`. Do not use the API-only domains' root or `/api/status` as health probes; both returned 520 while the actual `/v1` route remained healthy.
- On 2026-07-31, per-user model rate limits were implemented in `user_model_rate_limits`, and on 2026-08-01 production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-model-rate-limit.1` from merge `2a1b6b74d` (PR #30) deployed them at GHCR digest `sha256:9a2c6c0cd72e7ded81c59c17c21b55d2f01b2ab9e61be43b1c4657b5f9bd6a5c`. Administrators can replace multiple exact-model rules per user, each with an independent positive-second window and maximum request count; the relay middleware runs after `Distribute()` so channel retries consume one entry request only. Shared Redis stores both the atomic fixed-window counters and the short-lived rule cache; Redis failures fail closed, while no-Redis development mode is process-local. Publish workflow `30702773870` and staged deployment workflow `30702991506` passed. Container starts were JP-N2 `14:06:04Z`, EV-JP `14:06:35Z`, JP-M `14:07:18Z`, and EV-JP2 `14:07:45Z`; JP-N2, EV-JP, and EV-JP2 were healthy and JP-M passed its internal status probe without a Docker healthcheck. `tokeness.io/` and `/api/status` returned 200 with the release version; `n.tokeness.io`, `n.tokeness.cn`, and `n-cf.tokeness.io` `/v1/models` returned the expected unauthenticated 401 with `X-New-Api-Version` set to the release. The admin route `GET /api/user/:id/model-rate-limits` is mounted and rejects non-admin tokens; editing rules through an authenticated production admin session was not run during this release.

- On 2026-08-01, global per-user group+model rate limits were implemented in `group_model_rate_limits` and deployed as production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-global-rate-limit.1` from merge `41137b8b` (PR #32) at GHCR digest `sha256:2d38bf00f0e554b0bc9c24c52cc198b983c1351658edd779ef0812ab0cba9fe5`. A rule is keyed by `(group_name, model_name, window_seconds)` with a unique index and the whole rule set is replaced atomically; the relay middleware resolves the effective group (auto-routed group first, then the using group) and original model, then enforces Redis fixed-window counters per `(group, model, user, window)` independently of the per-user rules (either limit rejects). Rule data is cached in Redis for 30s with a process-local fallback; Redis failures fail closed. The admin UI lives in System Settings > Security > Rate Limiting as a dedicated editor section (GET/PUT `/api/group-model-rate-limits`, AdminAuth), separate from the legacy `ModelRequestRateLimitGroup` option JSON. Publish workflow `30704271205` and staged deployment workflow `30704520589` passed. Container starts were JP-N2 `14:51:45Z`, EV-JP `14:51:57Z`, JP-M `14:52:07Z`, and EV-JP2 `14:52:05Z`; the workflow verified all nodes and CDN routes expose the release version. `tokeness.io/api/status` returned 200 with the release version, `/v1/chat/completions` with the shared test key returned 200, and unauthenticated `/v1/models` on the API-only domains returned the expected 401. The admin route `GET /api/group-model-rate-limits` is mounted on `tokeness.io` and rejects unauthenticated tokens with 401; authenticated admin editing was not run during this release.

- On 2026-08-01, per-user model rate-limit editing moved out of the user edit drawer into System Settings > Security > Rate Limiting (`UserModelRateLimitsAdminSection` searches users by username/display name and edits their rules in place), and the group+model rate limit editor was fully translated into zh, zh-TW, ja, fr, ru, and vi. Production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-rate-limit-admin.1` from merge `d37ff7b2` (PR #33) deployed the UI at GHCR digest `sha256:1d6ed81ec16d2cbfbbb2280ac4142f2aead3d3d409071e84a95e5640324e2f8e`. Publish workflow `30705843768` and staged deployment workflow `30706094101` passed. `tokeness.io/api/status` returned 200 with the release version, `/v1/chat/completions` with the shared test key returned 200, and unauthenticated `/v1/models` on the API-only domains returned the expected 401.

- The self-service invoice workflow (2026-08-03 production release `v1.0.0-rc.21-tokeness-invoice-immediate.1`) uses `invoice_order_claims` (unique `(order_type, order_id)`) as the database-level anti-double-attach guard; claims are inserted on create and deleted on reject/cancel inside the same transaction. Order locks are taken in sorted id order to avoid multi-order deadlocks. Amount arithmetic goes through `shopspring/decimal` (`model.MoneyFromFloat`/`InvoiceMinAmountFromFloat`); `InvoiceMinAmount` is re-checked inside the creation transaction and NaN/±Inf/negative are rejected at option-save and fail-closed on read. Invoice list endpoints return explicit PII-free DTOs (`InvoiceListItem`/`AdminInvoiceListItem`); full material is detail-only.
- The system has no tax-bureau/e-invoice integration. A real invoice PDF is uploaded by an administrator on `complete-issue` (multipart `file`, magic-byte validated, <=10 MiB), read directly with `Request.MultipartReader` into request memory, and sent as the issued-email attachment; this service never saves the PDF, its filename, or its size, including as multipart temporary files. SMTP delivery occurs while the invoice row is locked, so concurrent completion requests cannot duplicate the attachment; an SMTP error leaves the application in `issuing` and requires a fresh upload. Only final statuses (`issued`/`rejected`/`cancelled`) send email, localized via backend i18n keys; intermediate states never mail. Email/audit logs omit recipient addresses; audit notes are truncated and email-scrubbed.
- Invoice transitions return `(changed bool, error)`; an idempotent repeat of an already-applied transition reports no change so the controller skips the second audit entry and duplicate final-status email. State-machine errors are localized (`ErrInvoiceInvalidTransition`/`ErrInvoiceNotIssuing`), and final-status emails render in the recipient user's saved language (`model.GetUserLanguage`) rather than the operator's request language. Admin `note` and user `remark` are length-checked (512 bytes) before reaching the database.
- Subscription payments now persist `PaymentCurrency` on `SubscriptionOrder` (from `plan.Currency`) and `upsertSubscriptionTopUpTx` copies `PaymentProvider`+`PaymentCurrency` onto the `TopUp` snapshot; orders with an empty currency are non-invoiceable (never faked as CNY). Backend invoice errors use `i18n.*` keys (en/zh-CN/zh-TW backend locales); the misplaced French value in frontend `ja.json` for "Failed to submit invoice application" was fixed. Frontend admin `complete-issue` dialog requires a real-PDF upload for first issuance and is idempotent on repeat.

- The immediate-delivery invoice release was built from commit `d66547700` by publish workflow `30758161157` and deployed by staged workflow `30758389567` at GHCR digest `sha256:6fc8d83e2fe298e7d4a582f89ccb80887e3f22aa7a4823fc54a959efca77e663`. JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 all reported the selected runtime digest and healthy state; `tokeness.io/api/status` returned 200 and `n.tokeness.io`, `n.tokeness.cn`, and `n-cf.tokeness.io` `/v1/models` returned the expected unauthenticated 401, all exposing the release version. Earlier immutable tags `invoice.1` and `invoice.2` existed and were rejected before any image build or deployment.
- Email binding is a global authenticated-app requirement, not an invoice-only rule. `AuthenticatedLayout` mounts `EmailBindingGate`, which waits for completed authentication bootstrap and blocks every signed-in account whose auth-bundle email is blank. Its required `EmailBindDialog` has no close icon or Cancel action; successful binding updates `auth-store` immediately. When Turnstile is enabled, the dialog mounts the verification widget, requires a token before sending the email code, passes it as `turnstile`, and resets it after a successful send or expiry. The invoice apply page only reads that account email and owns no separate binding state or profile lookup.

- The `v1.0.0-rc.23` upstream sync candidate adds API-key-specific Auto group ordering, Bedrock cancellation, and tiered retry billing changes while preserving Tokeness controls. Token Auto snapshots are intersected with the administrator's current global Auto allowlist both when saved and when routed, so globally withdrawn groups stop routing immediately. Group/model rate limits count each concrete group once per request and charge a new bucket only when a retry crosses groups; local task 429 responses are neither retried nor rewritten as upstream saturation. Wallet pre-consume and retry reservations use an atomic `quota >= amount` database update that bypasses process-local batching, preventing concurrent reservations from overdrawing a shared wallet. Auto-group UI descriptions continue to resolve `<tnt>` only at display boundaries, failed configuration/detail loads block initialization and saving, and API-key rows show Cross-group only when enabled.

- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.23-tokeness-geo-compliance.1` from merge `dfe3808b6653505495af924530ff68aedbf792fa` was published by workflow `31164694854` at GHCR digest `sha256:24c4672ab9c88d8f45959be384220970aa9d00eb546f441e42807fdfb87257a5`. Conflict resolution retained Tokeness invite-registration attribution and protocol-specific passthrough protection while adopting rc.24 replayable request bodies and transport retry metadata. Root Go tests for `common`, `relay/...`, `controller`, `middleware`, `model`, and `router`, RelayKit build, Go vet, frontend i18n sync, targeted frontend lint, typecheck, and production build passed. The initial PR frontend CI failure was caused by the new redemption-form test fixture updating only system currency settings while Tokeness renders browser-local USD/CNY preference; its fixture now sets both sources and its 6 cases pass. Docker image build was not run because Docker CLI is unavailable. The candidate has not been published or deployed.
- The site Notice automatically opens as a dialog only on `/pricing`: the first encounter with a Notice revision opens it, the same revision does not reopen after dismissal, and changed Notice content opens again. The home page never auto-opens it. Pricing checks the public Notice endpoint on mount and every five minutes; manual notification-center behavior remains unchanged.
- On 2026-08-13, the automatic notification surface is route-specific: `/` remains silent, `/pricing` uses only the site Notice dialog, and all other header routes automatically open the notification popover for an unread Notice revision. Automatic popover display marks that revision read, so closing it stays closed across unchanged refreshes and ordinary renders; a changed revision opens it again. The notification panel is intentionally not auto-opened when no unread notification content exists.
- Invoice payment-method restrictions use the `InvoiceAllowedPaymentMethods` JSON option. The normalized lower-case, de-duplicated list is matched against the persisted `TopUp.PaymentMethod` snapshot (not `PaymentProvider`); an empty/missing list preserves the existing all-methods behavior, while a configured list filters invoice options and is rechecked inside the locked invoice creation transaction. The Billing > Invoice Settings UI uses a multi-select and keeps configured/custom identifiers visible; corrupted stored values block invoice requests until corrected rather than silently changing policy.
- On 2026-08-15, a ZCode `gpt-5.6-sol` failure showed that an upstream overload can arrive as the first OpenAI Responses SSE event while the HTTP status is still 200. `OaiResponsesStreamHandler` now commits `503 Service Unavailable` before writing a first `response.failed`/`response.error` event (when no response bytes were committed), allowing clients to retry or fail over; a failure after normal output remains a committed 200 stream. Regression tests cover both cases.
- On 2026-08-15, the Tokeness fork added the signed HotPay settlement receiver at `POST /internal/v1/payment/settlements`. It validates `HOTPAY_SETTLEMENT_SECRET` HMAC signatures, command/idempotency headers, and a configurable `HOTPAY_SETTLEMENT_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` replay window, then commits wallet top-ups or subscription entitlements through the durable `PaymentGatewaySettlement` record before returning 2xx. `command_id` and `(business_type, merchant_order_id)` are unique idempotency keys; replayed committed commands return `duplicate=true`. Terminal local-order mismatches are not wrapped as retryable, `order_id` is required, and wallet quota settlement rejects overflow beyond `common.MaxQuota`. No secret values are stored in this memory.
- On 2026-08-15, New API gained an environment-configured HotPay checkout client. When `HOTPAY_GATEWAY_URL` is set, wallet EPay/Waffo Pancake and subscription EPay/Waffo Pancake facades create the local pending ledger order first, delegate provider checkout to `POST /v1/payment/orders` with `Idempotency-Key`, and return the hosted checkout URL while preserving legacy response shapes. Canonical Waffo methods are normalized (`CNY` → `wechat_pay`; `USD` → selected card/Apple Pay/Google Pay/WeChat), and legacy direct checkout remains available only when the gateway URL is unset. The old Pancake webhook is closed after cutover unless `HOTPAY_GATEWAY_ALLOW_LEGACY_WEBHOOKS=true`; gateway-generated EPay-compatible notifications acknowledge already-settled Waffo orders without re-crediting them. Configuration is documented in `.env.example`; no secret values are stored in this memory.
- On 2026-08-15, HotPay integration verification passed for `go test ./model ./controller ./service`, `go vet ./...`, and `go build ./...`. Full `go test ./...` runs reached all packages but remain environment-flaky in the existing Windows HTTP/2 relay tests `TestUpstreamGetBody_HTTP2RetryAfterGracefulGoAway_PassThrough` and `TestUpstreamGetBody_HTTP2CannotRetryWithoutGetBody`, which received local TCP `wsarecv` connection-aborted errors; each test passes when run in isolation, and no HotPay integration test failed. Do not treat this host-level transport flake as settlement or checkout evidence.
- On 2026-08-15, the HotPay settlement receiver was placed behind the dedicated `PW:hotpay_settlement` source-IP limiter instead of the interactive API bucket. Its replay-window override is bounded to 24 hours; invalid or larger values retain the five-minute default. Concurrent unique-key conflicts now re-check both `command_id` and the business-order settlement key before classifying an idempotent duplicate or a terminal conflict. Model coverage sends 100 concurrent copies of one command and proves one wallet credit and one durable settlement; failed settlement validation also proves the provisional settlement record is rolled back.
- On 2026-08-15, updated gateway architecture alignment added fail-closed New API checkout snapshot handling: gateway-enabled facades persist the provider account and test/prod environment locally, explicitly send both to HotPay, and reject a checkout response whose immutable account or environment differs. The EPay-compatible wallet facade now includes its environment. The signed settlement receiver validates the same snapshot before its atomic quota or entitlement update, while legacy Waffo callbacks remain drain-only. Target payment tests, `go vet ./...`, and `go build ./...` pass; the previously flaky Windows HTTP/2 retries also passed in isolation.
- On 2026-08-15, gateway-enabled wallet facades fail closed when the quota snapshot would exceed `common.MaxQuota`; the conversion returns an error instead of zero. Reused idempotency keys now compare local amount, payment method, provider account, and environment snapshots for wallet and subscription orders before delegating checkout.
- On 2026-08-15, HotPay checkout responses are now strict about immutable business/user/provider/currency/method/amount/status/attempt fields and the provider method whitelist. Gateway-enabled facades apply optional `HOTPAY_GATEWAY_MIN_AMOUNT_MINOR`/`HOTPAY_GATEWAY_MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR` bounds before local pending-order insertion. Pending reconciliation scans wallet and subscription orders when the subscription table is present. The settlement record persists provider account, environment, method, amount/currency, provider order/transaction IDs and rejects a provider-event replay under a different command. Legacy Waffo drain now snapshots the provider account and durably claims `(provider, environment, event_id)` without retaining raw payloads.
- On 2026-08-16, HotPay-enabled EPay callback aliases can verify gateway-generated notifications with dedicated `HOTPAY_EPAY_PID`/`HOTPAY_EPAY_KEY` runtime values after legacy direct EPay settings are removed. The New API top-up read model also keeps the online compatibility entry visible when only the HotPay gateway is configured; these values are verification-only and never create provider checkouts.
- On 2026-08-15, the signed settlement contract was tightened: New API rejects commands missing the provider account, `test`/`prod` environment, provider order ID, or provider transaction ID before opening the ledger transaction. HotPay now signs those two provider identifiers in the same field order as the receiver payload. The checkout client canonicalizes SDK method aliases such as `wechat`/`wechat_pay`, so Waffo's returned whitelist is accepted without weakening the method matrix.
- On 2026-08-15, the Tokeness frontend console was visually reworked around a paper/ink/signal-red design system: sharp rules, compact data density, monospace metadata, and no blue-purple marketing gradients or floating-card shell. The app shell, dashboard panels, public header, Model Square/Pricing catalog, model detail surfaces, and auth layout use the new tokens while preserving New API/QuantumNous attribution, routes, permissions, i18n, theme persistence, and API contracts. Desktop and 390px browser checks passed in light and dark themes; frontend typecheck, focused oxlint, focused pricing tests, and production build passed. Keep the PRD and Tech Spec in `docs/TOKENESS_CONSOLE_UI_REDESIGN_PRD.md` and `docs/TOKENESS_CONSOLE_UI_REDESIGN_TECH_SPEC.md` as the design source of truth.
- On 2026-08-15, the Tokeness brand mark from the approved asset bundle replaced the legacy gradient fallback. `web/public/logo.png` and `web/public/favicon.ico` are the canonical default runtime assets; `/logo.png` remains the persisted-config-compatible fallback path, while system-configured logos still override it at runtime.
- On 2026-08-16, the console UI redesign continued with a second pass. `/dashboard/overview` was rebuilt as flat hairline sections: a four-cell usage strip (balance with health dot and runway, 24h usage with a minimal sparkline that hides when all-zero, historical usage, request count) replaced the floating stat cards; the setup guide became a numbered flat step list plus a plain selectable curl block, with decorative window chrome, shimmer animation, icon badges, and nested cards removed. The guide now auto-collapses and persists `collapsed` the moment the final step completes while the page is open (a completion state present on first load still respects the saved visibility, key `dashboard_overview_setup_guide_expanded` unchanged). Uptime status moved from panel-local fetching to a parent `useQuery` (`['dashboard','overview','uptime-status']`), and the Uptime card renders nothing when Uptime Kuma returns no monitor groups, so the rail no longer shows an empty placeholder.
