Header fixes: `SystemBrand` lost its red left rule, public and console top-nav active tabs use a bottom primary underline with `startsWith` path matching instead of a left red border, and the sidebar drill-in back link lost its permanent red indicator (vertical sidebar active markers remain). `/pricing` was rebuilt as a pricing sheet: segmented controls use ink active fills instead of red, the filter sidebar is a flat row list with count metadata (section title now uses the existing `Provider` key), model cards are dense hairline grid cells (bare icon, mono name, inline input/output/cache prices, single muted meta line, whole-card click opens the existing details drawer, copy button appears on hover), and pagination lives inside the grid container. Local acceptance seeded the disposable SQLite dev DB with one channel and 18 ability rows; that data is local-only. Verification: typecheck, focused oxlint (0 errors on changed files), 7 pricing/overview tests, production build, and browser checks at 1440x900 and 390x844 in light and dark (no horizontal overflow, drawer opens from card click, table view intact) all passed.
- On 2026-08-16, the HotPay settlement receiver now treats temporarily missing wallet users or gateway orders as retryable 503 outcomes, preserving provider retries and avoiding a false terminal mismatch. Terminal status classification remains reserved for invalid/conflicting snapshots and invalid local order states; no ledger mutation or durable settlement record survives a retryable failure.
- On 2026-08-16, the Model Square gained release-date ordering and discount display. The Go `Pricing` struct now carries `created_time` (omitempty, mirrored from the `models` catalog metadata row; zero when no metadata exists) purely as an additive response field. The pricing page default sort is `newest` (created_time desc, metadata-less models last, name tiebreak; i18n key `Newest first` added to all 7 locales). When the displayed group ratio differs from 1, model cards strike through the undiscounted price beside the discounted one via `formatPriceParts`/`formatRequestPriceParts` in `lib/price.ts` (dynamic-pricing expressions are unchanged), and the per-unit token suffix moved from each price row into the card meta line to keep the two-column price grid from crowding. Group ratios render as localized discounts via `formatGroupDiscount` in `lib/model-helpers.ts` (zh: 2折, other locales: 80% off, ratio 1 hides the badge, premium ratios keep xN) in the filter sidebar, the details drawer group table (ratio 1 shows —), and the dynamic-pricing group header. Note the local acceptance DB seeds one channel, 18 abilities, and 12 `models` metadata rows with vendor IDs and varying created_time; the default group ratio there is 0.2, which is why every card shows strikethrough pricing locally.
- On 2026-08-16, the sitewide UI audit's P1-P3 findings were all fixed. Rankings lost its decorative radial-gradient hero (`features/rankings/index.tsx`), and the playground composer is now a square hairline box without shadow or backdrop blur (`playground-input.tsx`). Identity-colored badges were neutralized: `GroupBadge` is neutral everywhere, the models metadata table renders vendor/tags/endpoints/bound-channels/matched-models without `autoColor`, `ModelBillingModeBadge` and the profile `User ID` chip are neutral, and the sidebar accent tokens (`--sidebar-accent`) are neutral gray in both `theme.css` and the `sunset-glow` preset block (the preset block overrides `:root`, so both had to change; the red left border remains the active signal). Profile 2FA card dropped its duplicated description and its Enable CTA is outline while Passkey keeps primary. The overview performance-health panel uses flat divide-x metric cells like the usage strip. Wallet merges the topup-disabled and redemption-disabled alerts into one when both apply. The users invite column no longer truncates the Revenue badge (`[&>*]:shrink-0`, size 260). The 404 page renders inside `PublicLayout` so it keeps the header. `auth-session.ts` now retries `/api/user/auth/refresh` 429s twice with Retry-After-aware backoff (capped at 30s, attempts bounded so a persistent 429 still resolves to transient_error without clearing the session); two runner tests cover recovery and exhaustion. Verification: typecheck, focused oxlint (0 new errors; rankings has 1 pre-existing nested-ternary + 1 prefer-set-has at HEAD), 58 dashboard/pricing/lib tests, 17 wallet tests, production build, and browser re-checks of rankings/playground/models/profile/overview/settings/wallet/users/404 all passed. Note: the local dev backend was restarted with `CRITICAL_RATE_LIMIT_ENABLE=false` after repeated login-limit 429s during the audit; that flag is local-only.

- On 2026-08-16, the Playground was overhauled with multi-session persistence and image generation mode. `DEFAULT_PARAMETER_ENABLED` is all false; the search/globe icon and sidebar "聊天" sub-item were removed; the page was renamed to "模型对话". Browser localStorage stores `playground_sessions` (zod-validated, migrated from legacy single-messages key) with `playground_active_session`; the left panel (w-60 desktop, Sheet mobile) creates/switches/deletes sessions with `deriveSessionTitle` (24 chars + …). Image attachments go through `fileToImageDataUrl` (canvas downscale to 1024px, JPEG 0.85, white background fill) and persist as data URLs; user messages send the OpenAI vision `content` parts format when images are present. Image generation mode uses a "聊天/图片" segmented toggle that hides the parameter panel; the backend gains `controller.PlaygroundImages` (sharing `playgroundRelay` setup with `Playground`, calling `Relay(c, types.RelayFormatOpenAIImage)`), and `Path2RelayMode` maps `/pg/images/generations` → `RelayModeImagesGenerations` and `/pg/images/edits` → `RelayModeImagesEdits`. The frontend `use-imageGeneration` hook mirrors chat error handling: a generation counter guards stale updates, `b64_json` results are converted to `data:image/png;base64,` URLs on the assistant message's `images` array, and `shouldShowMessageContent` was updated to also check `images.length` so actions render for image-only assistant messages. Profile "记录 IP 地址" was removed; `model/log.go` now unconditionally sets `needRecordIp := true`. Verification: typecheck, focused oxlint (0 errors), 289 tests pass, production build, and browser acceptance (session create/switch/delete, mode toggle hides parameter panel, mobile Sheet) all passed. Five new i18n keys were added across 7 locales; `playground-session-list.tsx` uses `toIntlLocale(i18n.resolvedLanguage)` to avoid the `RangeError: Invalid language tag: zhCN` crash on `toLocaleTimeString`.

- On 2026-08-16, three Playground image-mode bugs were fixed after code review. `middleware/distributor.go` did not handle `/pg/images/generations` and `/pg/images/edits` for model extraction or group selection: the model-name branch only matched `/v1/images/*`, and the playground group-extraction branch only matched `/pg/chat/completions`. Both paths now share the same extraction logic as their `/v1/` counterparts, with multipart group parsed from `form.Value["group"]` and JSON group from `PlayGroundRequest`. `shouldShowMessageContent` in `message-content-utils.ts` now also checks `message.images?.length`, so image-only assistant messages render their action buttons (copy, delete, regenerate) instead of being silently invisible. Verification: backend build, `go vet ./middleware/`, `go test ./middleware/`, frontend typecheck (0 errors), oxlint (0 errors), 289 tests, production build, and live backend smoke test (503 = no image channel, confirming the route passes auth + model resolution instead of the previous 400 "model name is required"; 403 on unknown group confirms multipart group extraction).
- On 2026-08-16, `common.MessageWithRequestId` removes trailing `(request id: ...)` suffixes before adding the current node's ID, preventing nested upstream New API request IDs in relayed error messages. Regression coverage is in `common/utils_test.go`.
- On 2026-08-16, relay client errors now apply an administrator-editable `ErrorMessageFilterEnabled` switch and Go RE2 `ErrorMessageFilterPattern` immediately before the response request ID is added. The default removes provider inventories and `(Console Go)` while logs retain the original error; invalid patterns are rejected by the option API. The editor is under Operations > Error message privacy.
- On 2026-08-17, non-admin usage-log responses now apply the same error-message filter to `LogTypeError.Content` in `formatUserLogs`, covering `/api/log/self` and token-scoped log views. Admin `/api/log` responses retain the original error content for investigation; the filter remains controlled by the operation setting.
- On 2026-08-17, the Model Square model-details Overview pricing section renders group pricing (`GroupPricingSection`) before base price (`PriceSection`) and the dynamic-pricing breakdown. Model cards' perf badge now shows average TTFT instead of total latency: `QuerySummaryAll` aggregates `SUM(ttft_sum_ms)/SUM(ttft_count)` into `avg_ttft_ms` (DB summary queries plus the in-memory hot bucket both carry the counters), and `ModelPerfBadge` renders the `TTFT short` label (zh/zh-TW: 首字). TTFT samples are only recorded for streaming requests (`info.IsStream && info.HasSendResponse()` in `pkg/perf_metrics/metrics.go`), so models used mostly non-streaming show `—` on the card; the details Performance tab still shows both TTFT and total latency.
- On 2026-08-17, OpenAI non-stream responses now strip `usage.billing_usage` from both raw and force-formatted client responses while retaining the internal usage object for settlement. Regression tests cover normal forwarding, force formatting, and preservation of unrelated upstream usage extensions.
- On 2026-08-17, a production payment regression was traced to the legacy Waffo Pancake callback comparing the signed event `StoreID` with `MerchantID`. Legacy wallet and subscription orders now snapshot `WaffoPancakeStoreID`, callbacks fail closed on a missing or wrong store, and a narrowly scoped compatibility check accepts only the known historical MerchantID snapshot so affected paid pending orders can be retried. EPay callbacks were verified settling successfully in production; no automatic bulk credit is allowed without provider-paid evidence.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.24-tokeness-payment-recovery.1` from `b667ba2cb317964a1083d8d75bae11ab07b5c076` runs on all four nodes at digest `sha256:a42155e7187edddec0281c0c15a39f4f4c1480bddf233d399e9a69010f3fcc58`. Publish workflow `31984175594`, fleet verification `31984525662`, and production rollout `31984670009` passed. JP-M now runs `new-api-green` with the native Compose `new-api` alias; the temporary callback network was removed and the EPay PHP container still resolves and reaches `/api/status`. The recent-eight-hour EPay paid cohort reconciled at 12/12 local successes, zero pending, zero invalid success rows, and the public status route returned 120/120 HTTP 200 responses. No Waffo historical pending order was bulk-credited: provider-paid evidence was not established for the sampled cohort, so future recovery must use a signed provider retry or the existing verified admin completion path.

- On 2026-08-17, the console UX reliability pass added an additive `/api/observability/channel-availability` contract. It accepts up to 200 channel IDs and returns one server-side bucket series per channel, including exact request success/failure counts and weighted request latency; the channel list therefore avoids issuing one aggregate request per time bucket. Availability is rendered in the default card view, tag rows request and aggregate their child channel IDs, and channel-model detail consumers follow every response page before calculating totals. The paginated channel-model response also exposes exact request success/failure counters, while the frontend keeps percentage fallback for older backends. Multi-key test results include a persisted HTTP status, upstream error code/class, and a whitespace-normalized, length-bounded, sensitive-info-masked error message keyed by stable credential ID; raw credentials and proxy secrets are never returned. Announcement revisions hash content as well as IDs, a persisted pending revision keeps the unified notice/timeline dialog open across pricing header remounts, timeline changes are polled every five minutes while a header notification entry is active, and the public mobile header exposes the same announcement bell. Playground session mode/model/parameters remain per-session, stream state remains session-isolated, and the reorder handle supports keyboard arrow movement in addition to native drag. No production deployment was performed for this pass.
- On 2026-08-17, the console UX reliability final gate closed the remaining compatibility and interaction gaps. Channel availability clients batch lists larger than 200 IDs and merge successful responses; multi-key diagnostics use a MySQL 5.7-compatible `VARCHAR(2048)` field and never fall back from a stable credential ID to a positional result. Playground stream construction failures now clear per-session request state, and pointer plus keyboard ordering share explicit before/after placement so downward and end-of-list moves work. Tiered-expression original prices render in both model card and table views. Verification passed 331 frontend tests, frontend typecheck, changed-file lint/format, i18n sync, production build, focused and broad backend tests except for the known Windows-only HTTP/2 graceful-GOAWAY transport test; the independent relaykit suite, Go vet, and frontend distribution retention test passed. No image was published and no production deployment was performed.
- On 2026-08-17, legacy direct Waffo Pancake CNY wallet checkout failed because the bound pre-migration product supported only USD; changing the checkout currency alone does not add a provider price. The production product was migrated to CNY+USD and a CNY checkout canary succeeded. Future wallet bindings must verify an active production-enabled store, product ownership and active status, a published production version, and CNY+USD prices in that production version before persisting. The wallet now exposes five processing identifiers: bare `waffo_pancake` is unrestricted USD, while `:wechat`, `:card`, `:applepay`, and `:googlepay` select one provider method. Blank private-key saves may reuse the persisted key only for the unchanged merchant. No provider credentials or identifiers are stored here.
- On 2026-08-17, HotPay USD wallet orders may persist `payment_method=auto` only for a hosted Pancake method selector. The signed settlement command must carry the concrete provider method (`card`, `apple_pay`, `google_pay`, or `wechat_pay`), which the receiver matches against the local auto order and then credits atomically; an `auto` settlement method remains invalid. Regression coverage protects this boundary.
- On 2026-08-17, New API computes both sides of the optional CNY-WeChat fallback before calling HotPay. Only that route carries a typed USD fallback. When HotPay returns a final USD order, New API atomically validates and synchronizes the canonical order ID, provider/account/environment, amount, currency, payment method, quota, and price snapshot into the pending TopUp before exposing the checkout URL. Bare `waffo_pancake` remains USD `auto` and sends no payment method.
- On 2026-08-17, `BindPaymentGatewayWalletOrder` was tightened so a successful TopUp cannot be rewritten by a later checkout snapshot: only an identical gateway order/final money-currency-method snapshot is an idempotent no-op; fallback synchronization is allowed only while the local order is pending. Regression coverage verifies a successful CNY order rejects a USD fallback mutation without changing its ledger fields.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.24-tokeness-pancake-routing.1` from `04ac5e45405d25422f2594c512d0ae45b7356ef4` runs on JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 at digest `sha256:3a77beb6cb791e9c14f1b3d986c41607cdd1e34cecb582727c0b34415f287b5b`. Publish workflow `32013235028`, pre-deploy verify `32014321439`, rollout `32014587433`, and post-deploy verify `32015131204` passed. Public dashboard/status routes return 200 with the new version marker, and both API domains return the expected unauthenticated 401. This rollout did not deploy, configure, or cut traffic over to HotPay; the existing payment providers remain in production use.
- On 2026-08-17, the console UX follow-up added millisecond-resolution p95 for newly recorded channel observations through a bounded weighted quantile sketch stored inside the existing histogram JSON. Merge weights preserve population size across buckets and nodes; legacy database/Redis fixed buckets become weighted representatives during merges so they no longer discard the precise portion, although historical samples remain intrinsically bucket-derived. Channel detail rows aggregate by requested model, the dialog uses a wide fixed layout without horizontal scrolling, and availability tooltips show first-token latency with semantic theme colors. Public and authenticated desktop navigation is viewport-centered, the public header falls back to its mobile menu below `lg`, and the global command search is an icon button with its shortcut retained. `/keys` compact API information shows localized node descriptions plus URL copy/probe actions; generated Playground images have per-image downloads; discount labels keep up to two decimals. Verification passed `go test ./...`, 343 frontend tests, typecheck, changed-file lint/format, i18n sync, production build, and public-page browser checks at 1440x900, 768x800, and 390x844. No production deployment was performed.
- Production release `v1.0.0-rc.24-tokeness-console-ux.1` from merge `b0cc7db45fbca3e93242e39ce3ebb0a842c4ff91` runs on JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 at GHCR digest `sha256:ee8b6ff17f34f7d15ae7ab7ef976b25229f987b50da4fc27ee4c51f3e2569b81`. Publish workflow `32076344610`, fleet verification `32077393434`, and staged production rollout `32077593469` passed. Post-deploy checks confirmed `tokeness.io` and `/api/status` return 200, while `n.tokeness.io` and `n.tokeness.cn` return the expected unauthenticated 401; all nodes and CDN routes expose the release version. The rollout emitted an existing OpenResty duplicate MIME warning on `aib-na.swhub.cn`; nginx syntax and reload checks passed and the warning is unrelated to this release.

- On 2026-08-18, `/pricing` card-view pagination gained the same local page-size selector as the table view, with 10/20/30/40/50/100 options and a reset to page 1 when the size changes. The selector is covered by a focused interaction test; no API or URL contract changed.

- On 2026-08-18, the navigation announcement dialog now separates the `/system-settings/site/notice` content and `/system-settings/content/announcements` timeline into accessible Notice/Timeline tabs, preserves unread timeline highlighting, and restores the native Close Today/Close footer. Close Today persists a same-day auto-open suppression while manual bell access remains available; the pricing site-notice dialog remains unchanged.
- On 2026-08-18, the public and console navigation search trigger is a borderless ghost icon button, and the navigation theme entry is a single icon button that cycles Light, Dark, and System without opening the full Theme Settings drawer. Its icon and accessible label follow the selected mode, while the browser theme-color meta tag follows the resolved system theme.
- On 2026-08-18, multi-key channel management uses the same responsive `min(96vw, 1440px)` dialog width as channel observability, preventing its wide key table from being constrained by the shared desktop dialog maximum.
- On 2026-08-18, release review hardening completed the channel observability and multi-key credential work. Active Redis latency sketches now keep 10 ms precision through one minute, 100 ms through ten minutes, and 1 s afterwards instead of clipping every long request to 60000 ms. Codex OAuth JSON arrays preserve order across partial updates, model discovery, manual/automatic refresh, and per-key enabled state; refresh/model discovery resolve the selected credential's inherit/direct/custom proxy. Refresh persistence conditionally updates the old channel key plus affected credential secret/fingerprint rows in one transaction, so a concurrent administrator edit or stale credential row rolls the entire refresh back without changing status, proxy, or test fields. Dialog data requests use channel-scoped sequence guards to discard stale responses; strict proxy parsing now matches backend `?`/`#` rules; the compact API-node description tooltip is keyboard focusable. Verification passed focused backend packages, full frontend typecheck and 397 tests, production frontend build, Go vet, root and relaykit builds, changed-file lint/format, and deep dark-theme browser review. The all-package Go suite still has the established Windows-only `TestUpstreamGetBody_HTTP2RetryAfterGracefulGoAway_PassThrough` loopback transport failure; MySQL/PostgreSQL schema tests remain skipped without their test DSNs.
- On 2026-08-18, backend email and notification templates were fully i18n-ized. Verification/password-reset emails (`controller/misc.go`) now render from `email.verification.*` / `email.password_reset.*` keys; password-reset uses the user's saved language, while verification uses the current request language because the recipient may be unregistered. Quota warnings (`service/quota.go`) pick the recipient's language and per-channel bodies (`notify.quota_exceed.body` / `.body_bark` / `.body_gotify`), switching the legacy `{{value}}` sequential placeholder to `{{.field}}` template data; subscription quota warnings use a distinct localized subject. Channel disabled/enabled (`service/channel.go`), channel-test completion, and upstream model inspection notifications follow the same pattern. `dto.Notify` gained an optional `TemplateData` map (constructor `NewNotifyWithData`); all notify senders (`email`, `webhook`, `Bark`, `Gotify`) render through the shared `service.renderNotify`, which uses text/template for `TemplateData` and falls back to the legacy `{{value}}` replacement for backward compatibility. `NotifyUpstreamModelUpdateWatchersLocalized` supplies per-language content while the original `NotifyUpstreamModelUpdateWatchers(subject, content)` API remains available. Backend i18n supports `zh-CN`, `zh-TW`, and `en` (fallback `en`); user settings store the frontend codes `zhCN`/`zhTW`, so `normalizeLang` now maps `zhtw` before the generic `zh` prefix to fix `zhTW` users incorrectly falling back to `zh-CN` (this also affects invoice emails). The invoice status email now resolves language through `i18n.ResolveUserLang` instead of raw `model.GetUserLanguage`. All keys live in `i18n/locales/*.yaml`; missing keys fall back to the key name.
- On 2026-08-18, all outbound HTML emails gained a branded dark frame modeled on the Tencent Cloud verification email. `service/email_frame.go` `RenderBrandedEmail(lang, title, contentHTML)` renders a table-based inline-styled layout (email clients strip external CSS): dark header with `common.Logo` (only when it is an http(s) URL — data-URL logos are blocked by Gmail) plus `common.SystemName` above a `#1f6feb` accent line, a content card with an H1 title and "此致 / {SystemName} 团队" signature, and a footer with the system-notice line and a copyright that links `system_setting.ServerAddress`. Title/SystemName/footer strings are HTML-escaped; body content is trusted locale HTML. Newlines in content become `<br>` because all mail is `text/html` (this fixes the upstream-update digest, whose `\n-` lists previously collapsed). Frame chrome and the new per-type H1 titles come from `email.frame.*`, `email.verification.title`, `email.password_reset.title`, and `invoice.email.status_title` keys in all three locales. Verification codes now render in a large highlighted box and password reset has a CTA button plus a styled plain-link fallback; the invoice body dropped its own site-link paragraph because the frame footer carries it (the existing recipient-language test now asserts the site link through the fully framed email). Wiring: `sendEmailNotify` takes the recipient userId and resolves the frame language with `i18n.ResolveUserLang`; `misc.go` verification/reset and `invoice.go` status emails call the frame directly. `TestRenderBrandedEmailPreview` (env-gated on `EMAIL_PREVIEW_DIR`) writes sample framed emails per locale for browser review. Verified with `go build`, `go vet`, service+controller test suites, and browser screenshots of verification/password-reset/quota previews across zh-CN, zh-TW, and en. Production release status is recorded in a later entry.
- On 2026-08-18, backend i18n was expanded from three to the full frontend language set: fr, ru, ja, vi were added alongside en/zh-CN/zh-TW, each with complete 302-key translations (new `i18n/locales/{fr,ru,ja,vi}.yaml`). `i18n.normalizeLang` now maps fr/ru/ja/vi prefixes (user settings store the same codes as the browser BCP-47 tags for these four), and every new localizer is created with an explicit English fallback so a missing key degrades to English text rather than a raw key name. `i18n/locales_test.go` (TestLocaleKeyParity + TestNormalizeLang) enforces exact key-set equality across all seven locales and locks the language-code mapping — run it after adding keys or a new locale. Fr/ru/ja/vi users now get localized API errors and localized emails; `AGENTS.md` backend i18n note updated to list all seven languages.
- On 2026-08-18, outbound email templates were recolored to match the web brand instead of the earlier hardcoded GitHub-blue. The Tokeness design system defines a single signal red `#d7192a` (`--tokeness-red`, also the Home page `--tokeness-home-primary`, kept in both light and dark); the web Home dark palette is background `#0b0f14`, card `#14181d`, rule/border `#24303c`, foreground `#f5f7fb`, card-foreground `#eef1f5`, muted `#aab3bf`, with square corners (no border-radius). `service/email_frame.go` now builds the frame from these values, the header accent line / password-reset button / step accents use `#d7192a`, and small link text on the dark card uses a lighter red tint `#f05260` for contrast while keeping the hue. Template bodies across all seven locales dropped the blue button/link/code-box colors and removed `border-radius`. A frame test asserts the brand red is present and no `#1f6feb` remains.
- On 2026-08-18, HTML email dynamic values are context-escaped instead of being interpolated into trusted locale markup with `text/template`. Direct email bodies use `i18n.TranslateHTML`; notification templates keep `TemplateData` separate until `sendEmailNotify` renders them with `html/template`, so text, attributes, and URLs receive the correct escaping and unsafe URL schemes become inert. Safe localized bodies and `RenderBrandedEmail` use the explicit `template.HTML` type, and the frame only links an HTTP(S) `ServerAddress`. Legacy email values use separate template delimiters so literal `{{...}}` content remains unchanged. Webhook compatibility remains intentionally different: when `TemplateData` is nil, `SendWebhookNotify` preserves the historical per-value `fmt.Sprintf` behavior for `%s`/`%v` callers; structured notifications use the new template renderer. `dto.Notify.Values` remains present in JSON when nil to preserve the public serialization contract.
- On 2026-08-19, the localized email/notification hardening shipped to production through PR #50. Source commit `92f67482d87c26b0bdc9a582cb487fa75db65a2c` was merged as `29a8d69ba1452e7fc339424888e3fb2f0c3303c3`; production preflight run `32166008617` confirmed all four nodes on the prior `v1.0.0-rc.24-tokeness-github-email.1` digest. Publish run `32166351291` built `Dockerfile.tokeness`, passed Linux release regressions, published the frontend CDN distribution, and produced `v1.0.0-rc.24-tokeness-email-i18n.1` at immutable digest `sha256:30c3bd109cbccba1a2ad4264c5de61833362ff8a8de1b10cc90a8e9ed51975d3`. Staged production run `32167101961` deployed JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, then EV-JP2; every node reported the selected/runtime digest above, running state, application version, and remote command `2026-08-17.4` (JP-M has no Docker healthcheck and passed its local readiness/blue-green route verification). `https://tokeness.io/` and `/api/status` returned 200, while `https://n.tokeness.io/v1/models` and `https://n.tokeness.cn/v1/models` returned the expected 401; all exposed `X-New-Api-Version: v1.0.0-rc.24-tokeness-email-i18n.1`. JP-M OpenResty validation continues to emit a pre-existing duplicate `text/html` MIME-type warning for `/www/sites/aib-na.swhub.cn/proxy/back.conf:32`; syntax, reload, route, and release validation still pass.
- On 2026-08-19, request-level channel observability was hardened for failures that occur before channel initialization. Billing, request validation, or pricing can return before a relay handler calls `InitChannelMeta`; `RecordRequest` must therefore skip observations when the embedded `ChannelMeta` is nil, matching `RecordAttempt`. Without this guard, a legitimate 403 insufficient-wallet response triggered a deferred nil-pointer panic and Gin appended a `new_api_panic` JSON object to the already-written error body. Regression coverage verifies the uninitialized-channel path does not panic.
- On 2026-08-19, wallet pre-consume failures now re-read the authoritative database quota after the atomic debit condition fails and include both remaining and required quota in the 403 when available. This preserves the no-overdraft conditional update while making stale-cache/concurrent-reservation diagnosis actionable; a service regression test covers the response contract.
- On 2026-08-19, the wallet pre-consume initial balance check now reads the database directly instead of Redis. Billing reservations must not reject a request from a stale cache value after a recent top-up, refund, or cross-node update; the conditional database debit remains the final no-overdraft guard.
- On 2026-08-19, a review-only content moderation gate was added to the rc25 candidate. `controller.Relay` checks the latest text in OpenAI Chat/Responses, Anthropic Messages, and Gemini bodies before pricing, quota reservation, billing, and upstream forwarding; the gateway's distribution middleware has already prepared a candidate channel context, which the first relay attempt reuses. The gate skips the `/v1/moderations` endpoint itself, merges all text parts from the latest user turn, and uses an OpenAI-compatible `/v1/moderations` endpoint with bounded retries and key rotation. Configuration is stored under `content_moderation.config`, defaults to disabled/observe/fail-open, and stores only redacted excerpts plus hashes in the GORM `content_moderation_logs` table. Root-only configuration/log/unban APIs and a security-settings UI are included; image moderation and prompt-audit full-body persistence are intentionally out of scope. `EmailOnHit` sends through the existing branded email renderer to the user's notification email or account email. No production deployment or API credentials were performed.
- On 2026-08-20, content moderation follows channel affinity for conversation de-duplication. When the distributor exposes an affinity rule, identity, TTL, and selected channel, a successful decision is cached under a full SHA-256 key containing user/group/model/protocol/rule/channel/policy identity while excluding rotating multi-key credentials. Repeated requests in the same affinity conversation and channel reuse the decision; cached flagged results still block in `pre_block`, a different channel is audited independently, and failed API or required-log writes are not cached. The moderation extractor uses validated OpenAI Chat/Responses, Anthropic, and Gemini DTOs and adds no second `BodyStorage.Bytes()` read; a channel-affinity `gjson` source may still materialize the body once before moderation. Cache lookup precedes sampling, affinity sampling is stable, policy changes version the key, TTL is capped at 365 days, and Redis owner-token leases renew until cache publication while in-process `singleflight` covers non-Redis concurrency. Auto-ban uses a status-only auth-version-fenced transaction, publishes the disabled cache snapshot, revokes browser sessions, invalidates relay-token caches, and remains idempotent without writing stale unrelated user fields. Flagged email is claimed durably per log and sent asynchronously with a 15-second SMTP deadline: clear pre-delivery failures release for retry, while ambiguous post-delivery failures keep the claim to avoid duplicate mail. Fail-open API/config/persistence errors are logged without prompt text, API keys, or credential-bearing URLs. Operators can explicitly clear stored moderation keys; a blank edit still preserves them. Partial threshold updates retain omitted category defaults, legacy `record_logs` normalizes to `record_non_hits`, `UpdateOption` fails before mutating memory on database errors, and violation windows are capped at 8,760 hours. Final verification passed `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -race ./service -run "ContentModeration|Affinity|Email" -count=1`, `go test -race ./common -run "SendEmailContext|Email" -count=1`, frontend Vitest (36 files / 196 tests), Bun tests (68 files / 231 tests), typecheck, production build, changed-file Oxlint/oxfmt, i18n key sync, RelayKit tests, and `git diff --check`. Repository-wide frontend `format:check` still reports pre-existing unrelated files; changed content-moderation files pass focused formatting. Local browser validation reached the real SQLite-backed setup UI with no console errors, but the authenticated moderation page was not opened because the disposable instance had no root account and no local state was mutated to create one. No production deployment or credentials were used.
- On 2026-08-20, the content-moderation deep-security follow-up closed the residual long-input, configuration-refresh, and account-revocation gaps. Latest-user text is no longer truncated at 16,000 runes: oversized turns are sent as one OpenAI-compatible `input` array, the response must contain exactly one result per input, and category maxima are aggregated across all results, so missing or unsafe tail content cannot become a cached allow without adding another moderation HTTP round trip. Empty and partial result sets are errors and remain uncached. Multi-node gateways refresh the database-authoritative moderation policy at most once per second per process, publish database values only after parsing and normalization, apply local updates immediately, and keep the last known valid policy on transient reads or invalid externally-written values. The auto-ban helper also retries browser-session revocation after a committed disable whose initial Redis publication failed; a deterministic DB/Redis/session regression test covers that failure window. Redis guards for user/session/token invalidation consistently require both the feature flag and a live client, avoiding nil-client panics during partial initialization. `MEMORY.md`, the PRD, and the technical specification were updated; no deployment or credentials were used.
- On 2026-08-20, the production release plan for affinity-aware content moderation was recorded in `docs/content-moderation-production-rollout.md`. It requires merging the candidate into `tokeness/main`, publishing and deploying only an immutable GHCR digest with the existing four-node workflow, keeping moderation disabled during the code rollout, and enabling observe/pre-block/notifications/auto-ban progressively. The plan records the fixed HTTPS provider and egress requirement, main-database migration verification, shared Redis requirement, `/v1/responses/compact` coverage decision, log retention, monitoring, and configuration/digest rollback. No production state, credentials, or deployment workflow was touched.
- On 2026-08-20, content moderation gained Sub2API-aligned multimodal request review for OpenAI Chat, OpenAI Responses, Anthropic Messages, and Gemini. The extractor audits only the final conversation item: assistant/model turns and tool/function response items do not fall back to older user content. HTTP(S) and `data:image/*` images are normalized to the OpenAI `image_url` moderation contract; Anthropic/Gemini Base64 sources are rebuilt as data URLs, duplicate images are removed, and at most one image is randomly selected per audit. Pure text keeps its one-result-per-chunk contract, while text-plus-image and image-only inputs require exactly one aggregate result. Image references are never stored or logged; the audit hash contains only SHA-256-derived image identity, and channel-affinity caching continues to review a conversation once per selected channel even when later requests change text or images. Gemini DTO parsing now supports snake_case `file_data`, and typed relay extraction avoids an extra body read for every supported protocol. Verification passed focused service/controller tests, the service race suite, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, RelayKit standalone build/tests with `GOWORK=off`, and `git diff --check`. No moderation provider, production configuration, image, or credential was contacted or persisted.
- As of 2026-08-20, the reviewed production baseline is `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-production.1` from `5c5aa45d76c311da3bfecb6c7d4b86c8e0837724`, deployed to all four nodes at `sha256:5a32198cb10a2a1fddbc09357eda617b3555dc530f6760e270749117dcaf31c5`. Publish workflow `32259337968`, fleet verification `32260442064`, and staged rollout `32260628186` passed; public web/status probes returned 200 and both CDN `/v1/models` probes returned the expected 401 with consistent version headers. This is the rollback baseline for the moderation release unless a newer reviewed production digest supersedes it.
- On 2026-08-20, production preparation for multimodal moderation strengthened `.github/workflows/tokeness-publish.yml` to create the required ignored frontend embed placeholder, run the full Go test suite, `go vet`, and standalone RelayKit tests before publishing. The rollout runbook now pins implementation candidate `1d4b751eaa77ec2f46a20f6b1f7847c1b0ef0451`, requires real-provider multimodal contract and privacy validation, records image payload/latency budgets and protocol coverage limits, and defines image-specific canary and shutdown criteria. A production review follow-up added strict PNG/JPEG/WEBP/GIF Base64 validation, a 20 MB decoded data-image limit aligned with official OpenAI moderation documentation, an 8 KiB HTTP(S) URL limit with user-info rejection, a 16-distinct-candidate cap before one-image selection, and request-scoped rejection in `pre_block` rather than silently bypassing moderation. Responses extraction no longer aggregates historical input when the final item is unknown or an unroled message; only explicit flat direct input parts may be combined. `/v1/responses/compact` now uses the same typed Responses extraction and moderation protocol instead of bypassing the gate; image generation, Realtime, Audio, and other non-conversation routes remain out of scope. This preparation does not merge to `tokeness/main`, publish an image, configure a provider, or deploy production.
- On 2026-08-20, affinity-aware multimodal moderation code was deployed to production from source `23a2fec86facd375d6cc0fa2480a26dbbe5c9e20` as `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-moderation.1`, immutable digest `sha256:5c18d5e6d5e36f84bde90e43644b24bc1afded838df3cf4f9e25e2ced342a88d`. Baseline verify `32379322141`, publish `32379661916`, post-publish verify `32380481122`, staged rollout `32380644587`, and final verify `32381279396` all passed. The rollout completed `JP-N2 → EV-JP → JP-M → EV-JP2`; every node selected and ran the new digest, dashboard probes returned 200, and both CDN `/v1/models` probes returned the expected 401 with consistent version headers. The prior production digest `sha256:5a32198cb10a2a1fddbc09357eda617b3555dc530f6760e270749117dcaf31c5` remains the rollback baseline. The existing JP-M duplicate `text/html` MIME warning remains non-blocking. No moderation provider key or policy was configured, so content moderation remains disabled pending real-provider, privacy, database, Redis, SMTP, and observe-canary approval.
- On 2026-08-20, production content moderation was enabled and validated through the authenticated Tokeness Edge session without reading or exposing credentials. The effective policy used `observe`, `omni-moderation-latest`, two configured provider keys, 100% sampling, all groups/models, non-hit persistence, a 4-second timeout, and one retry. Safe requests reached upstream and produced `allow` audit rows. A single explicitly synthetic self-harm fixture on user 1/model `glm-5.2` was allowed through as expected in observe mode and persisted as action `observe`, category `self-harm/intent`, score `0.988`, and moderation latency `3340 ms`, confirming the provider, threshold evaluation, persistence, and observe-mode forwarding chain. During validation, `email_on_hit` and `auto_ban_enabled` changed from off to on through a concurrent production configuration update; the final policy showed both enabled with threshold 10 and a 24-hour window, so no further flagged fixtures were sent. The session remained authenticated after the single hit. Public dashboard/status probes returned 200 and both CDN `/v1/models` probes returned the expected 401. An initial affinity de-duplication comparison was invalidated by the concurrent policy change because policy identity is part of the cache key; do not treat those two audit rows as a cache regression. Edge automation did not provide a supported file-upload primitive, so the production image-provider path was not exercised in this validation.
- On 2026-08-21, short repeated model-probe traffic was addressed without adding a client-controlled moderation bypass. Content moderation now keeps the first in-scope request fully audited, then may reuse only a successful allow result for identical normalized pure text (same user/group/protocol/policy, target model intentionally excluded) for 30 seconds; eligibility is limited to text-only inputs of at most 256 runes. Flagged decisions, provider/persistence failures, images, and long text are not cached. Local singleflight plus a Redis owner lease merges concurrent first checks, and an allow hit can seed the existing channel-affinity cache. Focused service and race tests passed; this change is local and has not been published or deployed.
- On 2026-08-21, moderation provider capacity became a hard resource boundary instead of relying only on sampling. Each moderation API key has a configurable per-key in-flight limit, enforced locally and across gateway nodes with Redis owner-token slot leases whose TTL exceeds the moderation timeout; Redis failures fall back to the local limiter for one second at a time. Key selection avoids cooled-down credentials after 429, timeout, transport, response-read, or 5xx failures while preserving the retry budget. Capacity exhaustion persists `skipped_capacity`: observe remains fail-open, while pre-block returns a distinct `content_moderation_overloaded` error with only configured 429/503. A regression test preserves that typed capacity outcome even when exhaustion follows a transient provider failure. Redis keys use only SHA-256 key fingerprints, never raw credentials. Full Go, race, vet, frontend test/typecheck/build, changed-file lint/format, i18n sync, and standalone RelayKit checks passed. This change is local and has not been published or deployed.
- On 2026-08-21, the moderation log UI now exposes the stored relay request ID and uses offset/limit pagination with selectable 10/20/50/100 page sizes. Explicit log API pagination metadata reflects the actual normalized offset and limit. A new additive `content_moderation_user_states` table stores each user's violation-reset log-ID boundary; flagged logs are retained, while cumulative violation counts and auto-ban/email thresholds ignore entries at or before that boundary. The log-ID boundary avoids second-resolution timestamp races around a reset. The root-only `POST /api/content-moderation/users/:id/reset-violations` endpoint performs the reset after confirming the user exists. Full Go, moderation race, vet, frontend test/typecheck/build, changed-file lint/format, i18n sync, and standalone RelayKit checks passed. This change is local and has not been published or deployed.
- On 2026-08-22, Ollama prompt cache estimation was implemented as a channel-level feature toggle. When `OllamaCacheEstimationEnabled` is on in `ChannelSettings`, the Ollama stream and non-stream handlers estimate `cached_tokens` via strict message-prefix matching for channels whose upstream does not return `cached_tokens`. The estimator hashes each message's role/content/name/prefix/reasoning/tool fields, isolates chat and completions relay modes, stores the chain and previous prompt token count in the existing Redis-backed `HybridCache` (with a 5,000-entry process-local hot-cache fallback when Redis is disabled), uses a 5-minute TTL, and caps tracked conversations at 256 messages. Cache keys include the persisted credential ID and multi-key index so legacy multi-key channels cannot share state across upstream credentials. A hit estimates `floor(previous_prompt_tokens * 0.5)` capped at current `prompt_tokens`. Real upstream `cached_tokens`, including an explicit zero, always take precedence; upstream token counts are normalized to non-negative values and cached tokens are capped at prompt tokens. Null/structured session identifiers and unauthenticated user IDs are rejected. Estimated usage is marked `BillingUsage.Estimated = true` with source `oai_chat` and semantic `openAI`, so the billing path logs `billing-usage-openai-estimated`; internal billing fields are stripped from stream and non-stream client responses while `cached_tokens` remains visible. The feature is restricted to Ollama handlers only; embedding requests are excluded. Frontend adds a conditional toggle visible only for Ollama (type 4) channels. This change is local and has not been published or deployed.
- On 2026-08-22, the Model Square default page size changed from 20 to 30 because the card grid uses 1/2/3 responsive columns and 20 leaves one empty cell in every full three-column page. PR #53 merged as `f006a2328490343cc03f4bd848efc9ead8e69c9b`; focused pricing regression, frontend CI, Go, RelayKit, vet, typecheck, and production build checks passed. Release `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-pricing-grid.1` was published by workflow `32558406192` at immutable GHCR digest `sha256:6d9635ca155d5e61f76dd82fc7d06e5b4c291461950009f5b47ba8745ffbd232`. Fleet verify `32559168331` passed, and staged production rollout `32559251754` completed JP-N2 -> EV-JP -> JP-M -> EV-JP2. All nodes and CDN routes expose the release version; dashboard routes returned 200 and `n.tokeness.io` / `n.tokeness.cn` API probes returned the expected 401. The previous moderation digest `sha256:5c18d5e6d5e36f84bde90e43644b24bc1afded838df3cf4f9e25e2ced342a88d` remains the rollback baseline.
- On 2026-08-22, the first cyber-policy interception slice was integrated across the relay boundary. OpenAI-compatible `error.code` and Responses `response.error.code` values matching `cyber_policy` are marked case-insensitively, original non-stream bodies are forwarded once, stream handlers emit a native error envelope plus terminal marker, and all marked errors carry skip-retry/no-generic-log semantics so channel failover and automatic disable are bypassed. Upstream input/output usage is retained for synchronous settlement through the existing billing session; a redacted `content_moderation_logs` row uses action `cyber_policy`, while the violation counter excludes that action. Coverage includes OpenAI Chat/Responses conversion paths, Anthropic compatibility, images, generic HTTP error handling, and existing model-count exclusion tests. A follow-up audit also closes code-only error envelopes, clamps upstream token values, keeps nil contexts safe, and verifies body forwarding plus Responses usage ordering. Session blocking, dedicated cyber configuration/email, and Realtime per-turn interception remain follow-up scope. Focused service/model/relay/controller tests, full Go tests, vet, and `git diff --check` passed locally; no deployment or credentials were used.
- On 2026-08-22, a cyber-policy review closed billing and termination gaps in Responses/Claude conversion streams: usage from failed events is retained for settlement, generic HTTP errors also extract top-level or nested response usage, and all OpenAI Responses conversion streams emit `[DONE]` after the native cyber error. Upstream cyber messages remain intact for client forwarding, while gateway/ops/error-log paths mask and bound sensitive message text; normal debug response-body logs now use the same masking and preview limits. Full Go tests, vet, focused race tests, and `git diff --check` passed. No deployment or credentials were used.
- The same review masks cyber-policy stream status error fields before they enter consume-log metadata; ordinary stream error metadata remains unchanged.
- On 2026-08-22, content moderation capacity exhaustion was changed to fail-open in every mode. `skipped_capacity` remains persisted and logged, but `pre_block` no longer returns `content_moderation_overloaded` or blocks the model request; confirmed flagged results still block in `pre_block`.
- On 2026-08-22, `content_moderation.max_in_flight_per_key` no longer has the legacy 64-value ceiling. Backend and frontend validation now require only a positive integer; the unrelated 64-key credential-count cap is unchanged.
- On 2026-08-22, multi-key channel retries now track attempted credential positions per request when `RetryTimes > 0`, skipping previously attempted enabled keys across random, polling, and affinity modes until the retry budget or key set is exhausted. Affinity mode records the successful credential fingerprint per channel/token in the Redis-backed hybrid cache (with an in-process fallback) and prefers it on the next request, while falling back to token hashing when absent. Forced credential/index probes and non-multi-key channels remain unchanged; success is recorded only from explicit controller success paths so streamed failures cannot poison affinity.
- On 2026-08-22, release review hardening changed request-local multi-key retry state to credential fingerprints so reordering or replacing stored keys cannot retry the same identity; the selector still supports position exclusions for compatibility. Affinity success writes now fall back to a process-local cache when Redis is unavailable, and compatibility error formatting uses the typed RelayFormat contract. The frontend moderation section was formatted without changing behavior. The latest local `tokeness/main` commit is the deployment candidate; nothing has been pushed, published, or deployed.
- On 2026-08-22, production release `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-cyber-multikey.1` from `d8a3f6c4d90744f488095b5461f082a9a5c45d6c` was published by workflow `32571407200` at immutable GHCR digest `sha256:3cac56eac22f97fc267268a059f7dda02b32ee5abf5365f410be88714adccaf5`. Pre-deploy fleet verification `32571923401`, staged rollout `32572017907`, and post-deploy fleet verification `32572270109` all passed. The rollout completed `JP-N2 -> EV-JP -> JP-M -> EV-JP2`; every node reported the selected/runtime digest, `running`/`healthy`, and the release version. `https://tokeness.io/` and `/api/status` returned 200; `https://n.tokeness.io` and `https://n.tokeness.cn` returned the expected unauthenticated 401 with the release version. This is the current production baseline; the previous pricing-grid digest remains the rollback reference until superseded.
- On 2026-08-22, production diagnosis found Ollama prompt-cache estimation enabled for `CN_Ollama` (channel 65), but the latest 72-hour usage window had 144 records with 6,514,000 prompt tokens and zero `cache_tokens`; other production channels still record positive cache values. The channel is not multi-key and its header override does not map a stable session header. The estimator currently accepts only body `prompt_cache_key`, `metadata.user_id`, or `user`, so OpenCode-compatible requests without those fields take the intentional no-op path. A future fix may add a `session_id`/`conversation_id` header fallback or an explicit header-to-body mapping, while retaining fail-safe isolation when no stable identifier exists. No production configuration or code was changed during this diagnosis.
- On 2026-08-22, the Ollama cache fix added a fail-safe fallback to `session_id`/`conversation_id` request headers (case-, hyphen-, and single `x-`-prefix tolerant) after body identifiers, preserving per-user/channel/model/session isolation. OpenCode's current request builder sends `X-Session-Id`, `x-session-affinity`, and `x-opencode-session`, so this fallback covers the production client paths. Regression coverage verifies header hits, body precedence, cross-session isolation, invalid values, full package tests, race tests, vet, and relay compilation. Commit `b96c958bd5635ff6e9e31dc6cf8cf0cd96b88b13` was published as `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-ollama-cache.1` by workflow `32577234008` at digest `sha256:c2c72829dfb19f75b30a351733451f4cbc37289261480ef5c05925978bb0f4ae`; staged deployment `32577946456` and post-deploy verification `32578269484` passed across all production nodes and CDN routes. No channel 65 request arrived during the post-deploy observation window, so positive `cache_tokens` business evidence remains pending the next repeated-session request.
- On 2026-08-22, the post-header audit confirmed that some OpenCode-compatible requests still omit every explicit body/header session identifier. The estimator therefore now mirrors channel affinity's established fallback: `token:<TokenId>`, then `user:<UserId>` for authenticated playground requests. Explicit body/header identifiers retain precedence, and the existing channel/credential/model/relay-mode/user/system/tool partitions remain in the hashed key. Regression coverage verifies first-write/follow-up estimation, token isolation, authenticated-user fallback, and the existing no-user fail-safe. This follow-up is local until its immutable release is published and deployed.
- On 2026-08-23, the first cache-release attempt exposed an unrelated async quota-cache race in the full model suite: post-reservation goroutines used the mutable global Redis client after a test or runtime Redis replacement, causing stale-client writes and a nil-client panic. The reservation sync and token invalidation paths now capture the Redis client at goroutine start and use that same client for compensation. The model package passes ten repeated runs; the fix is included in the Ollama cache release candidate.
- On 2026-08-23, terminal production verification confirmed the Ollama fallback release is running (version `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-ollama-cache.3`, image digest recorded in the deployment evidence), but channel 65 `CN_Ollama` is manually disabled with `status_reason=manual operation` since `2026-08-22 14:56:48 UTC`, before the release started. The shared Redis namespace `ollama_prompt_cache:v1:*` currently has zero keys and no post-release channel-65 requests exist, so zero `cache_tokens` is expected and is not evidence of a regression. Do not re-enable the channel or send paid probes as part of diagnosis; verify the first natural repeated-session traffic after an operator re-enables it, requiring a first miss followed by `cache_tokens > 0` and `billing-usage-openai-estimated`.
- On 2026-08-23, read-only production logs showed three same-token `deepseek-v4-flash` requests with identical `131572` prompt-token usage during the failed CN_Ollama test, all billed through `upstream` with `cache_tokens=0`. The estimator had written Redis state but rejected exact replays because it required a strict shorter prefix. Exact prompt replays now count as cache hits; prefix-extension and non-prefix isolation behavior remain unchanged. Focused Ollama tests and race tests pass. The channel remains disabled pending natural post-release verification.
- Release `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-ollama-cache.4` from `ed103721c` was published successfully at immutable digest `sha256:f42c470b80e54e6ff4c1e55f5521067feea72139b45220b933d6bd19ee2b9b2a` (publish workflow `32588835706`). The staged rollout workflow `32589532692` stopped on JP-N2 while registering the image layer with `no space left on device`; the remote rollback restored the previous `.3` digest and a follow-up fleet verify `32589747669` passed. No production node is running `.4` yet; disk cleanup or capacity expansion is required before retrying deployment.
- On 2026-08-23, the follow-up staged rollout `32589992224` completed successfully after reclaiming unused Docker layers on JP-N2. JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 now run `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-ollama-cache.4` at immutable digest `sha256:f42c470b80e54e6ff4c1e55f5521067feea72139b45220b933d6bd19ee2b9b2a`. Read-only production verification found channel 65 `CN_Ollama` still manually disabled (`status=2`, `status_reason=manual operation`) with `ollama_cache_estimation_enabled=true`; it has zero usage rows after the `.4` start and zero `ollama_prompt_cache:v1:*` Redis keys, so cache effectiveness is not yet observable. The prior 72-hour window contained 321 channel-65 rows and zero positive `cache_tokens`, consistent with the pre-`.4` exact-replay defect. Do not send paid probes or re-enable automatically; after an operator re-enables the channel, verify one initial miss followed by positive `cache_tokens` and `billing-usage-openai-estimated` on the same token/session.
- On 2026-08-23, after the operator re-enabled `CN_Ollama`, read-only production verification confirmed the `.4` fix works. The shared Redis namespace contained four live `ollama_prompt_cache:v1:*` entries with 10-139 message hashes and prior prompt-token state. In the PostgreSQL consume log, a same-token `deepseek-v4-flash` conversation first recorded `upstream/cache_tokens=0` at 82,449 prompt tokens, then recorded repeated `billing-usage-openai-estimated` rows with cache tokens 41,224 through 49,456 as the conversation grew to 101,048 prompt tokens; another 154,527-token conversation changed from an initial upstream miss to an estimated 76,888-token hit. The 48-hour channel-65 aggregate contained 208 flash rows (10 positive) and 25 pro rows (17 positive); one-off/new-session rows remain expected misses. Focused Ollama tests and the race suite passed. No code or production configuration change was needed in this verification.
- On 2026-08-23, the same-model cache comparison showed `CN_Ollama` has a materially lower aggregate than provider-backed channels: over 48 hours `deepseek-v4-flash` was about 8.1% cached tokens / 9.5% hit requests on channel 65 versus 93.9%-99.7% cached tokens on the other channels; the post-enable six-hour window was 32.8% versus 97.6%-98.5%. The comparison is not a direct provider-semantic equivalent because Ollama estimation is per channel/model/session, has a five-minute TTL, stores only the latest prompt chain, and requires a strict prefix. Within channel 65, the stable 12898/12245 cohort reached 95.45% hits among five-minute repeats, while several other repeated cohorts had zero hits despite five-minute spacing, indicating interleaved sessions or upstream zero-usage responses are suppressing/invalidating estimation rather than Redis writes failing. Treat the aggregate as a real optimization gap; do not use it as evidence that the estimator is globally broken without inspecting per-request session identity and upstream cached-token-field presence.
# Production refund audit

- On 2026-08-23, the Ollama prompt-cache estimation test incident was reconciled conservatively. The only records with sufficient evidence of a missed cache charge were channel 65 logs `6927694` and `6927704`: same user/token/model, identical 131,572 prompt tokens, exact replay within 27 seconds, `usage_billing_path=upstream`, `cache_tokens=0`, and wallet billing. The cold-start log `6927675` was excluded. A PostgreSQL transaction refunded `4,204 + 4,203 = 8,407` quota, adjusted user 13274 wallet/used quota, token 12672 remain/used quota, and channel 65 used quota, marked both source logs, and wrote refund logs `6982001`/`6982002` under incident ID `ollama-cache-estimation-test-20260823`. Redis user/token balance caches were invalidated. Re-running the transaction found the incident already applied and made no changes. The remaining test-window rows were not refunded because logs do not preserve prompt/session identity and cannot prove cache misses without risking normal requests.
- On 2026-08-23, the operator declared all `CN_Ollama` channel 65 usage through `2026-08-23 19:15:00 Asia/Shanghai` to be non-billable test traffic. Incident `ollama-testing-before-20260823-1915` refunded the remaining `22,169,328` quota after subtracting the prior `8,407`, exactly reconciling `4,720` source consume logs and `22,177,735` original quota. The transaction credited 187 wallets, restored 184 token balances (`22,068,687`; the remaining `100,641` belonged to 13 playground/tokenless groups), reduced only the current channel-counter period by `10,647,716`, wrote 197 aggregate refund logs (`7031009`-`7031205`), and marked 4,719 non-zero remaining source rows. Redis invalidated 31 hydrated user hashes and 29 token hashes. A second database execution made zero changes; final checks found no unmarked target, no negative affected used-quota values, and channel 65 `used_quota` equal to post-cutoff consumption.

- On 2026-08-23, quota-warning notification state was corrected from a user-level subscription flag to `user_subscriptions.quota_warning_sent`. Wallet claims remain keyed by user, while subscription claims lock and update only the requested `(subscription_id, user_id)` row, so multiple subscriptions no longer suppress one another. Nullable legacy values are read with `COALESCE(..., FALSE)`; the old `subscription_quota_warning_sent` field is no longer read or written, avoiding a destructive migration. Focused model tests, race tests, full Go tests, and `go vet ./...` passed. Deployment transaction tests passed for remote commands, blue-green recovery, and frontend distribution retention; rollout self-test correctly failed closed when its fixture omitted SSH metadata. This candidate is local on `tokeness/main` and has not been committed, pushed, published, or deployed.
- On 2026-08-23, the quota-warning subscription-isolation fix was committed as `1185c27675067fd63b73ec8a0115c24d722a620d`, published as `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-quota-warning.1` at immutable GHCR digest `sha256:3e12fbb459233147d33bb3d6738e047296d2dd33850438151fe6bb0105cc782c` by workflow `32623438834`. Release regression tests, full Go tests, vet, RelayKit tests, and frontend distribution retention passed. Production preflight `32624368254`, staged rollout `32624762616`, and post-deploy verification `32625103227` passed; JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 all report the new digest, running state, and healthy/expected no-healthcheck status. Dashboard routes returned 200 and CDN routes returned the expected 401 with the new release version. The previous Ollama-cache digest remains the rollback reference until superseded.
- On 2026-08-23, read-only production cache audit found `CN_Ollama` channel 65 enabled for Ollama estimation and running the quota-warning release digest. PostgreSQL `logs` and `channel_model_perf_metrics` agree: over the latest 48 hours, `deepseek-v4-flash` had 1,130 eligible requests, 68.20M prompt tokens, 14.08M estimated cache tokens (20.64% aggregate token rate), and 396 positive-hit requests (35.04%); `deepseek-v4-pro` had 118 requests, 4.70M prompt tokens, 1.89M cache tokens (40.29%), and 63 hits (53.39%). The latest six-hour rates were 24.70%/42.31% for flash and 40.81%/54.78% for pro. Positive rows continue to carry `billing-usage-openai-estimated`, and no `ollama prompt cache update failed` log was found on JP-M or EV-JP2. Because the estimator intentionally bills only 50% of the previous prompt-token count, hit rows average about 48% cache-token ratio; the low aggregate is therefore primarily low session reuse/cold starts and five-minute TTL, not a total cache failure. Same-model provider channels remain roughly 93%-99% aggregate cached-token rate, so CN_Ollama is below a comparable business optimization target even though the estimator is operational. No production configuration, database, Redis data, or code was changed; temporary audit scripts were removed.
- On 2026-08-23, observe-mode content moderation affinity caching now treats a cached flagged result as recheck-required: each subsequent request with the same affinity key calls the moderation provider again, writes a new flagged audit row, and runs the existing violation side effects. A fresh allow result replaces the affinity entry with reusable allow state; `pre_block` flagged-cache blocking is unchanged. Focused moderation tests, race tests, and `go vet ./service` passed. This change is local and has not been deployed.
- On 2026-08-23, the Model Square dynamic-pricing display was fixed to evaluate time-based billing expressions at the current browser time before choosing the displayed tier. Previously it always used the first parsed tier, so a weekday `pro_peak` tier was shown on Sunday even though billing selected `pro_offpeak`. Complete tier tables in model details remain unchanged and continue to show every configured tier. Focused pricing tests cover Sunday off-peak and weekday peak selection; no production deployment was performed.
- On 2026-08-23, the model-details pricing view now shows the stored dynamic billing expression above the tier table when the full (non-compact) dynamic-pricing breakdown is rendered. Usage-log compact breakdowns intentionally omit the expression to preserve their dense layout. Component regression coverage verifies both display modes; no production deployment was performed.
- On 2026-08-23, `/keys` and `/usage-logs/common` group-ratio labels were aligned with Model Square via the shared `formatGroupDiscount` formatter: Chinese uses `x折`, non-Chinese uses `x% off`, premium ratios use `xN`, and ratio `1` is hidden. Group badges, auto-group visuals, common-log rows/details, and the usage-log details dialog now use the same locale-aware output. Focused and full frontend tests, typecheck, focused lint, and changed-file formatting passed; no production deployment was performed.

- On 2026-08-23, the Ollama prompt-cache candidate was expanded for the 95% final weighted cached-token coverage target, with 90% as the production baseline. The release metric is `sum(cache_tokens) / sum(prompt_tokens)` across all usage rows, including cold starts, uncacheable requests, and real misses; request hit rate and reusable-session hit rate remain diagnostic metrics. Estimation now uses the full validated previous prompt-token count without a fixed discount, while Ollama's `prompt_eval_count` remains the complete input count (`cache_n + prompt_n`) and `eval_count` is output-only. A completed chat response may add a separate assistant-prefix candidate only when the final Ollama assistant content, thinking, and tool calls match exactly; output tokens are never added to an ordinary prompt candidate and generate requests never receive this extension. Final outbound Ollama bodies are authoritative, image/invalid/`keep_alive: 0` requests are uncacheable, explicit short keep-alive values cap Redis TTL, and request-start snapshots prevent candidates created during an in-flight request from becoming same-request hits. Positive estimated rows carry `billing-usage-openai-estimated` and admin-only `ollama_prompt_cache` outcomes. Release `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-ollama-cache.5` from `e23f55b99` was published by workflow `32634460729` at immutable digest `sha256:c0c39f867f60a00d0187dd37120016b0a21c5d975a6c9cd9b6b4fc8d0db417ba`; preflight `32635091023`, staged rollout `32635212772`, and post-deploy verification `32635514021` passed across the production fleet. The main `tokeness.io/api/status` route reports the new version; `n.tokeness.io`, `n.tokeness.cn`, and `n-cf.tokeness.io` returned edge 520 during terminal checks and require separate origin/CDN investigation. Production cache coverage is not yet declared healthy: complete natural-traffic windows must reach at least 90% weighted coverage before baseline status and sustain 95%+ before declaring the final target met.
- On 2026-08-23, the first post-release production metrics query from JP-M showed channel 65 `CN_Ollama` still enabled and receiving natural traffic, but the latest six-hour weighted coverage remained below baseline: `deepseek-v4-flash` 24.85% (`23,189,944 / 93,307,730` cache/prompt tokens), `deepseek-v4-pro` 0.90% (`2,132 / 238,104`), `glm-5.2` 1.62%, `kimi-k2.6` 10.85%, and `minimax-m3` 8.74%; aggregate status is therefore below 90% and far below the 95% final target. JP-M, JP-N2, and JP-N4-EV reported the new container image prefix `c0c39f867f60`; the rollout and post-deploy workflow remain green. Treat these values as the initial post-deploy observation window, not a final regression verdict; continue natural-traffic monitoring and investigate session reuse/TTL/concurrency before attempting to claim the target.
- Domain routing constraint: `tokeness.io` is the main site and may serve site/dashboard routes; all other public node domains are API-only and must expose model calls under `/v1` only. Do not use their root or site `/api` routes as health or application endpoints.
- On 2026-08-23, `CN_Ollama` stream support was confirmed: channel type 4 is in `streamSupportedChannels`, and `Adaptor.DoResponse` dispatches `info.IsStream` requests to the Ollama NDJSON stream handler. Its missing TTFT/首字 metrics are a timing instrumentation bug, not a streaming capability issue: the provider-specific `ollamaStreamHandler` bypassed the shared stream scanner and did not mark the first non-terminal upstream frame. The local fix now records `RelayInfo.SetFirstResponseTime()` on the first non-`done` Ollama frame and ignores pre-FRT synthetic stream writes when calculating TTFT, including after retries; non-stream first-write timing is unchanged. Tests pass, but this fix has not been committed or deployed.
- On 2026-08-23, production analysis of `CN_Ollama` long-context throughput showed the usage-log TPS is end-to-end `completion_tokens / use_time`, where `use_time` includes upstream queueing, prompt prefill, generation, and network time and is stored in whole seconds. Ollama `prompt_eval_duration` and `eval_duration` are parsed but not persisted, so current logs cannot report true decode TPS. In the latest 12-hour sample, 100k+ `deepseek-v4-flash` requests had median displayed TPS 96; only 25/715 (3.50%) were below 10 TPS and 40/715 (5.59%) below 15. The slow tail correlated with concurrency: below-10 requests averaged 4.28 overlapping channel-65 requests versus 1.71 for 30+ TPS. Matched same-user/token examples changed from 292k context at 9.89 TPS with eight overlaps to 149.60 TPS with two overlaps, and from 102k at 10.82 TPS with eight overlaps to 179 TPS with one. Simulated Ollama cache only changes usage/billing and does not create upstream KV-cache reuse; the below-10 cohort still showed 76.47% estimated cache coverage. Treat single-digit rows as provider queue/prefill slow tails, not stable decode speed or evidence that the billing estimator accelerates inference.
- On 2026-08-23, read-only production diagnosis of `skipped_capacity` found the three reported rows were provider-capacity waits, not the observe async queue: each had `latency_ms=1000` and error `content moderation capacity exhausted after 1000ms`. The effective policy was `observe`, `timeout_ms=2000`, `queue_wait_ms=1000`, `max_in_flight_per_key=250`, with two newline-separated API keys, so the nominal distributed limit was 500 active provider calls. The acquisition loop first skips locally/shared cooled-down credentials, then waits 25 ms polls until the queue budget expires; 429, timeout, transport, read, and 5xx results put a credential into a 5-second cooldown. Historical node logs contain repeated moderation 429s and timeouts, making simultaneous key cooldown the leading cause of the isolated skips even though model traffic concurrency is about 100. Observe-mode background errors are intentionally discarded by `SubmitContentModeration`, so provider 429/timeout evidence is absent from the application log for async requests; only `skipped_capacity` remains in the database. Focused service/controller moderation tests passed; no code, configuration, database, Redis, or provider state was changed.
- On 2026-08-23, the follow-up capacity audit strengthened that diagnosis. PostgreSQL showed 45 `skipped_capacity` rows since local midnight, including a 12:49-12:54 burst with 500 ms waits and repeated requests from user 9582 across several models; the current 1000 ms configuration was active after the 19:02 fleet restart. Around the reported failures, consume-log counts in a five-second window were only 6, 7, 6, and 12, and the 19:25:30-19:26:10 per-second rate was 1-3 requests, far below the two-key nominal 500-slot limit. All four nodes run the same immutable image `sha256:c0c39f867f60...` and no current Redis degradation or provider-slot release error was logged. The exact per-row trigger remains unobservable because capacity outcomes do not persist a reason and observe-mode background provider errors are discarded; add reason-level metrics before changing limits.
- On 2026-08-23, content moderation capacity observability was added locally: `content_moderation_logs.capacity_reason` records `all_keys_cooling_down`, `local_slots_full`, `redis_slots_full`, `redis_error`, `async_queue_full`, or `capacity_unavailable`; provider slot acquisition preserves the existing Redis-to-local fallback. Provider 429, timeout, 5xx, transport, response-read, and Redis failures are counted in a ten-second in-memory window and emitted as one aggregated system log line, including observe-mode background checks. Focused service/model/controller tests and `go vet ./service` passed; this change has not been deployed.
- On 2026-08-23, the local Ollama relay hardening candidate was completed across Chat Completions, Completions, Messages compatibility, Embeddings, prompt-cache estimation, streaming integrity, tool-call billing, and model management. Ollama JSON/NDJSON errors and incomplete streams now fail explicitly; protocol conversion validation is a non-retryable 400; unsupported endpoints are also rejected during URL selection so request-body passthrough cannot bypass validation; committed responses are not followed by controller JSON; and SSE/non-stream write failures propagate before tool-call billing or assistant cache candidates are committed. Shared text settlement saturates prompt-plus-completion totals so malformed extreme usage cannot overflow into a free request. Tool calls are deduplicated by stable identity and billed only after a successful terminal response; management list/pull/delete/version calls inherit HTTP or background-task cancellation without mutating shared client timeouts. Focused tests, race tests, full `go test ./...`, full `go vet ./...`, RelayKit standalone build, and `git diff --check` passed. The candidate remains uncommitted and undeployed.
- On 2026-08-24, content-moderation capacity observability and Ollama relay hardening were deployed from merge `b4ef61d19027c6570e63e8d2ed69c977d01aacc9` as `v1.0.0-rc.25-tokeness-ollama-hardening.1`. Publish workflow `32650271273` produced immutable digest `sha256:c42879e42971b94330fce459d895f3e57ca9e892fccfd57f82c001bca1c8c9ac`; baseline verify `32650271219`, staged rollout `32650801524`, and post-deploy verify `32651080154` passed. JP-N2, EV-JP, JP-M, and EV-JP2 all selected and ran the same digest and version; the replicas reported healthy, while JP-M passed its internal status and OpenResty route checks without a Docker healthcheck. `tokeness.io/api/status` returned 200, and `n.tokeness.io`, `n.tokeness.cn`, and `n-cf.tokeness.io` `/v1/models` returned the expected unauthenticated 401 with the new version header. This digest is the current production baseline; the previous Ollama cache `.5` digest remains the rollback reference until superseded.

- On 2026-08-24, a local group-based access and content-policy implementation was completed in `apps/new-api` but not committed, published, or deployed. `GroupAccessPolicy` stores normalized deny overlays by authenticated users' base group, covering channel IDs, model aliases/compact variants, target groups, and a platform-AI-moderation exemption. Admin GET/PUT routes, React system-settings editor, Redis/local caching, migrations, model discovery, memory/DB/affinity/retry/specific-channel routing, async task submission, and moderation cache fingerprints are covered. Routing policy loads fail closed; moderation exemption loads fail closed to normal moderation; existing task polling remains available. Validation: focused Go tests and vet passed, RelayKit standalone tests/build passed, frontend 203 Vitest tests + 236 Bun tests + typecheck + i18n sync + changed-file lint/format passed. A serial `go test ./...` run reached all relevant packages but the full suite remains red only because unrelated pre-existing Ollama unsupported-endpoint worktree changes are inconsistent in one `relay` test (`TestOllamaRerankPassThroughStillReturnsClientError`); no production deployment was made. The supplied API key was not stored, logged, or used for paid probes.

- On 2026-08-24, the user explicitly authorized a transient API-key debug probe for the DeepSeek V4 compatibility investigation. The key value was not stored in files, source, tests, logs, or this memory; no production code or configuration was deployed. The live matrix confirmed the primary and backup API routes were reachable, while some primary-route stream/logprob probes timed out and the backup route returned valid OpenAI-compatible responses for basic, streaming usage, tools, stop, and logprob cases before the route pool became unavailable.

- On 2026-08-24, group discovery was tightened so an authenticated user's `/api/user/groups` response omits `auto` when the base-group policy blocks every automatic target group. - On 2026-08-24, the group access-policy follow-up kept blocked model names exact: pricing-only thinking/gizmo wildcard normalization is not used by policy matching, while compact aliases still match their base model. Focused model, middleware, service, and controller tests plus vet passed; no deployment was made.

- On 2026-08-24, the local capability-failover and DeepSeek V4 compatibility candidate was reviewed and fast-forward merged into local `tokeness/main` as commits `942b21614` and `1158b24d1`.
